. We decompose the integral (
5.60
) into two integrals corresponding to
0\leqslant s\leqslant r
and
r\leqslant s\leqslant t
. For
0\leqslant s\leqslant r
, by the same argument as for (
5.57
), we have

(5.65)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r}\bigg|\Imaginary\displaystyle\frac{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})}{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})+1}\bigg|\cdot\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{+}_{s})^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{r}]^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I^{*}_{t})}.

For
r\leqslant s\leqslant t
, the square root behavior (
3.38
) gives

(5.66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\frac{\eta_{s}}{(t_{c}-s)^{1/4}\sqrt{\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s}}}&\asymp\bigg|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\displaystyle\frac{f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})}{f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1}\bigg|=\bigg|\Imaginary\displaystyle\frac{f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})}{f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})+1}\bigg|\asymp\frac{\eta_{t}}{(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}\sqrt{\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}}}.\end{split}

We can bound the integral (
5.60
) as,

(5.67)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\displaystyle\int_{r}^{t}&\bigg|\Imaginary\displaystyle\frac{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})}{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})+1}\bigg|\cdot\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}s}{\eta_{s}\dist(z_{s},I^{+}_{s})^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\int_{r}^{t}\bigg|\Imaginary\displaystyle\frac{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})}{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})+1}\bigg|\cdot\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{\eta_{s}\big((\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})^{1/2}(t-s)/(t_{c}-s)^{1/4}+\eta_{s}\big)^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\int_{r}^{t}\frac{\eta_{s}/(t_{c}-s)^{1/4}{\sqrt{\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s}}}}{\eta_{s}\big((\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})^{1/2}(t-s)/(t_{c}-s)^{1/4}+\eta_{s}\big)^{2}}{\rm d}s\\
&\lesssim\int_{r}^{t}\frac{{\rm d}s}{(t_{c}-s)^{3/4}(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})^{3/2}\big((t-s)/(t_{c}-s)^{1/2}+\eta_{s}/(t_{c}-s)^{1/4}(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})^{1/2}\big)^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{(\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t})^{3/2}}\int_{r}^{t}\frac{{\rm d}s}{(t_{c}-s)^{3/4}\big((t-s)/(t_{c}-s)^{1/2}+\eta_{t}/(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}(\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t})^{1/2}\big)^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta_{t}(\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t})},\end{split}

where in the first bound we used (
5.14
), and in the second and fourth bounds we used that (
5.66
) and
\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s}\geqslant\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}
from (
5.30
). To verify the last inequality of (
5.67
), we must show that the integral in the fourth line of (
5.67
) is bounded by
\sqrt{\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}}/\eta_{t}
. There are two cases to consider, namely, if
\eta_{t}/\sqrt{\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}}
is less than
(t_{c}-t)^{3/4}
or is greater than
(t_{c}-t)^{3/4}
. In the first case, let
y=t-s
. We divide the integral into three integrals according to the dominant term in the denominator, so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}&\frac{{\rm d}y}{(y+t_{c}-t)^{3/4}\big(y/(y+t_{c}-t)^{1/2}+\eta_{t}/(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}(\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t})^{1/2}\big)^{2}}\\
&\leqslant\int_{0}^{(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}\eta_{t}/\sqrt{\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}}}\frac{{\rm d}y}{(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}\eta^{2}_{t}/(\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t})}+\int_{t_{c}-t}^{t}\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{7/4}}+\int_{(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}\eta_{t}/\sqrt{\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}}}^{t_{c}-t}\frac{(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}{\rm d}y}{y^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}}}{\eta_{t}}.\end{split}

The proof in the second case
\eta_{t}/\sqrt{\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t}}\geqslant(t_{c}-t)^{3/4}
is very similar and thus omitted.
∎

5.3.
Dynamical Equation

In this section we derive difference equations for
\Delta_{t}(z)=m_{t}(z)-m_{t}^{*}(z)
. We recall
\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z)=m_{t}^{*}(z)-\log\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)+\log\big({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z\big)
. By plugging the characteristic flow (
3.9
) in (
3.27
), we get

(5.68)

\displaystyle\log f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})=\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t}).

To get the difference equation of
\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})
, we recall the expression of
\partial_{t}\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})
from (
5.40
). We can take one more time derivative,

(5.69)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{t}^{2}\log\tilde{g}^{*}_{t}(z)&=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\partial_{t}\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{*}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}\\
&=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\left(\frac{\partial_{t}f_{t}^{*}(w)}{f_{t}^{*}(w)+1}-\frac{1}{w-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)}\right)\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\partial_{w}f_{t}^{*}(w)f_{t}^{*}(w)}{(f_{t}^{*}(w)+1)^{2}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\partial_{w}\left(\frac{f_{t}^{*}(w)}{f_{t}^{*}(w)+1}\right)\frac{f_{t}^{*}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}},\end{split}

where the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})
, which encloses
[\mathfrak{a}(t),\mathfrak{b}(t)]
and
z
. For
w\in\mathscr{D}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})
, we have
\big|\partial_{w}(f^{*}_{t}/(f^{*}_{t}+1))\big|\lesssim 1
(which holds by (
5.50
) and (
5.51
)), and
\big|f_{t}^{*}(w)\big|\lesssim 1
. Thus we conclude that
\big|\partial_{t}^{2}\log\tilde{g}^{*}_{t}(z)\big|\lesssim 1
.

We can obtain the following difference equation of
\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}
by taking the difference of (
5.68
) at times
t
and
t+1/n
,

(5.70)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\tilde{m}_{t+1/n}^{*}(z_{t+1/n})-\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})&=-\log\tilde{g}_{t+1/n}^{*}(z_{t+1/n})+\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})\\
&=-\log\tilde{g}_{t+1/n}^{*}(z_{t+1/n})+\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t+1/n})-\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t+1/n})+\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})\\
&=-\frac{(\partial_{t}\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*})(z_{t+1/n})}{n}-\partial_{z}\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t})+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\end{split}

where we used the bound
|\partial_{t}^{2}\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}|\lesssim 1
from the discussion after (
5.69
),
|\partial_{z}^{2}\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}|\lesssim 1
from the analyticity of
\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}
, and (
3.28
) so
|z_{t+1/n}-z_{t}|=|f^{*}(z_{t})/n(f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1)|\lesssim 1/n
. By plugging (
5.40
) into (
5.70
), we get that

(5.71)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{m}_{t+1/n}^{*}(z_{t+1/n})-\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})=\frac{1}{2n\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\partial_{w}\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{*}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z_{t+1/n}}-\partial_{z}\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t})+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right).\end{split}

For the transition probability (
5.1
), we can decompose the time difference of
m_{t+1/n}(z)-m_{t}(z)
as

(5.72)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{t+1/n}(z)-m_{t}(z)&=\int_{z-1/n}^{z}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t+1/n)}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t)}\right){\rm d}u\\
&=\Delta M_{t}(z)+\int_{z-1/n}^{z}{\mathcal{L}}_{t}\big(m_{t}(u)\big){\rm d}u,\end{split}

where
\Delta M_{t}(z)
is a martingale difference,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta M_{t}(z)&=\int_{z-1/n}^{z}\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t+1/n)}-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t+1/n)}\bigg|{\bm{x}}_{t}\bigg]\Bigg){\rm d}u\\
&=\int_{z-1/n}^{z}\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t+1/n)}-\sum_{{\bm{e}}\in\{0,1\}^{m}}a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}}_{t})\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t)-e_{i}/n}\Bigg){\rm d}u,\end{split}

and the drift term is given by

(5.73)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mathcal{L}}_{t}\big(m_{t}(u)\big)&=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t+1/n)}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t)}\bigg)\bigg|{\bm{x}}_{t}\Bigg]\\
&=\sum_{{\bm{e}}\in\{0,1\}^{m}}a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}}_{t})\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t)-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{u-x_{i}(t)}\right),\end{aligned}

where
a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})
is as defined in (
5.1
).

Transition probabilities in the form of (
5.1
) can be analyzed using dynamical loop equations. The following proposition gives us the leading order term of the drift term
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(m_{t}(z))
, and bounds on the moments of the martingale term
\Delta M_{t}(z)
.
We postpone the proof of
Proposition

5.5
to Sections
6
and
7
below.

Proposition 5.5
.

Under the assumptions of
Proposition

4.4
, for
0\leqslant t\leqslant\sigma
, uniformly for any
z\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm L}\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm F}
, we have

(5.74)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}(t+1/n)}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}(t)}\right]&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z)/(f_{t}(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I_{t})}\right),\end{split}

where
{\mathcal{B}}_{t}
is from (
5.42
), the expectation is with respect to the transition probability (
5.1
), and the contour
\omega
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
but not
z
. Moreover,

(5.75)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}(t)-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}(t)}-\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}(t)-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}(t)}\right]\right|^{2p}\\
&\qquad\qquad=\OO\left(\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z)/(f_{t}(z)+1)]}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I_{t})^{2}}\right)^{p}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z)/(f_{t}(z)+1)]|}{n^{2p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I_{t})^{4p-2}}\right).\end{split}

Remark 5.6
.

We recall the complex Burgers equation from (
5.39
). From the expression (
5.40
),
\partial_{t}\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)
is analytic in a neighborhood of
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
. We can do a contour integral on both sides of (
5.39
) to get rid of
\partial_{t}\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)
on both sides of (
5.39
)

(5.76)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}m_{t}^{*}(z)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{t}m_{t}^{*}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{*}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{*}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}},

where the contour
\omega
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
but not
z
.
The equation (
5.74
) can therefore be viewed as the discrete analog of the complex Burgers equation (
5.76
).

We next use
Proposition

5.5
to approximate the difference
\Delta_{t+1/n}(z_{t+1/n})-\Delta_{t}(z_{t})
, through the following proposition.

Proposition 5.7
.

Under the assumptions of
Proposition

4.4
, for
0\leqslant t\leqslant\sigma
(recall from (
5.12
)), uniformly for any
z_{t}=z_{t}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm L}\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm F}
, we have the following equation for the difference of the Stieltjes transforms

(5.77)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta_{t+1/n}(z_{t+1/n})-\Delta_{t}(z_{t})&=\Delta M_{t}(z_{t+1/n})+\frac{\partial_{z}f_{t}(z_{t})}{n}\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})-\log f_{t}(z_{t})}-1}{\big(f_{t}(z_{t})+1\big)\big(f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})+1\big)}\\
&\qquad+\OO\left(\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(t)}{n^{2}}+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})/(f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})+1)]}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I^{+}_{t})}\right),\end{split}

where the control parameter
{\mathfrak{M}}(t)
is from (
5.11
).

Proof.

Proposition

5.5
gives us that

(5.78)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\int_{z-1/n}^{z}{\mathcal{L}}_{t}\big(m_{t}(u)\big){\rm d}u=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{z-1/n}^{z}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w{\rm d}u}{(w-u)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z)/(f_{t}(z)+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I_{t})}\right),\end{split}

where the contour
\omega
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
but not any number on the segment
[z-1/n,z]
. For the contour integral on the right side of (
5.78
), we can rewrite it as

(5.79)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}&\int_{z-1/n}^{z}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w{\rm d}u}{(w-u)^{2}}\\
&=-\int_{z-1/n}^{z}\partial_{u}\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(u){\rm d}u+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{z-1/n}^{z}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w{\rm d}u}{(w-u)^{2}}\\
&=-\big(\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(z)-\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(z-1/n)\big)+\frac{1}{2n\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\\
&=-\Big(\log\big(f_{t}(z)+1\big)\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)-\log\big(f_{t}(z-1/n)+1\big)\big(z-1/n-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)\Big)\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{2n\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\end{split}

where the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
and
[z-1/n,z]
. Here, we have used the fact that the derivatives of
\log\mathcal{B}_{t}(w)
are uniformly bounded for
w\in\omega+
, which holds since
\mathcal{B}_{t}(w)=\big(f_{t}(w)+1\big)\big(w-\mathfrak{a}(t)\big)
and
\omega+
is bounded away from
\big[\mathfrak{a}(t),\mathfrak{b}(t)\big]
.

Recall
\tilde{m}_{t}(z)=m_{t}(z)-\log\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)+\log\big({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z\big)
. By plugging (
5.78
) and (
5.79
) into (
5.72
), and noticing that
{\mathfrak{a}}(t+1/n)={\mathfrak{a}}(t)+1/n
and
\mathfrak{b}(t+1/n)=\mathfrak{b}(t)
, we get

(5.80)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\tilde{m}_{t+1/n}(z)-\tilde{m}_{t}(z)&=m_{t+1/n}(z)-\log\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t+1/n)\big)-m_{t}(z)+\log\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)\\
&=\Delta M_{t}(z)-\Big(\log\big(f_{t}(z)+1\big)-\log\big(f_{t}(z-1/n)+1\big)\Big)\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{2n\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z)/(f_{t}(z)+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I_{t})}\right).\end{split}

We decompose the difference equation of
\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})
as

(5.81)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\tilde{m}_{t+1/n}(z_{t+1/n})-\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})=\big(\tilde{m}_{t+1/n}(z_{t+1/n})-\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t+1/n})\big)+\big(\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t+1/n})-\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})\big).\end{split}

For the second term on the righthand side of (
5.81
), thanks to (
5.41
)

(5.82)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t+1/n})-\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})&=\log f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})-\log f_{t}(z_{t})-\big(\log\tilde{g}_{t}(z_{t+1/n})-\log\tilde{g}_{t}(z_{t})\big)\\
&=\log f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})-\log f_{t}(z_{t})-\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}_{t}(z_{t})(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t})+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\end{split}

where we used
|\partial_{z}^{2}\log\tilde{g}_{t}|\lesssim 1
from the first statement of
Proposition

5.3
, and as a consequence of (
3.28
):
|z_{t+1/n}-z_{t}|=|f^{*}(z_{t})/n(f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1)|\lesssim 1/n
.
By plugging (
5.80
) and (
5.82
) into (
5.81
), we obtain the following difference equation of
\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})
,

(5.83)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\tilde{m}_{t+1/n}(z_{t+1/n})-\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})\\
&\qquad=\Delta M_{t}(z_{t+1/n})+\log f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})-\log f_{t}(z_{t})-\log\big(f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})+1\big)+\log\big(f_{t}(z_{t+1/n}-1/n)+1\big)\\
&\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2n\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z_{t+1/n})^{2}}-\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}_{t}(z_{t})(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t})+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I_{t})}\right),\end{split}

where by (
3.20
) the error term is at least of order
1/n^{2}
. Finally by taking difference of (
5.71
) and (
5.83
), we get the difference equation of
\Delta_{t}(z_{t})=m_{t}(z_{t})-m_{t}^{*}(z_{t})=\tilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})-\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z_{t})
,

(5.84)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\Delta_{t+1/n}(z_{t+1/n})-\Delta_{t}(z_{t})=\Delta M_{t}(z_{t+1/n})+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I_{t})}\right)\\
&+\log f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})-\log f_{t}(z_{t})-\log\big(f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})+1\big)+\log\big(f_{t}(z_{t+1/n}-1/n)+1\big)\\
&+\frac{1}{2n\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{(\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w)-\log{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{t}(w)){\rm d}w}{(w-z_{t+1/n})^{2}}-(\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}_{t}(z_{t})-\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}^{*}_{t}(z_{t}))(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t}).\end{split}

In the following we estimate (
5.84
) term by term. By Taylor expansion around
z_{t}
, the first order term for the second line in (
5.84
) is given by

(5.85)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\partial_{z}\log f_{t}(z_{t})(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t})-\frac{\partial_{z}\log(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)}{n}\\
&\qquad=\frac{\partial_{z}f_{t}(z_{t})}{nf_{t}(z_{t})}\frac{f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})}{f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1}-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial_{z}f_{t}(z_{t})}{f_{t}(z_{t})+1}=\frac{\partial_{z}f_{t}(z_{t})}{n}\frac{f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})/f_{t}(z_{t})-1}{(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)(f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})+1)},\end{split}

where we used the equality
z_{t+1/n}-z_{t}=f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})/n(f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1)
.

Let us show that the next order term for the second line in (
5.84
) is negligible and can be absorbed in the error term. To that end, first observe that
z_{t}
is bounded away from either
\mathfrak{b}(t)
or
\mathfrak{a}(t)
(since
\mathfrak{b}(t)-\mathfrak{a}(t)
is of order
1
). We only consider the former case, when
z_{t}
is bounded away from
{\mathfrak{b}}(t)
, as the analysis for the latter is very similar. Then,
f_{t}(z_{t})
is bounded away from
0
; combining with (
3.28
), we deduce
\big|f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)\big|\asymp 1
. To cancel the possible singularity at
{\mathfrak{a}}(t)
, we reorganize the second line in (
5.84
) as

\displaystyle\log\frac{f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})}{f_{t}(z_{t+1/n})+1}-\log\frac{f_{t}(z_{t})}{f_{t}(z_{t})+1}-\log\big(f_{t}(z_{t})+1\big)+\log\big(f_{t}(z_{t+1/n}-1/n)+1\big).

The second order term by a Taylor expansion around
z_{t}
is

(5.86)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\partial_{z}^{2}\left.\log\frac{f_{t}(z)}{f_{t}(z)+1}\right|_{z=z_{t}}\frac{(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t})^{2}}{2}+\partial^{2}_{z}\log\big(f_{t}(z)+1\big)|_{z=z_{t}}\frac{(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t}-1/n)^{2}}{2}\\
&\qquad=\partial_{z}^{2}\left.\log\frac{f_{t}(z)}{f_{t}(z)+1}\right|_{z=z_{t}}\frac{f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})^{2}}{2n^{2}\big(f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1\big)^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}_{z}\log(f_{t}(z)+1)|_{z=z_{t}}}{2n^{2}\big(f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1\big)^{2}}.\end{split}

For the first term on the right side of (
5.86
), we have

(5.87)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\partial_{z}^{2}\left.\log\frac{f_{t}(z)}{f_{t}(z)+1}\right|_{z=z_{t}}\frac{f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})^{2}}{2n^{2}\big(f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1\big)^{2}}\right|&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left(\left|\partial^{2}_{z}\left.\frac{f_{t}(z)}{f_{t}(z)+1}\right|_{z=z_{t}}\right|+\left|\partial_{z}\left.\frac{f_{t}(z)}{f_{t}(z)+1}\right|_{z=z_{t}}\right|^{2}\right)\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I_{t})}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)]|^{2}}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I_{t})},\end{split}

where we used that
\big|f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})/(f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})+1)\big|,\big|f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)\big|\asymp 1
in the first inequality, (
5.50
) in the second inequality, and (
5.51
) for the last inequality. For the second term on the righthand side of (
5.86
), we have

(5.88)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\frac{\big|\partial^{2}_{z}\log(f_{t}(z)+1)|_{z=z_{t}}\big|}{2n^{2}\big|f_{t}^{*}(z_{t})+1\big|^{2}}&\lesssim\frac{\big|\partial^{2}_{z}\log(f_{t}(z)+1)|_{z=z_{t}}\big|}{2n^{2}\big|f_{t}(z_{t})+1\big|^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\big|f_{t}(z_{t})+1\big|}\left|\partial^{2}_{z}\left.\frac{f_{t}(z)}{f_{t}(z)+1}\right|_{z=z_{t}}\right|+\left|\partial_{z}\left.\frac{f_{t}(z)}{f_{t}(z)+1}\right|_{z=z_{t}}\right|^{2}\right)\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)|]}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I_{t})},\end{split}

where we used (
5.44
) in the first inequality, and (
5.50
) in the second inequality.
Both error terms (
5.87
) and (
5.88
) are in the same form as the error term in (
5.84
), and can thus be absorbed into it.

For the first term in the last line in (
5.84
), the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})
, and
w\in\omega+
is bounded away from
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
. Thus, since
t\leqslant\sigma
we have
|m_{t}(w)-m_{t}^{*}(w)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{M}}(t)/n
from (
5.12
). It thus follows from (
5.44
) that
|\log f_{t}(w)-\log f_{t}^{*}(w)|\lesssim{\mathfrak{M}}(t)/n
, and

\displaystyle\big|\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w)-\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{*}(w)\big|

\displaystyle=\left|\log\left(1+(e^{\log f_{t}(w)-\log f_{t}^{*}(w)}-1)\frac{f_{t}^{*}(w)}{f_{t}^{*}(w)+1}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(t)}{n}.

Hence,

(5.89)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2n\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\big(\log{\mathcal{B}}_{t}(w)-\log{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{t}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z_{t+1/n})^{2}}\right|\lesssim\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(t)}{n^{2}}.

For the last term in the last line in (
5.84
), using (
5.44
) and
|z_{t+1/n}-z_{t}|\lesssim 1/n
, we have

(5.90)

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}_{t}(z_{t})-\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}^{*}_{t}(z_{t})\big)(z_{t+1/n}-z_{t})\Big|\lesssim\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(t)}{n^{2}}.

For the error term in (
5.84
), thanks to (
5.49
), for
0\leqslant t\leqslant\sigma
, it holds
I_{t}\subset I_{t}^{+}
, and thus
\dist(z_{t},I^{+}_{t})\leqslant\dist(z_{t},I_{t})
. Using (
5.47
), we can rewrite the error as

(5.91)

\displaystyle\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}(z_{t})/(f_{t}(z_{t})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I_{t})}=\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})/(f^{*}_{t}(z_{t})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t}]\dist(z_{t},I^{+}_{t})}\right).

The claim (
5.77
) follows from combining (
5.85
), (
5.89
), (
5.90
) and (
5.91
).
∎

5.4.
Optimal Rigidity Estimates

In this section, we analyze (
5.77
) and prove
Proposition

4.4
. By abbreviating
f_{s}=f_{s}(z_{s})
and
f_{s}^{*}=f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})
, and then summing over (
5.77
) from time
0
to
t\wedge\sigma
, we get

(5.92)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})&=\Delta_{0}(z_{0})+\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}(z_{s+1/n})+\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{\partial_{z}f_{s}}{n}\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{s}-\log f_{s}}-1}{(f_{s}+1)(f^{*}_{s}+1)}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\OO\left(\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(s)}{n^{2}}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{+}_{s})}\right).\end{split}

The following proposition bounds the martingale terms in (
5.92
).

Proposition 5.8
.

Under the assumptions of
Proposition

4.4
, there exists an event
{\mathcal{W}}
with overwhelming probability on which the following holds for every
u\in\mathscr{L}
.
If
z_{t}=z_{t}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm L}
, then

(5.93)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}(z_{s+1/n})\right|\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/4}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}.

If
z_{t}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm F}
then

(5.94)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}(z_{s+1/n})\right|\lesssim n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/4}\left(\frac{1}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}}+\frac{1}{(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}])^{2}}\right).

Proof.

In the rest of the proof, we simply write
\Delta M_{s}=\Delta M_{s}(z_{s+1/n})
. For any time
0\leqslant t\leqslant{\mathfrak{t}}
such that
z_{t}\in\mathscr{D}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm L}\cup\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm F}
, the Burkholder–Rosenthal inequality gives

(5.95)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\sup_{r\leqslant t}\sum_{s\in[0,r\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}\right|^{2p}\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}_{p}\max\left\{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta M_{s}|^{2}\big|{\bm{x}}_{s}\big]\bigg)^{p}\Bigg],\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta M_{s}|^{2p}\big]\right\}

To bound the first term in (
5.98
), thanks to (
5.75
) in
Proposition

5.5
by taking
p=1
,

(5.96)

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta M_{s}|^{2}\big|{\bm{x}}_{s}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{2}}.

For the second term in (
5.95
), thanks to (
5.75
)

(5.97)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta M_{s}|^{2p}\big|{\bm{x}}_{s}\big]\lesssim\left(\left(\frac{1}{n^{3}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{2}}\right)^{p}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{n^{4p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{4p-2}}\right),

Observe that the sum over
s
of the first term on the right side of (
5.97
) can be bounded by the
p
-th moment of (
5.96
). Thus, (
5.95
) implies

(5.98)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eqref{e:Ros}&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{2}}\right)^{p}\right]\\
&\qquad+\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{n^{4p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{4p-2}}\right].\end{split}

For the first term on the righthand side of (
5.98
),
since we always have that
|z_{s}-x|\geqslant\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z_{s}
for
x\in I_{s}
,
it follows that

(5.99)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{2}}&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]^{3}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]^{3}}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]^{2}},\end{split}

where we used (
5.47
) for the first inequality, and (
5.57
) for the last inequality.

For the second term in (
5.98
), we have

(5.100)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z_{s})/(f(z_{s})+1)]|}{n^{4p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{4p-2}}\right]&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{4p-1}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]^{4p-1}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{4p-2}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]^{4p-1}}\\
&\leqslant\frac{1}{n^{4p-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]^{4p-2}},\end{split}

where we used (
5.57
) for the last inequality. By plugging (
5.99
) and (
5.100
) into (
5.98
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\sup_{r\leqslant t}\sum_{s\in[0,r\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}\right|^{2p}\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2p}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]^{2p}}+\frac{1}{n^{4p-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]^{4p-2}}\right).

Therefore, by taking
p
large in Rosenthal’s inequality (
5.98
), for any
u\in{\mathscr{L}}
and
z_{t}=z_{t}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm L}
, the following holds with overwhelming probability, i.e.,
1-n^{-D}
for any constant
D>1
(assuming
n
is sufficiently large),

(5.101)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{r\leqslant t}\left|\sum_{s\in[0,r\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}\right|\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/4}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}.\end{split}

If
z_{t}\in\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm F}
, we have

(5.102)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\eta_{s}\dist(z_{s},I_{s})^{2}}&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\eta_{s}\dist(z_{s},I^{+}_{s})^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|{\rm d}s}{\eta_{s}\dist(z_{s},I^{+}_{s})^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\frac{1}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})},\end{split}

where we have denoted
z_{s}=\kappa_{s}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}
, and have used (
5.47
) and (
5.49
) for the first inequality, and (
5.60
) for the last inequality. Therefore, by plugging (
5.100
) and (
5.102
) into (
5.98
), we get

(5.103)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\sup_{r\leqslant t}\sum_{s\in[0,r\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}\right|^{2p}\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n^{2p}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I_{t\wedge\sigma}^{*})\big)^{p}}+\frac{1}{n^{4p-2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]^{4p-2}}\right).

By taking
p
large in (
5.103
),
for any
u\in{\mathscr{L}}
and
z_{t}(u)\in\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm F}
, we deduce that, with overwhelming probability,

(5.104)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{r\leqslant t}\left|\sum_{s\in[0,r\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}\right|\leqslant n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/4}\left(\frac{1}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I_{t\wedge\sigma}^{*})}}+\frac{1}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\big)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

We define
{\mathcal{W}}
to be the set on which (
5.101
) and (
5.104
) hold for any
u\in\mathscr{L}
and any time
0\leqslant t\leqslant{\mathfrak{t}}
such that
z_{t}=z_{t}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm L}\cup\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm F}
.
The above discussions imply that
{\mathcal{W}}
holds with overwhelming probability, thereby establishing the proposition.
∎

Now we can establish
Proposition

4.4
.

Proof of
Proposition

4.4
.

We can now start analyzing the difference equation (
5.92
) for
\Delta_{t}(z_{t})
. By the third statement of
Proposition

5.2
, for any lattice point
u\in{\mathscr{L}}=\mathscr{L}_{t}
with
z_{t}(u)\in\mathscr{D}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})
, we have that for any
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
that
z_{s}=z_{s}(z)\in\mathscr{D}_{s}({\mathfrak{r}})
. As a consequence, (
5.44
) and our construction of the stopping time (
5.12
) imply that
\big|\log f_{s}(z_{s})-\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})\big|\lesssim{\mathfrak{M}}(s)/n
for
s\leqslant\sigma
, and so

(5.105)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial_{z}f_{s}}{n}\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{s}-\log f_{s}}-1}{(f_{s}+1)(f^{*}_{s}+1)}\right|=\Bigg|\frac{\partial_{z}f_{s}(e^{\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})-\log f_{s}(z_{s})}-1)}{n(f_{s}+1)^{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})(e^{\log f_{s}(z_{s})-\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})}-1)}{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})+1}\bigg)\Bigg|\lesssim\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(s)}{n^{2}},

where we used (
3.28
) and the bound
\big|\partial_{z}(f_{s}/(f_{s}+1))\big|\lesssim 1/\dist(z,I_{s}^{+})\lesssim 1
(which holds by (
5.50
) and (
5.51
)). By
Proposition

5.8
, on the event
{\mathcal{W}}
, we also have

(5.106)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\Delta M_{s}(z_{s+1/n})\right|\lesssim n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/4-1}.

Under our assumption (
4.16
) for
z_{0}=u\in{\mathscr{D}}_{0}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{0}^{\rm L}\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{0}^{\rm F}
, we have

(5.107)

\displaystyle\big|m_{0}^{*}(u)-m_{0}(u)\big|\leqslant\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\big|H^{*}_{0}(x)-H_{0}(x)\big|{\rm d}x}{|u-x|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}}{\dist(u,I_{0}^{*})}.

In particular, for
z_{0}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{0}({\mathfrak{r}})
,
(
5.107
) gives
\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|\lesssim n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}
.
By plugging (
5.105
) and (
5.106
) in (
5.92
), we obtain

(5.108)

\displaystyle\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|\lesssim n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}+\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(s)}{n^{2}}\lesssim n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(s){\rm d}s}{n}.

We recall from (
5.11
), that
{\mathfrak{M}}(t)=Me^{Mt}n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3}
, so we can further simplify the right side of (
5.108
) and conclude that on the event
{\mathcal{W}}
,

\displaystyle\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|\lesssim(1+e^{M(t\wedge\sigma)})n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|\leqslant\frac{M}{2}e^{M(t\wedge\sigma)}n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1},

provided
M
is large enough. By our construction of the lattice set
\mathscr{L}
, (
5.56
) implies that for any
w\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}({\mathfrak{r}})
, there exists some
u\in{\mathscr{L}}
such that
z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}({\mathfrak{r}})
, and
\big|z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)-w\big|\lesssim 1/n^{2}
. Moreover, on the domain
\mathscr{D}_{t\wedge\sigma}({\mathfrak{r}})
, both
m_{t\wedge\sigma}
and
m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}
are Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant
\OO(1)
. Therefore

(5.109)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|m_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)\big|&\leqslant\Big|m_{t\wedge\sigma}\big(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)\big)-m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}\big(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)\big)\Big|\\
&\qquad+\Big|m_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m_{t\wedge\sigma}\big(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)\big)\Big|+\Big|m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}\big(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)\big)\Big|\\
&\leqslant\frac{M}{2}e^{M(t\wedge\sigma)}n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)<Me^{M(t\wedge\sigma)}n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}=\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(t\wedge\sigma)}{n}.\end{split}

In the following we analyze (
5.92
) for
z_{t}
close to the real interval
\big[\mathfrak{a}(t),\mathfrak{b}(t)\big]
. For the last error term in (
5.92
),

(5.110)

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(s)}{n^{2}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{{\mathfrak{M}}(s){\rm d}s}{n}=e^{M(t\wedge\sigma)}n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}\ll n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2-1}.

Since we always have that
\dist(z_{s},I_{s}^{+})\geqslant\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z_{s}
, we can bound the error term, as in (
5.99
)

(5.111)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{+}_{s})}&\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|{\rm d}s}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]},\end{split}

where we have used (
5.57
) for the last inequality. For
z_{t}\in\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm F}
,
we can bound the error term using (
5.59
)

(5.112)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{+}_{s})}&\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|{\rm d}s}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I_{s}^{+})}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\log n}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}}.\end{split}

Therefore, for
u\in{\mathscr{L}}
with
z_{t}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm L}
, by (
5.93
), (
5.110
) and (
5.111
) we can write (
5.92
) as

(5.113)

\displaystyle\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|\leqslant\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\left|\partial_{z}f_{s}\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{s}-\log f_{s}}-1}{(f_{s}+1)(f^{*}_{s}+1)}\right|+\OO\left(\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2-1}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\right).

Similarly, for
u\in{\mathscr{L}}
with
z_{t}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm F}
, using (
5.94
), (
5.110
) and (
5.112
) we can write (
5.92
) as

(5.114)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|\leqslant\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|&+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\left|\partial_{z}f_{s}\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{s}-\log f_{s}}-1}{(f_{s}+1)(f^{*}_{s}+1)}\right|\\
&+\OO\left(\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}}+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}])^{2}}\right).\end{split}

For the summation in (
5.113
) and (
5.114
), we slightly rewrite the summand as,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\partial_{z}f_{s}(z_{s})\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})-\log f_{s}(z_{s})}-1}{\big(f_{s}(z_{s})+1\big)\big(f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})+1\big)}\right|&=\Bigg|\frac{\partial_{z}f_{s}(z_{s})}{\big(f_{s}(z_{s})+1\big)^{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})(e^{\log f_{s}(z_{s})-\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})}-1)}{f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})+1}\bigg)\\
&\qquad\times\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})-\log f_{s}(z_{s})}-1}{\log f_{s}(z_{s})-\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})}\Bigg|\big|\log f_{s}(z_{s})-\log f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})\big|.\end{split}

We denote the first factor on the right side by
\alpha_{s}
,

(5.115)

\displaystyle\alpha_{s}:=\Bigg|\frac{\partial_{z}f_{s}}{(f_{s}+1)^{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{f_{s}^{*}(e^{\log f_{s}-\log f^{*}_{s}}-1)}{f_{s}^{*}+1}\bigg)\frac{e^{\log f^{*}_{s}-\log f_{s}}-1}{\log f_{s}-\log f^{*}_{s}}\Bigg|,

where we omitted the dependence on
z_{s}
.
For
0\leqslant s<t\wedge\sigma
, by (
5.44
) and (
5.45
) we have
|\log f^{*}_{s}-\log f_{s}|\lesssim n^{-{\mathfrak{d}}}
, and by (
3.28
) we have
|f_{s}^{*}/(f_{s}^{*}+1)|\lesssim 1
. So,

(5.116)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\alpha_{s}=\left(1+\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}}}}\right)\left|\frac{\partial_{z}f_{s}(z_{s})}{(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)^{2}}\right|&=\left(1+\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}}}}\right)\left|\left.\partial_{z}\left(\frac{f_{s}}{f_{s}+1}\right)\right|_{z=z_{s}}\right|\\
&\lesssim\left(1+\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}}}}\right)\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{s}(z_{s})/(f_{s}(z_{s})+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z_{s}}\\
&\lesssim\left(1+\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}}}}\right)\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{s}(z_{s})/(1+f^{*}_{s}(z_{s}))]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z_{s}}=\left(1+\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}}}}\right)\frac{-\partial_{s}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z_{s}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z_{s}},\end{split}

where in the second line we used (
5.50
), in the third line we used (
5.46
), and in the last equation we used the equality
\partial_{s}z_{s}=f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})/\big(f_{s}^{*}(z_{s})+1\big)
. With
\alpha_{s}
, we can by (
5.44
) rewrite (
5.113
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|\leqslant\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\alpha_{s}(z_{s})\big|\Delta_{s}(z_{s})\big|+\OO\left(\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\right).\end{split}

By discrete Grönwall’s inequality
[
Hol09
]
, this implies the estimate

(5.117)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\right|&\lesssim\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{s\in[0,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\alpha_{s}\prod_{\tau\in(s,t\wedge\sigma)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\left(1+\frac{\alpha_{\tau}}{n}\right)\cdot\left(|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}\right)\\
&\lesssim\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{s}e^{\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{\tau}{\rm d}\tau}\left(|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}\right){\rm d}s.\end{split}

For the integral of
\alpha_{\tau}
, we have

(5.118)

\displaystyle e^{\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{\tau}{\rm d}\tau}\leqslant e^{\left(1+\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}}}\right)\log\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\right)}=\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\right)^{1+\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}}}}\leqslant\OO(1)\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}.

In the last inequality, we used that by our construction of
{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm L}
, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]=\OO(n)
.
Combining the above inequality (
5.118
) with (
5.116
) we can bound the last term in (
5.117
) by

(5.119)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{s}e^{\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{\tau}{\rm d}\tau}\left(|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}\right){\rm d}s&\lesssim\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{-\partial_{s}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\left(|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}\right){\rm d}s\\
&\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{-\partial_{s}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\rm d}s+\frac{\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{0}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\\
&\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\log\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{0}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\right)+\frac{\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{0}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\\
&\lesssim\frac{(\log n)n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}+n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}-1}}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]},\end{split}

where in the last line we used (
5.107
), which implies
|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})|\lesssim n^{\mathfrak{d}/3-1}/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{0}]
. It follows by combining (
5.117
) and (
5.119
) that

(5.120)

\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\right|\leqslant\frac{Mn^{{\mathfrak{d}}-1}}{2\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]},

provided we take
M
large enough.

For
u\in{\mathscr{L}}
with
z_{t}=z_{t}(u)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm F}
, similarly to (
5.117
), we deduce from (
5.114
) that

(5.121)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|&\lesssim\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}}+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\big)^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{s}e^{\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{\tau}{\rm d}\tau}\left(\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{*}_{s})}}+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\big)^{2}}\right){\rm d}s.\end{split}

Thanks to (
5.116
) and (
5.118
), we can bound the last term in (
5.121
) by

(5.122)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{s}&e^{\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\alpha_{\tau}{\rm d}\tau}\left(\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{*}_{s})}}+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\big)^{2}}\right){\rm d}s\\
&\lesssim\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{-\partial_{s}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\left(\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|+\frac{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{*}_{s})}}+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\big)^{2}}\right){\rm d}s\\
&\lesssim\frac{\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{0}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}+\frac{n^{{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{-\partial_{s}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{*}_{s})}}{\rm d}s+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\big)^{2}},\end{split}

where in the last bound we again used the fact that
\Imaginary z_{s}
is nonincreasing in
s
. To bound the first term on the right side of (
5.122
) first suppose that
z_{t}
is in the domain (
5.7
), say
z_{t}=E_{1}(t)-\kappa_{t}-\mathrm{i}\eta_{t}
(or
z_{t}=E_{2}(t)+\kappa_{t}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{t}
) with
\kappa_{t}>0
. Then set
z_{s}=E_{1}(s)-\kappa_{s}-\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}
(or
z_{s}=E_{2}(s)+\kappa_{s}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}
, respectively), for any
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
. Using the square root behavior (
3.29
), we find

\displaystyle\eta_{0}=\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}-(t\wedge\sigma)\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\left[\frac{f^{*}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})}{f^{*}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})+1}\right]\lesssim\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}+(t\wedge\sigma)\frac{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}{\sqrt{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}}.

Thus, by (
5.107
) and (
5.22
), the first term can be bounded by

(5.123)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{0}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\lesssim\frac{\eta_{0}n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}(\kappa_{0}+\eta_{0})}&\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}\big(\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}+(t\wedge\sigma)\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}/\sqrt{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\big)}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}\big(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}+(t\wedge\sigma)\sqrt{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\big)}\\
&=\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}}{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}},\end{split}

where we remark that for the last term in the first line, there is an exact cancelation between the numerator and the denominator.
We have the same estimate if
z_{t}
is in the domain (
5.8
), by using (
5.30
) and the square root behavior (
3.38
):

(5.124)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\big|\Delta_{0}(z_{0})\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{0}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}\lesssim\frac{\eta_{0}n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}(\kappa_{0}+\eta_{0})}&\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}\big(\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}+(t\wedge\sigma)\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}/(t_{c}-t\wedge\sigma)^{1/4}\sqrt{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\big)}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}\big(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}+(t\wedge\sigma)\sqrt{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}/(t_{c}-t\wedge\sigma)^{1/4}\big)}\\
&=\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}}{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/3-1}}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}}.\end{split}

We can bound the integral in (
5.122
) using (
5.58
),

(5.125)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{-\partial_{s}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]}{\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{s}]\dist(z_{s},I^{*}_{s})}}{\rm d}s\lesssim\frac{\log n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]}}{\sqrt{\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}}.\end{split}

It follows by combining (
5.121
), (
5.122
), (
5.124
), (
5.123
) and (
5.125
), and using the bound (
5.107
) of
\Delta_{0}(z_{0})
that

(5.126)

\displaystyle\big|\Delta_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\big|\lesssim\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}}}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma})}}+\frac{n^{{\mathfrak{d}}/2}}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\big)^{2}}.

On the domain
{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}^{\rm L}\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}^{\rm F}
, we again have that both
m_{t\wedge\sigma}
and
m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}
are Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant at most
\OO(n)
. Similarly to (
5.109
), we can approximate
w\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}^{\rm L}\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}^{\rm F}
by the image
z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)
of some lattice point
u\in{\mathscr{L}}
, and deduce from (
5.120
) and (
5.126
) that, on the event
{\mathcal{W}}
as defined in
Proposition

5.8
, we have

(5.127)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|m_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)\big|<\frac{Mn^{{\mathfrak{d}}}}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]},\\
&\big|m_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m^{*}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)\big|<\frac{Mn^{{\mathfrak{d}}}}{n\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\dist(z_{t\wedge\sigma},I_{t\wedge\sigma})}}+\frac{Mn^{\mathfrak{d}}}{\big(n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z_{t\wedge\sigma}]\big)^{2}},\end{split}

uniformly for
w\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}^{\rm L}
and
w\in{\mathscr{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}^{\rm F}
. Comparing (
5.109
), (
5.127
) with the definition of
\sigma
in (
5.12
), we conclude that
\sigma={\mathfrak{t}}
on the event
{\mathcal{W}}
. Since
{\mathcal{W}}
holds with overwhelming probability, this together with the fourth statement in
Proposition

5.2
finish the proof of

Proposition

4.4
.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

Here, we briefly comment on the modifications needed to address the first case in the second statement of
Assumption

2.4
, namely, when the north boundary
\partial_{\rm no}({\mathfrak{D}})
of
{\mathfrak{D}}
is packed; this corresponds to random lozenge tilings of (single-sided) trapezoid domains, or equivalently families of non-intersecting Bernoulli bridges with tightly packed ending data, i.e.,
{\bm{x}}_{\mathfrak{t}}=({\mathfrak{a}}({\mathfrak{t}}),{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathfrak{t}})+1/n,{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathfrak{t}})+2/n,\cdots,{\mathfrak{b}}({\mathfrak{t}})-1/n)
. Then, the arctic curve will be tangent to the north boundary of
{\mathfrak{D}}
at
(E,{\mathfrak{t}})
, and
f^{*}_{\mathfrak{t}}(E)=-1
, see Figure
11
.
For such non-intersecting Bernoulli bridges, the transition probability is explicitly (we no longer need to consider a weighted version that approximates it) given by

(5.128)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\bm{x}}_{t+1/n}={\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n|{\bm{x}}_{t}={\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{Z_{t}({\bm{x}})}\frac{V({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{V({\bm{x}})}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\big({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-1/n-x_{i}\big)^{e_{i}}\big(x_{i}-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)^{1-e_{i}}.

Moreover, in this setting, the solution to the variational principle is also explicit. For any height function
\beta(x;{\bm{x}})
corresponding to the particle configuration
{\bm{x}}=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\subseteq[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
, the complex slope satisfies the algebraic equation:

\displaystyle f_{r}(x;\beta,t)=-\frac{\big(x-r-{\mathfrak{b}}(t)\big)f_{r}(x;\beta,t)+x-{\mathfrak{b}}(t)}{\big(x-r-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)f_{r}(x;\beta,t)+x-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{(x-t-x_{i})f_{r}(x;\beta,t)+x-x_{i}}{(x-r-x_{i}-1/n)f_{r}(x;\beta,t)+x-x_{i}-1/n};

see
[
Pet14
, Proposition 2.5]
. The complex slope
f_{t}(x;\beta,t)
can be extended to
f(z;\beta,t)
with
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
,

\displaystyle f(z;\beta,t)=\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z}{z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}}{z-x_{i}-1/n}=\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z}{z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)}e^{m(z;\beta,t)}.

This is the case when
g(z;\beta,t)=\big({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z\big)/\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)
in (
3.21
).

For
{\mathfrak{t}}-t\gtrsim 1
,
f^{*}_{t}(x)
bounded away from
-1
, the dynamics (
5.128
) can be analyzed in the same way as the weighted Bernoulli bridge (
4.9
). For
{\mathfrak{t}}-t\ll 1
, the behavior of the limiting complex slope
f_{t}^{*}(z)
can be analyzed as in
Proposition

3.5
, and we have
E_{2}(t)-E,E-E_{1}(t)\asymp\sqrt{{\mathfrak{t}}-t}
. To analyze the dynamics (
5.128
), we can restrict the spectral domains
\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm L}
and
\mathscr{D}_{t}^{\rm F}
from (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) to a radius
{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{{\mathfrak{t}}-t}
neighborhood of
E
, for some large constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
. See Figure
11
.
Inside this spectral domain,
\big|f_{t}^{*}(z)+1\big|
is nonzero but its size will depend on
t
:
|f^{*}_{t}(z)+1|\asymp\sqrt{{\mathfrak{t}}-t}
. Moreover, when
z
is close to the spectral edge, say
z=E_{1}(t)-\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta
,
f_{t}^{*}(z),m_{t}^{*}(z)
have square root behavior with constants depending on
t
,

\displaystyle-\sin(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{t}^{*}(z)])\asymp-\sin(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log f_{t}^{*}(z)])=-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}^{*}(z)]\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}({\mathfrak{t}}-t)^{1/4}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
({\mathfrak{t}}-t)^{1/4}\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta},&\kappa\leqslant 0.\end{array}\right.

Similarly to (
4.14
), we define the distance function
\tau(E(t),t)=n^{-2/3+6{\mathfrak{d}}}({\mathfrak{t}}-t)^{-1/6}
, and the enlarged intervals

(5.129)

\displaystyle I_{t}^{+}=\Big[E_{1}(t)-\tau\big(E_{1}(t),t\big),E_{2}(t)+\tau\big(E_{2}(t),t\big)\Big].

With the notations defined above,
Proposition

5.2
holds with (
5.13
) replaced by
\dist(z_{s},I_{s}^{+})\gtrsim\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z_{s}+\sqrt{\dist(z_{s},I_{s}^{*})}(t-s)({\mathfrak{t}}-s)^{1/4}
; And Propositions
5.3
,
5.4
,
5.5
,
5.7
and
5.8
also hold, except now we have
\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f_{t}^{*}(z)/(f_{t}^{*}(z)+1)]\big|\lesssim 1/\sqrt{{\mathfrak{t}}-t}
, and
g(z;\beta,t)=\big({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z\big)/\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)
(independent of
\beta
). Using them as input, we get
Proposition

4.4
with
I_{t}^{+}
given by the enlarged intervals (
5.129
).

y=0
y={\mathfrak{t}}
y=t

Shown to the left is a liquid region corresponds to lozenge tiling of a (single-sided) trapezoid domain. The arctic curve is tangent to the north boundary at
(E,{\mathfrak{t}})
. Shown to the right is the spectral domain restricted to a radius
{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{{\mathfrak{t}}-t}
neighborhood of
