6.
Dynamical Loop Equation

In this section we introduce a general family of transition probabilities
\mathbb{P}
, which includes those of weighted non-intersecting Bernoulli bridge as a special case. In Section
6.1
,
We first construct another transition probability
\mathbb{Q}
, which consists of the leading order terms in
\mathbb{P}
, and state
Proposition

6.4
, which is an analogous version of
Proposition

5.5
for
\mathbb{P}
. In Section
6.2
we introduce the dynamical loop equation, which will be used to study the transition probability
\mathbb{P}
. In Section
6.3
we state a weaker version of
Proposition

6.4
, which is optimal in the liquid region but suboptimal in the frozen region. And its proof is given in Section
6.4
.

6.1.
General Transition Probability

The transition probability of weighted non-intersecting Bernoulli bridge as in (
4.9
) is in the following form.
Fix a particle configuration
{\bm{x}}=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\in\mathbb{Z}_{n}^{m}
with
{\mathfrak{a}}\leqslant x_{1}<x_{m}\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}-1/n
. For any
{\bm{e}}=\{0,1\}^{m}
, the transition probability is given by

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\bm{e}})=\frac{1}{Z({\bm{x}})}\frac{V({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{V({\bm{x}})}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\phi^{+}(x_{i})^{e_{i}}\phi^{-}(x_{i})^{1-e_{i}}\cdot\exp\Bigg(\sum_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant m}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})+\OO(1/n)\Bigg).

We denote the empirical measure of
{\bm{x}}
and its Stieltjes transform by

\displaystyle\rho(x;{\bm{x}})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{1}\big(x\in[x_{i},x_{i}+1/n]\big),\quad m(z)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\rho(x;{\bm{x}}){\rm d}x}{z-x}.

We will study more general transition probabilities of the form (
6.1
) satisfying the following assumption.

Assumption 6.1
.

Suppose there exists a neighborhood
\Lambda
of
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
such that the transition probability (
6.1
) satisfies the following three assumptions.

(1)

The functions
\phi^{+}(z)
and
\phi^{-}(z)
have the decompositions

(6.2)

\displaystyle\phi^{+}(z)=({\mathfrak{b}}-1/n-z)\tilde{g}(z)e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(z)},\quad\phi^{-}(z)=z-{\mathfrak{a}},

where
\tilde{g}(z)
and
\psi(z)
are analytic and uniformly bounded on
\Lambda
. Moreover,
\tilde{g}(z)
satisfies
\tilde{g}(\bar{z})=\overline{\tilde{g}(z)}
; it does not have zeros or poles in
\Lambda
; and
\tilde{g}(x)>0
for
x\in\Lambda\cap{\mathbb{R}}
.
Both
\psi(z)
and
\kappa(z,w)
are analytic for
z,w\in\Lambda
, and they map reals to reals.

(2)

The complex slope defined by

(6.3)

\displaystyle f(z)=e^{m(z)}\tilde{g}(z)\frac{{\mathfrak{b}}-z}{z-{\mathfrak{a}}},

satisfies
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}f(z)<0
for
\Imaginary z>0
. We further assume that
f(x)+1\in(0,1)
for
x>{\mathfrak{b}}
, and
f(x)+1\in(-\infty,0)
for
x<{\mathfrak{a}}
, and that the following quantity is uniformly bounded away from
0
,

(6.4)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Imaginary z|}\cdot\bigg|\Imaginary\frac{f(z)}{f(z)+1}\bigg|\gtrsim 1,\quad\text{for $z\in\Lambda$}.

(3)

There exist small constants
{\mathfrak{c}},{\mathfrak{r}}>0
, and a domain
\mathscr{D}\subset\big\{z\in\Lambda:\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant n^{{\mathfrak{c}}-1}\big\}
, where
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]:=[{\mathfrak{a}}-{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{b}}+{\mathfrak{r}}]
, containing the annulus
\big\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:{\mathfrak{r}}\leqslant\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}\big\}
(see Figure
12
) such that the following are uniformly bounded

(6.5)

\displaystyle\big|f(z)+1\big|\gtrsim 1,\quad\left|\frac{f(z)}{f(z)+1}\right|\lesssim 1,\qquad\text{for $z\in\mathscr{D}$}.

Moreover,
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(x+0\mathrm{i})/(f(x+0\mathrm{i})+1)]
defines a negative measure on
{\mathbb{R}}
. We denote its support by
I
, which satisfies

(6.6)

\displaystyle I=\supp\Bigg(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\bigg[\displaystyle\frac{f(x+0\mathrm{i})}{(f(x+\mathrm{i})+1)}\bigg]\Bigg)\subseteq[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}].

{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}
{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}
{\mathfrak{a}}
{\mathfrak{b}}
\mathscr{D}
{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime}
{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}
{\mathfrak{a}}
{\mathfrak{b}}
\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})

Figure 12.
Shown to the left the interval
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]=[{\mathfrak{a}}-{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{b}}+{\mathfrak{r}}]
and the domain
\mathscr{D}
, which contains the annulus
\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:{\mathfrak{r}}\leqslant\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}\}
. Shown to the right is the domain
\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant{\mathfrak{r}}\}
.

Throughout the remainder of this paper, we adopt
Assumption

6.1
. Let us define the domains for any
r>0
,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}(r):=\Big\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant r\Big\}.

We will mainly use
\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
and
\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
. Thanks to the third statement of
Assumption

6.1
, we have
\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})\neq\emptyset
. By
Proposition

4.3
and equation (
4.11
), the transition probability of weighted non-intersecting Bernoulli bridge as in (
4.9
) satisfies
Assumption

6.1
with
\beta=\beta(x;{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle\phi^{\pm}(z)=\phi^{\pm}(z;\beta,t),\quad\kappa(z,w)=\kappa(z,w;\beta,t),\quad\tilde{g}(z)=\tilde{g}(z;\beta,t),\quad f(z)=f(z;\beta,t),

for
t\leqslant\sigma
(recall from (
5.12
)).

We can take the domains

\displaystyle\Lambda=\mathscr{U}_{t},\quad\mathscr{D}={\mathscr{D}}_{t}({\mathfrak{r}})\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm L}\cup{\mathscr{D}}_{t}^{\rm F},

from Section
5.1
.
The signs of
f(x)+1
for
x<\mathfrak{a}
and
x>\mathfrak{b}
, as well as the lower bound (
6.4
), follow from the second statement of
Proposition

3.4
.
Also, thanks to the third statement of
Proposition

3.4
, we have the decomposition (
6.2
), and that
\tilde{g}(z;\beta,t)
is real analytic and positive on
\Lambda\cap{\mathbb{R}}
.
The estimate (
6.5
) follows from (
3.28
) and (
5.46
). Thanks to the third statement of
Proposition

5.3
,
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(x+0\mathrm{i})/(f(x+0\mathrm{i})+1)]
defines a negative measure on
{\mathbb{R}}
, and (
6.6
) holds.

We need more notation; as in (
4.11
) and (
5.42
), we let

\displaystyle\varphi^{+}(z)=({\mathfrak{b}}-z)\tilde{g}(z),\quad\varphi^{-}(z)=z-{\mathfrak{a}},

and

(6.8)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}(z)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{G}}(z)\varphi^{+}(z)+\varphi^{-}(z)=\big(f(z)+1\big)(z-{\mathfrak{a}}),\quad\quad{\mathcal{G}}(z)=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\left(1+\frac{1/n}{z-x_{i}-1/n}\right)=e^{m(z)}.

The same discussion as after (
5.40
) gives that
\log{\mathcal{B}}(z)
(where the branch cut for the logarithm is
\mathbb{R}_{<0}
) is well defined on
\Lambda\setminus[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
, and so

(6.9)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{B}}(z){\rm d}z=0,

where
\omega\subseteq\Lambda
is any contour enclosing
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
.
In what follows, we take the branch of the logarithm to be so that
\Imaginary\log u\in[-\pi,\pi)
. We have the following decomposition of
\log{\mathcal{B}}(z)
as

(6.10)

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{B}}(z)=b^{+}(z)-b(z),\quad b(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z},\quad b^{+}(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z},

where
\omega\subseteq\Lambda
is any contour enclosing
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
but not
z
, and
\omega+\subseteq\Lambda
is any contour enclosing
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
and
z
.

From the construction,
b(z)
is analytic in a neighborhood of
\infty
, and we have that
\lim_{z\rightarrow\infty}b(z)=0
; we will abbreviate this by simply writing
b(\infty)=0
. For
b^{+}(z)
it is analytic and uniformly bounded in a neighborhood of
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
.

Remark 6.2
.

The functions
f(z)
,
\tilde{g}(z)
, and
{\mathcal{B}}(z),b^{+}(z)
are only defined on the domain
\Lambda
; we will typically use them for
z\in\mathscr{D}
. The functions
m(z)
,
{\mathcal{G}}(z)
, and
b(z)
are defined for any
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus[\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}]
; we will typically use them for
z\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r})
.

In the following lemma, we collect some estimates which will be used repeatedly throughout the remainder of this section.

Lemma 6.3
.

Adopting
Assumption

6.1
, for any
z\in\mathscr{D}
we have

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{z}\log f(z)\big|,\left|\partial_{z}\frac{f(z)}{f(z)+1}\right|,\big|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{B}}(z)\big|,\big|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{G}}(z)\big|,\big|\partial_{z}b(z)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])}.

The last two estimates also hold for
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
.

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{z}m(z)\big|\lesssim\displaystyle\frac{1}{\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]\big)};\qquad\big|\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}(z)\big|\lesssim 1;\qquad\bigg|\displaystyle\frac{1}{z-\mathfrak{b}}\bigg|+\bigg|\displaystyle\frac{1}{z-\mathfrak{a}}\bigg|\lesssim\displaystyle\frac{1}{\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]\big)},

our assumption (
6.3
) gives

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{z}\log f(z)\big|=\left|\partial_{z}m(z)+\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}(z)+\frac{1}{z-{\mathfrak{b}}}-\frac{1}{z-{\mathfrak{a}}}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}])}\leqslant\frac{1}{\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])}.

Since
{\mathcal{B}}(z)=\big(f(z)+1\big)(z-{\mathfrak{a}})
and
{\mathcal{G}}(z)=e^{m(z)}
, the same argument (together with (
6.5
)) gives the bound for
|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{B}}(z)|
and
|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{G}}(z)|
. The bound for
\partial_{z}\big(f(z)/(f(z)+1)\big)
follows along the same reasoning as the ones used to deduce (
5.50
) and (
5.51
), and is thus omitted. By taking the contour
\omega+
in (
6.10
) bounded away from
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
, we have
\big|\partial_{z}b(z)\big|=\big|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{B}}(z)\big|+\OO(1)\lesssim 1/\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)
.
∎

To study the transition probability (
6.1
), we introduce a new transition probability

(6.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}({\bm{e}})=a_{\bm{e}}=\frac{1}{\tilde{Z}({\bm{x}})}\frac{V({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{V({\bm{x}})}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\phi^{+}(x_{i})^{e_{i}}\phi^{-}(x_{i})^{1-e_{i}}.

Then we can write
\mathbb{P}
as a change of measure from
\mathbb{Q}
, namely,

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\cdot]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\Bigg[\cdot\exp\Bigg(\displaystyle\sum_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant m}\displaystyle\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})+\OO(1/n)\bigg)\Bigg]}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\Bigg[\exp\bigg(\displaystyle\sum_{1\leqslant i,j\leq m\leqslant m}\displaystyle\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})+\OO(1/n)\bigg)\Bigg]}.

We denote the empirical measure of
{\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}
by

(6.13)

\displaystyle\rho(x;{\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{1}(x\in[x_{i}+e_{i}/n,x_{i}+e_{i}/n+1/n]).

The main goal of this and next sections is to understand the difference of the empirical measures
\rho(x;{\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)
and
\rho({\bm{x}})
under the transition probability (
6.1
), and prove
Proposition

5.5
.
We will first prove the following proposition for the transition probability
\mathbb{Q}
as defined in (
6.11
). Then
Proposition

5.5
will be a consequence of it.

Proposition 6.4
.

Adopting
Assumption

6.1
, for any
z\in\mathscr{D}
, we have

(6.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}\right]&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)}\right),\end{split}

where the contour
\omega\subseteq\Lambda
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
, but not
z
. Moreover, for any integer
p\geqslant 1
,

(6.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}&\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}\right]\right|^{2p}\\
&=\OO\left(\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2}}\right)^{p}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n^{2p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{4p-2}}\right).\end{split}

6.2.
Dynamical Loop Equation

Our analysis of the transition probability (
6.11
) is based on the following dynamical loop equation from
[
Hua21
]
.

Lemma 6.5

(
[
Hua21
, Lemma 7.2]
)
.

Assume that the functions
\phi^{\pm}
from the transition probability
\mathbb{Q}
are analytic in a neighborhood of
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
. Further assume that the particle configuration
{\bm{x}}=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\in\mathbb{Z}_{n}^{m}
satisfies
{\mathfrak{a}}\leqslant x_{1}<x_{m}\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}-1/n
. Then the following function is analytic in a neighborhood of
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
,

(6.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}+(1-e_{i})/n}{z-x_{i}}\phi^{+}(z)+\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\phi^{-}(z)\right].

Remark 6.6
.

If
\phi^{\pm}
is meromorphic in a neighborhood
\Lambda
of
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
and does not have poles on the interval
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
, then
Lemma

6.5
implies that all possible poles of (
6.16
) are given by those of
\phi^{\pm}
.

Fix any large positive integer
R\geqslant 1
. We compute observables of the form

(6.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}},\quad\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}},

for either

(6.18)

\displaystyle 0\leqslant r\leqslant 2R-1,\quad{\bm{v}}=(v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}),\quad\bm{s}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{r}),\quad v_{j}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}}),\quad s_{j}=\pm 1,

or

(6.19)

\displaystyle r=2R,\quad{\bm{v}}=(v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}),\quad\bm{s}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{r}),\quad v_{2j-1}=\overline{v}_{2j}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}}),\quad s_{j}=-1.

For any
r\leqslant 2R
, to compute (
6.16
) with vectors
{\bm{v}},\bm{s}
as described in (
6.18
) or (
6.19
), we define the deformed weights

(6.20)

\displaystyle a_{\bm{e}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}=a_{\bm{e}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(x_{i})^{1-e_{i}},\quad\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)=\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{r}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{v_{j}-z-1/n}{v_{j}-z}\bigg)^{-s_{j}}.

To understand the transition probability (
6.11
) on mesoscopic scale, we need to take
v_{j}
close to
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
, where the function
\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
is singular. In fact,
\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
is meromorphic with possible poles at
v_{j}
if
s_{j}=-1
, or
v_{j}-1/n
if
s_{j}=1
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant r
.
For these deformed weights as in (
6.20
), we can absorb
\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
into
\phi^{-}(z)
, and then
Lemma

6.5
and Remark
6.6
imply that the following quantity is analytic in
\Lambda\setminus\{v_{j}-(s_{j}+1)/2n\}_{1\leqslant j\leqslant r}
,

(6.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)&=\sum_{{\bm{e}}}a^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{\bm{e}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}+(1-e_{i})/n}{z-x_{i}}\phi^{+}(z)+\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\phi^{-}(z)\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\right).\end{split}

We will use (
6.21
) to analyze the following quantity, which encodes the information of
\rho(x;{\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-\rho(x;{\bm{x}})
,

(6.22)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{e}}}a^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{\bm{e}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m}\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(x_{i})^{1-e_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right].

We may alternatively write
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
as

(6.23)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)=\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j})^{s_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right],

which are quantities in (
6.17
) up to some factors of
{\mathcal{G}}
. By sending
z\rightarrow\infty
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)=\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j})^{s_{j}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\right].

By taking
r=1
,
v_{1}=v
, and
s_{1}=-1
,
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
becomes

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)={\mathcal{G}}(v)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v-x_{i}}{v-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right],

and
the derivative
\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
after specializing at
z=v
gives the Stieltjes transform of
\rho(x;{\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-\rho(x;{\bm{x}})
,

(6.24)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}|_{z=v}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v-x_{i}}\right].

More generally, taking
r=2r^{\prime}+1
,
s_{1}=s_{2}=\cdots=s_{r^{\prime}}=1
and
s_{r^{\prime}+1}=s_{r^{\prime}+2}=\cdots s_{r}=-1
the derivative

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r^{\prime}}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}=\partial_{z}\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r^{\prime}}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right]

after specializing at
z=v_{r}=v,v_{j}=v_{j+r^{\prime}}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant r^{\prime}
, gives the
(r^{\prime}+1)
-th joint cumulants of

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v-x_{i}},\quad\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\overline{v}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\overline{v}-x_{i}}.

Using the definition (
6.2
), we rewrite the first part of
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
in (
6.21
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\sum_{{\bm{e}}}&a^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{\bm{e}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}+(1-e_{i})/n}{z-x_{i}}\phi^{+}(z)\\
&={\mathcal{G}}(z+1/n)\phi^{+}(z)\sum_{{\bm{e}}}a^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{\bm{e}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z+1/n-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z+1/n-x_{i}}\\
&={\mathcal{G}}(z+1/n)\phi^{+}(z){\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z+1/n)={\mathcal{G}}(z+1/n)\varphi^{+}(z+1/n){\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z+1/n)\frac{\tilde{g}(z)}{\tilde{g}(z+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(z)}.\end{split}

The second part of
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
is given by

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{e}}}a^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{\bm{e}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\phi^{-}(z)\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\varphi^{-}(z)\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z).

Then in this way we rewrite
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
as

(6.25)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z){\mathcal{B}}(z)\left(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\right)

where the complex slope
f(z)
is as in (
6.3
), and

(6.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)&={\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{0}(z)+\frac{\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-1}{f(z)+1},\\
\phantom{{}={}}{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{0}(z)&=\left(\frac{f(z+1/n)}{f(z+1/n)+1}\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z+1/n){\mathcal{B}}(z+1/n)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z){\mathcal{B}}(z)}\frac{\tilde{g}(z)}{\tilde{g}(z+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(z)}-\frac{f(z)}{f(z)+1}\right).\end{split}

6.3.
Liquid Region Estimates

The following proposition gives a weak bound on
\big|\partial_{z}(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-b(z))\big|
. It will eventually coincide with the required bound (
6.14
) in the liquid region. However, the estimate is suboptimal if
\dist(z,I)\geqslant\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z
, and is therefore not sufficient in the frozen region. In Section
7
, we will bootstrap it to obtain optimal estimates in the frozen region.

Proposition 6.7
.

Adopt
Assumption

6.1
, and fix an integer
R>1
. Then for
z\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
, and
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})=\big((v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}),(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{r})\big)
as in (
6.18
) or (
6.19
), we have

(6.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{z}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-b(z)\big)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}},\quad b(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z}\end{split}

where the contour
\omega\subseteq\mathscr{D}
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
but not
\{z\}\cup\big\{v_{j}-(s_{j}+1)/2n\big\}_{1\leqslant j\leqslant r}
. Let
1/n\ll\eta\leqslant{\mathfrak{r}}
. Then uniformly for
w\in\mathscr{D}
satisfying
\dist\big(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\cup\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}\big)\geqslant\eta
, the error term is bounded by

(6.28)

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{n\eta}.

We will deduce
Proposition

6.7
as a consequence of the following two propositions.
Proposition

6.8
gives estimates of
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
when
z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}
are bounded away from
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
.
Proposition

6.9
states that if we have some (weak) a priori estimate on
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
with
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta
, then we can obtain an improved estimate on
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
in the larger domain
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/2K)\eta
, for some large
K\geqslant 0
(which will be chosen later). Its proof, based on the discrete loop equations, will appear in
Section

6.4
below.

Proposition 6.8
.

Adopt
Assumption

6.1
, and fix an integer
R>1
. Let
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
be as in (
6.18
) or (
6.19
), with
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}
. The following estimates hold

(6.29)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}

\displaystyle=1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

and

(6.30)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}

\displaystyle=1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Proposition 6.9
.

Adopt
Assumption

6.1
, and fix an integer
R>1
. Further assume that there exists a parameter
n^{{\mathfrak{c}}-1}\leqslant\eta\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}
, and a constant
\mathfrak{C}>1
(independent of
\eta
) such that, for any
z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
satisfying
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta
, the following holds. If
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
is as in (
6.18
) then

(6.31)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}\leqslant\Big|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}\Big|\leqslant\mathfrak{C};\qquad\mathfrak{C}^{-1}\leqslant\Big|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}\Big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}},

and if
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
is as in (
6.19
) then

(6.32)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}\leqslant\Big|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}\Big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}.

Then there is a constant
K=K(R,\mathfrak{C})>1
such that the following holds.

(1)

Let
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
be as in (
6.18
) or (
6.19
), and let
w\in\mathscr{D}
satisfy
\dist\big(\{w,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
. If
\dist\big(w,\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}\big)\geqslant\eta/2K
, then the error term
{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
from (
6.26
) satisfies

(6.33)

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{n\eta}.

(2)

Let
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
be as in (
6.18
) or (
6.19
), and let
z\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
satisfy
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/2K)\eta
. Then,

(6.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\log{\mathcal{B}}(w)\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}\\
&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\log{\mathcal{B}}(w)\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Here, the contour
\omega
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
but not
\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}
; is contained inside the region
\big\{w\in\mathscr{D}:\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta\big\}
; and satisfies
\dist\big(\omega,\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}\big)\geqslant\eta/2K
. We additionally have the improved estimates

(6.35)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}

\displaystyle=1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta}\right),

and

(6.36)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}

\displaystyle=1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta}\right).

Given above propositions, we can quickly establish
Proposition

6.7
.

Proof of
Proposition

6.7
.

It follows from
Proposition

6.8
and the definitions (
6.8
) and (
6.10
) that

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}(z)\big|,\big|\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}(\infty)\big|\asymp 1;\qquad\displaystyle\max_{1\leqslant j\leqslant r}\Big\{\big|b(v_{j})\big|,\big|\log\mathcal{G}(v_{j})\big|\Big\}=\mathcal{O}\left(1\right),

whenever
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}
.
In particular, if we take
\eta_{0}=2{\mathfrak{r}}
, then there exists a constant
\mathfrak{C}>2
such that (
6.31
) holds if
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta_{0}
. Letting
K=K(R,\mathfrak{C})>1
denote the constant guaranteed by
Proposition

6.9
, define the sequence
\eta_{0},\eta_{1},\ldots
by

(6.37)

\displaystyle\eta_{i+1}=\bigg(1-\displaystyle\frac{1}{2K}\bigg)\eta_{i},\quad i\geqslant 0.

We show by induction on
i
that the proposition holds if
\eta=\eta_{i}
whenever
\eta_{i}\geqslant n^{{\mathfrak{c}}-1}
(meaning
i=\mathcal{O}(\log n)
). To that end, recall from the above that
Proposition

6.9
applies for
\eta=\eta_{0}
. Its second statement yields (
6.27
), and its first statement yields (
6.28
), where the
\eta
there is equal to
(1-1/2K)\eta_{0}=\eta_{1}
here. This verifies the proposition if
\eta=\eta_{1}
.

Moreover, the second statement of
Proposition

6.9
implies, for
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
as in (
6.18
) or (
6.19
), that we have the improved bounds

(6.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}=1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta}\right),\\
&{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j})))}=1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta}\right),\end{split}

whenever
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/2K)\eta=\eta_{1}
. In particular, this gives (
6.31
) and (
6.32
) if
\eta=\eta_{1}
(with the
\mathfrak{C}
there equal to the
\mathfrak{C}>2
here). Through the above argument, we see that if (
6.31
) and (
6.32
) hold for
\eta=\eta_{i}
, then
Proposition

6.9
implies that (
6.27
), (
6.28
), (
6.31
), and (
6.32
) all hold for
\eta=\eta_{i+1}
. After repeating this
\OO(\log n)
times, we conclude that (
6.27
) and (
6.28
) hold for any
z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r})
. This finishes the proof of
Proposition

6.7
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

6.8
.

For
w\in\mathscr{D}
, we can rewrite the decomposition (
6.25
) as

(6.39)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w){\mathcal{B}}(w)+{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w){\mathcal{B}}(w){\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w),

where

(6.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w){\mathcal{B}}(w){\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\varphi^{-}(w)(\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1)\\
&+{\mathcal{G}}(w+1/n)\varphi^{+}(w+1/n){\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)\frac{\tilde{g}(w)}{\tilde{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}-{\mathcal{G}}(w)\varphi^{+}(w){\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w).\end{split}

For
w\in\mathscr{D}
bounded away from
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}
, namely
\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\cup\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\})\geqslant{\mathfrak{r}}/2
, we have
|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)|,|{\mathcal{B}}(w)|\lesssim 1
,
|\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1|\lesssim 1/n
(recall from (
6.20
)), and
|\partial_{w}\log{\mathcal{G}}(w)|\lesssim 1
(from
Lemma

6.3
). Moreover, from the definition (
6.22
), the difference
|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)-{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)|
is bounded as

(6.41)

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{e}}}|a_{\bm{e}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}|\left|\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{w-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{w-x_{i}}\right|\left|\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{w+1/n-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{w+1/n-x_{i}}\frac{w-x_{i}}{w-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-1\right|\lesssim 1/n.

where the implicit constant depends on
{\mathfrak{r}}
.
The discussions above imply that the error term (
6.40
) is bounded by
\OO(1/n)
.

We recall the decomposition of
\log{\mathcal{B}}(w)
from (
6.10
), and multiply
\exp(-b^{+}(w))
on both sides of (
6.39
),

(6.42)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b^{+}(w)}={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}+\OO(1/n),

where we used
|b^{+}(w)|=\OO(1)
for
\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}
. Since the lefthand side of (
6.42
) is analytic for
\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}
, we can use a contour integral to get rid of it. For any
z
with
\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}
, we have

(6.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b^{+}(w)}{\rm d}w}{w-z}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{({\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}+\OO(1/n)){\rm d}w}{w-z}\\
&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}{\rm d}w}{w-z}+\OO(1/n)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{+}}\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}{\rm d}w}{w-z}-{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)}+\OO(1/n)\end{split}

where the contour
\omega
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
but not
\{z,v_{1},\cdots,v_{r}\}
, and the contour
\omega_{+}
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
z
. For the last equality, we used that
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
and
b(w)
are analytic outside the contour
\omega
.

As
w
approaches
\infty
, we have
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)+\OO(1/w)
and
b(w)=\OO(1/w)
. We can further deform the contour
\omega+
to infinity, and the first term on the righthand side of (
6.43
) can be computed

(6.44)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{+}}\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}{\rm d}w}{w-z}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{+}}\frac{({\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)+\OO(1/w)){\rm d}w}{w-z}={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty).

It follows by plugging (
6.44
) into (
6.43
), we get for any
\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant 2{\mathfrak{r}}
,

(6.45)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)}={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)+\OO(1/n).

Thanks to the relation (
6.45
), (
6.30
) implies (
6.29
). Thus we only need to prove (
6.30
). We prove (
6.30
) by induction on
r
. If
r=0
, then (
6.30
) holds trivially, since
{\mathcal{A}}(\infty)=1
. We assume (
6.30
) holds for
r
and prove it for
r+1
.
If
s_{j}=-1
for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant r+1
, let
\hat{\bm{v}}=(v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r})
and
\hat{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{r})
, then (
6.23
) and (
6.45
) together give

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{(\hat{\bm{v}},\hat{\bm{s}})}(\infty)-\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{r+1})-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)=\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v_{r+1})-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)=b(v_{r+1})+\OO(1/n).\end{split}

Thus it follows by rearranging that we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)&=\log{\mathcal{A}}^{(\hat{\bm{v}},\hat{\bm{s}})}(\infty)-\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{r+1})-b(v_{r+1})+\OO(1/n)\\
&=\log{\mathcal{A}}^{(\hat{\bm{v}},\hat{\bm{s}})}(\infty)+s_{r+1}\big(\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{r+1})+b(v_{r+1})\big)+\OO(1/n)\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{r+1}s_{j}\big(\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j})+b(v_{j})\big)+\OO(1/n),\end{split}

where we used the induction hypothesis in the last step.

Next suppose some
s_{j}=1
; we may assume (after permuting the
s_{j}
if necessary) that
s_{r+1}=1
. Letting
\hat{\bm{v}}=(v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r})
and
\hat{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{r})
, we have by similar reasoning that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{(\hat{\bm{v}},\hat{\bm{s}})}(v_{r+1})-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{(\hat{\bm{v}},\hat{\bm{s}})}(\infty)&=\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)-\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{r+1})-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{(\hat{\bm{v}},\hat{\bm{s}})}(\infty)=b(v_{r+1})+\OO(1/n).\end{split}

By rearranging and the induction hypothesis, it again follows that

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)=\sum_{j=1}^{r+1}s_{j}\big(\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j})+b(v_{j})\big)+\OO(1/n).

Hence, (
6.30
) holds for any
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
in (
6.18
) or (
6.19
). This finishes the proof of
Proposition

6.8
.

∎

6.4.
Proof of
Proposition

6.9

Before proving
Proposition

6.8
and
Proposition

6.9
, we first show the following proposition, which gives estimates on the derivatives of
\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
.

Proposition 6.10
.

Adopt the notation and assumptions of
Proposition

6.9
. There is a universal constant
K=K(R,\mathfrak{C})>1
(independent of
\eta
) such that the following holds. Let
z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
be complex numbers such that
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
. If
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
is as in (
6.18
) then

(6.46)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\big|,\big|\partial_{v_{j}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\big|,\big|\partial_{v_{j}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)\big|\lesssim 1/\eta,\quad 1\leqslant j\leqslant r,

and if
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
is as in (
6.19
) then

(6.47)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\big|,\big|\partial_{v_{j}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)\big|\lesssim 1/\eta,\quad 1\leqslant j\leqslant r,

where the implicit constants depend on
R
and
\mathfrak{C}
(but not
K
). In particular, for
\dist\big(\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
, we have the following. If
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
is as in (
6.18
), then

(6.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(2{\mathfrak{C}})^{-1}\leqslant\Big|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}\Big|\leqslant 2\mathfrak{C};\\
&(2\mathfrak{C})^{-1}\leqslant\Big|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}\Big|\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{C}},\end{split}

and if
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
is as in (
6.19
) then

(6.49)

\displaystyle(2{\mathfrak{C}})^{-1}\leqslant\Big|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}\Big|\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{C}}.

Proof.

For
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
as in (
6.19
), the weights
a_{{\bm{e}}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
in (
6.20
) are real and positive. We can rewrite
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
in terms of its residue decomposition

(6.50)

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}=1+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\Res_{z=x_{i}}}{z-x_{i}},

where its residue at
x_{i}
is given by

\displaystyle\Res_{z=x_{i}}:=-\frac{e_{i}}{n}\prod_{j:j\neq i}\frac{x_{i}-x_{j}-e_{j}/n}{x_{i}-x_{j}}\leqslant 0,

is nonpositive. We assume
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z\geqslant 0
, as the case
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z\leqslant 0
is entirely analogous.
Our assumption that
\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
implies that either
|\Imaginary z|\geqslant\min\{({\mathfrak{r}}n/m),1\}\eta/10
or
|\Imaginary z|\leqslant\min\{({\mathfrak{r}}n/m),1\}\eta/10
,
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z\not\in[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
\dist\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta/2
. We begin by considering the first case. Using (
6.50
), we have the estimate

(6.51)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right|\lesssim-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\Res_{z=x_{i}}}{|z-x_{i}|^{2}}=\frac{1}{\Imaginary z}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right],

and it follows that

(6.52)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\right|=\left|\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}\right|\lesssim\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\big]}{\big|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\big|\Imaginary z}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta}.

For the second case we recall that
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]=[{\mathfrak{a}}-{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{b}}+{\mathfrak{r}}]
, and
x_{i}\in[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
. Thus we have
|z-x_{i}|\geqslant|\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}[z]-x_{i}|\geqslant{\mathfrak{r}}+\eta/2\gtrsim 1+\eta
, and

(6.53)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right|\lesssim-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\Res_{z=x_{i}}}{|z-x_{i}|^{2}}\lesssim-\frac{1}{(1+\eta)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Res_{z=x_{i}}\lesssim\frac{1}{(1+\eta)^{2}}.

where in the last inequality, we used that from (
6.50
), by comparing the coefficient of
1/z
, the total residual is given by
0\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{m}e_{i}/n\leqslant m/n\lesssim 1
.
In the following we show that

(6.54)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right]\gtrsim 1.

Recalling that
a_{\bm{e}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
are real and nonnegative, then (
6.53
) and (
6.54
) together gives

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\right|=\left|\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}\right|\lesssim\frac{\sum_{{\bm{e}}}a_{\bm{e}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}\left|\partial_{z}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right|}{\sum_{{\bm{e}}}a_{\bm{e}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right]}\lesssim\frac{1}{(1+\eta)^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta}.

To prove (
6.54
), we can rewrite it as,

(6.55)

\displaystyle\left|\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right|\geqslant\prod_{i=1}^{m}\left(1-\frac{1}{n|z-x_{i}|}\right)\geqslant\prod_{i=1}^{m}\left(1-\frac{1}{n{\mathfrak{r}}}\right)\gtrsim 1.

and its argument is bounded by

(6.56)

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{m}\arg\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\left[\log\frac{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{z-x_{i}}\right]\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\left[-\frac{e_{i}}{n(z-x_{i})}+\OO\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n^{2}|z-x_{i}|^{2}}\right)\right]\leqslant\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}{n|z-x_{i}|^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\\
&\leqslant\frac{m}{n}\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}{{\mathfrak{r}}\dist(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}[z],[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\leqslant\frac{\min\{({\mathfrak{r}}n/m),1\}\eta/10}{({\mathfrak{r}}n/m)(\eta/2)}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\leqslant 1/2,\end{split}

where we used
|z-x_{i}|\geqslant{\mathfrak{r}}
and
|z-x_{i}|\geqslant\dist(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}[z],[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])
.
The claim (
6.54
) follows from combining (
6.55
) and (
6.56
). Thus in both cases we have
\left|\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\right|\lesssim 1/\eta
.

For
\partial_{v_{j}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)
and (
6.46
), we recall the expression of
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
from (
6.23
). Then
\partial_{v_{j}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)
in (
6.47
), or
\partial_{z}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z),\partial_{v_{j}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z),\partial_{v_{j}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)
as in (
6.46
) are all of the following form

(6.57)

\displaystyle\partial_{v_{k}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\right],\quad 0\leqslant r\leqslant 2R,\quad s_{j}=\pm 1,\quad v_{j}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}}),

and
\dist\big(v_{j},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
, with possibly an extra
\partial_{v_{k}}\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{k})
term. Thanks to
Lemma

6.3
, the derivative
|\partial_{v_{k}}\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{k})|\lesssim 1/\eta
. In the following we show that (
6.57
) is also bounded by
1/\eta
.

For complex numbers
\dist\big(\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
, we take
v_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r})
such that
\dist\big(v^{\prime}_{j},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta
, with
|v_{j}^{\prime}-v_{j}|\leqslant\eta/K
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant r
. Then, we have for any
1\leqslant j\leqslant r
,

(6.58)

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}^{\prime}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}^{\prime}-x_{i}}}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}e^{\OO\left(\frac{|v_{j}^{\prime}-v_{j}|}{n|v_{j}^{\prime}-x_{i}|^{2}}\right)}=\exp\Bigg(\mathcal{O}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{nK}\bigg)\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\displaystyle\frac{1}{|v_{j}^{\prime}-x_{i}|^{2}}\Bigg)=e^{\mathcal{O}(m/nK)}=e^{\OO(1/K)}.

Moreover, our assumptions (
6.31
) and (
6.32
) gives that for any
0\leqslant\hat{r}\leqslant 2R
the following holds

(6.59)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}\leqslant\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{\hat{r}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\hat{v}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{\hat{v}_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{\hat{s}_{j}}\right]e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{\hat{r}}\hat{s}_{j}b(\hat{v}_{j})}\right|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}},\quad\hat{s}_{j}=\pm 1,\quad\hat{v}_{j}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}}),

where
\dist(\hat{v}_{j},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant\eta
.

We may rewrite (
6.57
) as

(6.60)

\displaystyle\eqref{e:dervv}=\frac{\partial_{v_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\right]e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}b(v^{\prime}_{j})}}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\right]e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}b(v^{\prime}_{j})}}.

Let us verify that the denominator in (
6.60
) is bounded above and below by constants (that is, it is of order
1
). To that end, observe that

(6.61)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\right]e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}b(v^{\prime}_{j})}\\
&\qquad\qquad=\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}e^{\OO(1/K)}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}b(v^{\prime}_{j})},\end{split}

where we used (
6.58
) to replace
v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}
by
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime},\cdots,v_{r}^{\prime}
, which gives an extra factor
e^{\OO(1/K)}
. The leading order term in (
6.61
) will be given by (
6.59
) with
\hat{v}_{j}=v^{\prime}_{j}
and
\hat{s}_{j}=s_{j}
, and it is bounded below by
\mathfrak{C}^{-1}
and above by
\mathfrak{C}
.

We show the remainder is of order
\OO(1/K)
. Observe that this remainder is bounded by a constant multiple of

(6.62)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}}\right|^{s_{j}}e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}b(v^{\prime}_{j})}.

Letting
r^{\prime}=\lceil{r/2}\rceil
, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality gives

(6.63)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eqref{e:tobbb}&\leqslant\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\bar{v}^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{\bar{v}^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}(s_{j}b(v^{\prime}_{j})+s_{j}b(\bar{v}^{\prime}_{j}))}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}\prod_{j=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\bar{v}^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{\bar{v}^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}e^{-\sum_{j=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}(s_{j}b(v^{\prime}_{j})+s_{j}b(\bar{v}^{\prime}_{j}))}.\end{split}

The first term in (
6.63
) is in the form (
6.59
) with
\hat{r}=2\lceil{r/2}\rceil\leqslant 2R
,

\hat{\bm{v}}=(v_{1}^{\prime},\bar{v}^{\prime}_{1},v_{2}^{\prime},\bar{v}_{2}^{\prime},\cdots,v_{r^{\prime}}^{\prime},\bar{v}_{r^{\prime}}^{\prime})
and
\hat{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{1},\cdots,s_{r^{\prime}},s_{r^{\prime}})
. It is bounded by
{\mathfrak{C}}/K
. Similarly, the second term in (
6.63
) is also of form (
6.59
), and is bounded by
{\mathfrak{C}}/K
.
By taking
K
large enough, (
6.61
) is then bounded below by
\mathfrak{C}^{-1}-\mathcal{O}({\mathfrak{C}}/K)\geqslant(2\mathfrak{C})^{-1}
and above by
\mathfrak{C}+\mathcal{O}({\mathfrak{C}}/K)\leqslant 2\mathfrak{C}
.

For the numerator in (
6.60
), by the same argument we have

(6.64)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left|\partial_{v_{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}\right]e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v^{\prime}_{j})}\right|\\
&=\left|\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v_{k}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v_{k}-x_{i}}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v_{j}-x_{i}}\right)^{s_{j}}e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v^{\prime}_{j})}\right|\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\dist\big(v_{k},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)}\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{v^{\prime}_{j}-x_{i}}\right|^{s_{j}}e^{-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}b(v^{\prime}_{j})}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\dist\big(v_{k},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta}.\end{split}

We conclude from (
6.61
) and (
6.64
) that
|\eqref{e:dervv}|\lesssim 1/\eta
. This finishes the proof of (
6.46
) and (
6.47
).

The bounds (
6.48
) and (
6.49
) will follow as consequences of (
6.46
) and (
6.47
) by taking
K
large enough. Let us verify this for the second estimate in (
6.48
), on
|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}|
; the other bounds can be checked the same way. We take
z^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,v_{r}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r})
such that
|z-z^{\prime}|,|v_{j}^{\prime}-v_{j}|\leqslant\eta/K
and
\dist\big(z^{\prime},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big),\dist\big(v^{\prime}_{j},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant r
. Then,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z^{\prime})\right|=\left|\int^{z}_{z^{\prime}}\partial_{u}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(u){\rm d}u\right|&=\OO\left(\frac{1}{K}\right),\\
\big|\log b(w)-\log b(w^{\prime})\big|=\left|\int^{w}_{w^{\prime}}\partial_{u}\log b(u){\rm d}u\right|&=\mathcal{O}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\bigg),\\
\big|\log{\mathcal{G}}(w)-\log{\mathcal{G}}(w^{\prime})\big|=\left|\int^{w}_{w^{\prime}}\partial_{u}\log{\mathcal{G}}(u){\rm d}u\right|&=\OO\left(\frac{1}{K}\right),\end{split}

for
w=z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}
, where we used (
6.46
), (
6.47
) and
Lemma

6.3
to bound the derivative. Then, by taking
K
sufficiently large, we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2{\mathfrak{C}}}\leqslant\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathfrak{C}}+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\bigg)\leqslant|{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)-\sum_{j=1}^{r}s_{j}(b(v_{j})+\log{\mathcal{G}}(v_{j}))}|\leqslant\mathfrak{C}+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\bigg)\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{C}},

establishing the proposition.
∎

Now we can establish
Proposition

6.9
.

Proof of
Proposition

6.9
.

With the estimates (
6.46
) and (
6.47
) as input, we can analyze the error term (
6.26
) and prove (
6.33
).
We recall
{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{0}(w)
from (
6.26
), and rewrite it as

(6.65)

\displaystyle\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n){\mathcal{B}}(w+1/n)\tilde{g}(w)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w){\mathcal{B}}(w)\tilde{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}-1\right)+\left(\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}-\frac{f(w)}{f(w)+1}\right).

For the first term on the right side of (
6.65
), using (
6.46
) and (
6.47
), we have

(6.66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n){\mathcal{B}}(w+1/n)}}{{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w){\mathcal{B}}(w)}}\frac{\tilde{g}(w)}{\tilde{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}\\
&\qquad=\exp\Bigg(\int_{w}^{w+1/n}\partial_{u}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(u)+\log{\mathcal{B}}(u)\big){\rm d}u\Bigg)\left(1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)=1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta}\right),\end{split}

for
\dist\big(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
, where we also used the bound
\big|\partial_{u}\log{\mathcal{B}}(u)\big|\lesssim 1/\eta
from
Lemma

6.3
. For the second term on the right side of (
6.65
), using
Lemma

6.3
, we have

(6.67)

\displaystyle\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}-\frac{f(w)}{f(w)+1}=\int_{w}^{w+1/n}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{f(u)}{f(u)+1}\right){\rm d}u\lesssim\frac{1}{n\dist\big(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)}.

For the last term on the right side of (
6.26
), since
|f(w)+1|\gtrsim 1
we have

(6.68)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1}{f(w)+1}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{n\eta},

for
\dist\big(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
and
\dist\big(w,\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}\big)\geqslant\eta/2K
.
Combining (
6.66
), (
6.67
) and (
6.68
), we deduce the bound on
{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
,

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{n\eta},

provided
\dist\big(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
and
\dist\big(w,\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}\big)\geqslant\eta/2K
.
This finishes the proof of (
6.33
).

It therefore remains to establish the second statement of the proposition. To that end, we can rewrite (
6.25
) in the following linear form

(6.69)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}{{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}=\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}+\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{B}}(z)}{{\mathcal{B}}(z)}+\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}{1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}.

In the following we first prove that
\partial_{z}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)/{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
is analytic for
z
inside

(6.70)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}=\bigg(\mathscr{D}\cup\Big\{z:\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\leqslant{\mathfrak{r}}\Big\}\bigg)\cap\Big\{z\in\Lambda:\dist\big(z,\{v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}\big)\geqslant\eta/(2K)\Big\}\subseteq\Lambda.

The poles of
\partial_{z}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)/{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
are the zeros and poles of
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
. Thanks to the loop equation (
6.16
), we know that
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
is analytic and does not have poles in
\Lambda\setminus\big\{v_{j}-(s_{j}+1)/2n\big\}_{1\leqslant j\leqslant r}
. So, we only need to show that
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
does not have zeros inside
\Lambda_{0}
.
We recall the expression (
6.25
),

(6.71)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)={\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z){\mathcal{B}}(z)\left(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)\right).

For
z\in\Lambda\setminus[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
, by our
Assumption

6.1
,
{\mathcal{B}}(z)\neq 0
.

The estimate (
6.33
) implies that
1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)=1+\OO(1/n\eta)\neq 0
for
w\in\Lambda_{0}\cap\mathscr{D}
and
\dist\big(w,[\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
.
Next we show that
\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}(w)
is nonzero when
w\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
and
\dist\big(w,[\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
. By
Proposition

6.10
, (
6.48
) holds for
z\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
with
\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
. Thus
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\neq 0
for
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
as in (
6.18
). For
({\bm{v}},\bm{s})
as in (
6.19
), the weights
a_{\bm{e}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
are positive, the decomposition (
6.50
) implies that
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big]>0
for
\Imaginary w>0
. For
\Imaginary w=0
and
\dist\big(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta
, since each
x_{i}\in[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}-1/n]
, we have that

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{w-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}{w-x_{i}}>0.

So, the definition (
6.22
) of
\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}
and the positivity of the
a_{\bm{e}}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}
, together imply that
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)>0
. Thus on
\big\{w\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r}):\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta\big\}
, we have
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\neq 0
. We conclude from (
6.71
) that
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\neq 0
on
\Lambda_{0}\cap\big\{w\in\mathscr{D}:\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta\big\}
.

For
\big\{w\in\Lambda_{0}:\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])<(1-1/K)\eta\big\}
, we take a contour
\omega
inside the region
\big\{w\in\Lambda_{0}:\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta\big\}
, which encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
but not
v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}
, and whose distance from the set
\{v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{r}\}
is at least
\eta/(2K)
. We may count the zeros of
{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
inside
\omega
by

(6.72)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{\rm d}w=\sum_{p:{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(p)=0\atop\text{$p$ inside the contour}}1.

We recall the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
from (
6.22
) and the decomposition (
6.50
),

(6.73)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)=\sum_{\bm{e}}a_{\bm{e}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\Res_{w=x_{i}}}{w-x_{i}}\right).

It has the same number of zeros and poles, and we have shown that it does not have zeros or poles inside the region
\big\{w\in\mathscr{D}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}}):\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant(1-1/K)\eta\big\}
. Therefore,
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
has the same number of zeros and poles inside the contour
\omega
, so it follows that

(6.74)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{\rm d}w=0.

We perform the same contour integral on both sides of (
6.69
), we get

(6.75)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{p:{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(p)=0\atop\text{$p$ inside the contour}}1=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{\rm d}w&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\left(\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}+\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{B}}(w)}{{\mathcal{B}}(w)}+\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}\right){\rm d}w\\
&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\partial_{w}\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w,\end{split}

where in the last equality, we used (
6.9
) and (
6.74
). Thanks to (
6.33
), along the contour
\omega
,
|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)|\lesssim 1/n\eta\ll 1
. Since the left side of (
6.75
) is an integer, we conclude that the above integral is
0
and
\partial_{w}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)/{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
is analytic for
w\in\Lambda_{0}
.

From the discussion above,
\partial_{z}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)/{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
is analytic inside the contour
\omega
, we can use a contour integral to get rid of
\partial_{z}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)/{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
and recover
\partial_{z}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)/{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
,

(6.76)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w-z}&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\left(\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{B}}(w)}{{\mathcal{B}}(w)}+\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}}{1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}}-\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}{{\mathcal{C}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)}\right)\frac{{\rm d}w}{w-z}\\
&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{w}{\mathcal{B}}(w)}{{\mathcal{B}}(w)}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w-z}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}},\end{split}

where the contour
\omega
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
but not
\{z,v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}\}
, and is at distance at least
\eta/2K
from
z
. Here, we have used the fact (which follows from (
6.73
)) that
\big|\partial_{w}\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}(w)/\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}(w)\big|
tends to
0
as
w
tends to
\infty
. Along the contour
\omega
,
\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|\lesssim 1/n\eta
, so we conclude that

(6.77)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{z}{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta^{2}}\right).

This finishes the proof of (
6.34
).

By first integrating (
6.76
) from
z_{2}
to
z_{1}
, and then applying (
6.33
), we get

(6.78)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z_{1})-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z_{2})&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\Big(\log{\mathcal{B}}(w)+\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\Big)\left(\frac{1}{w-z_{1}}-\frac{1}{w-z_{2}}\right){\rm d}w\\
&=b(z_{1})-b(z_{2})+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta}\right).\end{split}

We can also take
z_{2}=\infty
in (
6.78
) to obtain

(6.79)

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)=b(z)+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n\eta}\right).

Thanks to the relation (
6.79
), (
6.36
) implies (
6.35
). Thus we only need to prove (
6.36
). The relation (
6.79
) is almost the same as (
6.45
), but with an error depending on
\eta
.
The same induction argument after (
6.45
) establishes (
6.36
), and
Proposition

6.9
.

∎

7.
Improved Edge Estimates

In this section, we strengthen the weak estimate
Proposition

6.7
through bootstrapping, and conclude the proof of Propositions
5.5
and
6.4
. We collect some notations, and miscellaneous derivative bounds in Section
7.1
. We prove the first statement (
6.14
) of
Proposition

6.4
in Section
7.2
, and the second statement (
6.15
) in Section
7.3
. Finally, in Section
7.4
, we prove
Proposition

5.5
using
Proposition

6.4
as input.

7.1.
Notations and Preliminary Estimates

In this section, we collect some notations, and miscellaneous derivative bounds, which will be used to prove
Proposition

6.4
in Sections
7.2
and
7.3
.

To prove (
6.14
), we take
r=1
,
s_{1}=-1
, and
v_{1}=v
in (
6.27
),

(7.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{z}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-b(z)\big)&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}},\end{split}

where the contour
\omega\subseteq\mathscr{D}
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
but not
z
,
\overline{z}
, or
v
. We recall from (
6.24
) that (
6.14
) is equivalent to

(7.2)

\displaystyle\bigg|\partial_{z}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-b(z)\big)\Big|_{z=v}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)}.

By the definition (
6.27
),
b(z)=\OO(1/z)
as
z\rightarrow\infty
. Thus,
by integrating (
7.1
) from
z
to
\infty
, we get

(7.3)

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)-b(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{{\rm d}w}{w-z}.

By taking a contour integral on both sides of (
7.3
), we get for any
k_{1}+k_{2}\geqslant 1
that

(7.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega^{\prime}}\frac{\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}&=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint_{w\in\omega}\oint_{w^{\prime}\in\omega^{\prime}}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w{\rm d}w^{\prime}}{(w-w^{\prime})(w^{\prime}-z)^{k_{1}}(w^{\prime}-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\\
&=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}},\end{split}

where the contour
\omega^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{D}
encloses
\omega
but not
z,\overline{z}
, or
v,\overline{v}
. In the following, we analyze (
7.4
), which is more general than (
7.1
). In particular, by taking
k_{1}=2
and
k_{2}=0
in (
7.4
), we recover (
7.1
), where we are using the facts that
\log\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}(\infty)
is bounded (in a way dependent on the
v_{j}
, by (
6.32
)); that
b(\infty)=0
; and that
\log\mathcal{A}^{(\bm{v},\bm{s})}(z)-b(z)
has no poles outside of
\omega^{\prime}
.

We recall the spectral region
\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})=\big\{z\in\mathscr{D}:\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant{\mathfrak{r}}\big\}
. Let
v\in\mathscr{D}\setminus\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
. We will later take either
z\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
, or
z\in\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r})
in (
7.1
). We divide them into three cases.
If
z\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
, then we deform the contour
\omega
in (
7.4
) to see that its right side equals

(7.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{v,\overline{v}}}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}.

If
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})\setminus\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
we deform the contour
\omega
in (
7.4
) to

(7.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{v,\overline{v}}}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}.

If
z\in\mathscr{D}(2\mathfrak{r})
,
we deform the contour
\omega
in (
7.4
) to

(7.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{v,\overline{v}}}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\widehat{\omega}+}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}.

Here, in all three cases, the contour
\omega_{v,\overline{v}}
encloses
v
and
\overline{v}
; the contour
\omega_{z,\overline{z}}
encloses
z
and
\overline{z}
; the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
v,\overline{v},z,\overline{z}
;
and the contour
\widehat{\omega}+\subseteq\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})\setminus\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
v,\overline{v}
, but not
z,\overline{z}
. See Figure
13
.

\bar{v}
