Figure 13.
Shown to the left is the case
z\in\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
, where the contour
\omega_{v,\overline{v}}
consists of two small circles enclosing
v,\overline{v}
respectively, and the contour
\widehat{\omega}+\subseteq\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})\setminus\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
v,\overline{v}
but not
z
.
Shown to the right is the case
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})\setminus\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
or
z=v,\bar{v}
, where the contour
\omega_{v,\overline{v}}
encloses
v,\overline{v}
, the contour
\omega_{z,\overline{z}}
encloses
z,\overline{z}
, and the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
v,\overline{v},z,\overline{z}
.

We recall
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
from (
6.26
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)={\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{0}(z)+\frac{\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-1}{f(z)+1},\\
&{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{0}(z)=\left(\frac{f(z+1/n)}{f(z+1/n)+1}\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z+1/n){\mathcal{B}}(z+1/n)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z){\mathcal{B}}(z)}\frac{\tilde{g}(z)}{\tilde{g}(z+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(z)}-\frac{f(z)}{f(z)+1}\right),\end{split}

and
b(z)
from (
6.10
),

\displaystyle b(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z}=-\log{\mathcal{B}}(z)+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z},

where the new contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
encloses both
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
z
. We introduce

\displaystyle\hat{g}(z)=\tilde{g}(z)\exp\Bigg(-{\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{w-z}}\Bigg),

where the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
encloses both
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
z
. Similar to
\tilde{g}(z)
(by
Assumption

6.1
), the function
\hat{g}(z)
is analytic in
\Lambda
and does not have zeros or poles in
\Lambda
. Moreover, we have

(7.8)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{z}\log\hat{g}(z)\big|\leqslant\big|\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}(z)\big|+\frac{1}{2\pi}\left|\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log{\mathcal{B}}(w){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{2}}\right|\lesssim 1,\quad\left|\frac{\hat{g}(z)}{\hat{g}(z+1/n)}-1\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{n},

by taking the contour
\omega+
to be bounded away from
z
, together with the fact that
\mathcal{B}(w)=\big(f(w)+1\big)(w-\mathfrak{a})
is bounded above and below for
w
bounded away from
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
.

With
\hat{g}(z)
, we can rewrite
{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{0}(z)
as

(7.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{f(z+1/n)}{f(z+1/n)+1}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z+1/n)e^{-b(z+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)}}\frac{\hat{g}(z)}{\hat{g}(z+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(z)}-1\right)+\frac{f(z+1/n)}{f(z+1/n)+1}-\frac{f(z)}{f(z)+1}.\end{split}

To estimate (
7.5
), (
7.6
) and (
7.7
), for any function
h
, which is analytic in small neighborhoods of
z,\overline{z}
, and
k\geqslant 0
, we let
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}h
denote the set of values of the form

(7.10)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{h(w)}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\mathrm{d}w,\quad k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1,

where the contour
\omega_{z,\overline{z}}
encloses
z,\overline{z}
. Observe that the expression (
7.10
) contains the first
k
derivatives of
h
(up to some constant) at
z,\overline{z}
, by taking
k_{1}=k+1,k_{2}=0
or
k_{1}=0,k_{2}=k+1
.
Although
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}h
is a set of numbers, with a slight abuse of notation, later we will simply treat
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}h
as a number, which can be any number in
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}h
.
We consider the product of two functions,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{h_{1}(w)h_{2}(w)}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\mathrm{d}w.

If
k_{2}=0
(and, similarly, if
k_{1}=0
), by the chain rule we have that

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\frac{(k_{1}-1)!}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{h_{1}(w)h_{2}(w)}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}}\mathrm{d}w=\partial_{z}^{k_{1}-1}\big(h_{1}(z)h_{2}(z)\big),

is a linear combination of

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{h_{1}(w)}{(w-z)^{k_{1}-a}}\mathrm{d}w\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{h_{2}(w)}{(w-z)^{a+1}}\mathrm{d}w,\quad 0\leqslant a<k_{1}.

More generally, for
k_{1},k_{2}\geqslant 1
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}(k_{1}-1)!(k_{2}-1)!&\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{h_{1}(w)h_{2}(w)}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\mathrm{d}w\\
&=\partial_{z}^{k_{1}-1}\partial_{\overline{z}}^{k_{2}-1}\left(\frac{h_{1}(z)h_{2}(z)-h_{1}(\overline{z})h_{2}(\overline{z})}{z-\overline{z}}\right)\\
&=\partial_{z}^{k_{1}-1}\partial_{\overline{z}}^{k_{2}-1}\left(\frac{h_{1}(z)-h_{1}(\overline{z})}{z-\overline{z}}h_{2}(z)+\frac{h_{2}(z)-h_{2}(\overline{z})}{z-\overline{z}}h_{1}(\overline{z})\right),\end{split}

which can be written as a linear combination of terms in the form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint\frac{h_{1}(w)\mathrm{d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}-a}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint\frac{h_{2}(w)\mathrm{d}w}{(w-z)^{a+1}},\quad 0\leqslant a<k_{1},

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint\frac{h_{2}(w)\mathrm{d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}-a}}\cdot\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint\frac{h_{1}(w)\mathrm{d}w}{(w-\overline{z})^{a+1}},\quad 0\leqslant a<k_{2}.

Therefore,
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}(h_{1}h_{2})
is a linear combination of
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k_{1})}(h_{1}){\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k_{2})}(h_{2})
, with
k_{1}+k_{2}=k
(and uniformly bounded coefficients). We write this by

(7.11)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}(h_{1}h_{2})\in\text{LinComb}_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k_{1})}(h_{1}){\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k_{2})}(h_{2}).

If
h(w)
is uniformly bounded (that is, if
\big|h(w)\big|=\mathcal{O}(1)
) for
|w-z|,|w-\bar{z}|\lesssim\eta
, we can take the contour
\omega_{z,\overline{z}}
in (
7.10
) as two small circles centered around
z,\bar{z}
with radius of order
\eta
. Then we have the trivial bound for any element of
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}h
, given by

(7.12)

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}h|=\left|\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{h(w)\mathrm{d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\right|\lesssim\oint_{\omega_{z,\overline{z}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}w}{\eta^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta^{k}},\qquad\text{for $k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1$},

which will be used repeatedly in the remaining of this section.

For the complex slope
f(z)
, we recall from (
6.6
) that
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[f(x+0\mathrm{i})/(f(x+\mathrm{i})+1)\big]
defines a negative measure on
{\mathbb{R}}
with support
I
. Thanks to (
6.5
), as in the fourth statement of
Proposition

5.3
we have for any
z\in\mathscr{D}
and
k\geqslant 1
that

(7.13)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{f(z)}{f(z)+1}\right|\lesssim 1,\quad\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\frac{f}{f+1}\right|\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/f(z)+1]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k-1}},\quad\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/f(z)+1]|}{\Imaginary z}\lesssim\frac{1}{\dist(z,I)}.

We remark that the second estimate in (
7.13
) is slightly more general than (
5.50
), but it can be proven essentially the same way as in (
5.53
), by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|{\mathcal{D}}^{(k)}_{z}\frac{f}{f+1}\right|&=\left|\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{I}\frac{k!\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[f(x+\mathrm{i}0)/(f(x+\mathrm{i}0)+1)\big]{\rm d}x}{(x-z)^{k_{1}}(x-\bar{z})^{k_{2}}}\right|+\OO(1)\\
&\lesssim\Bigg|\displaystyle\frac{1}{\dist(z,I)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\displaystyle\int_{I}\displaystyle\frac{k!\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[f(x+\mathrm{i}0)/(f(x+\mathrm{i}0)+1)\big]\mathrm{d}x}{|x-z|^{2}}\Bigg|+\mathcal{O}(1)\\
&\lesssim\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]\big|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k-1}},\end{split}

where
k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1
.

The last two relations in (
7.13
) together with (
7.11
) implies for any
k\geqslant 1
and
j\geqslant 0
,

(7.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\left(\frac{f}{f+1}\right)^{j}&\lesssim\sum\left|\prod_{u=1}^{j}{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(\tilde{k}_{u})}\left(\frac{f}{f+1}\right)\right|\\
&\lesssim\sum\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/f(z)+1]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}\right)^{j}\frac{1}{\dist(z,I)^{\sum_{u=1}^{j}(k_{u}-1)}}\\
&\lesssim\sum\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/f(z)+1]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]}\frac{1}{\dist(z,I)^{j-1}}\frac{1}{\dist(z,I)^{k-j}}\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/f(z)+1]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k-1}},\end{split}

where the sum is over index sets
(\tilde{k}_{1},\tilde{k}_{2},\ldots,\tilde{k}_{j})
such that
\sum_{u=1}^{j}\tilde{k}_{u}=k
; and in the second line we used the last estimate in (
7.13
).

7.2.
Proof of Statement (
5.74
)

We recall from our
Assumption

6.1
that
\mathscr{D}\subseteq\big\{z\in\Lambda:\dist(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant n^{{\mathfrak{c}}-1}\big\}
. We fix a large constant

(7.15)

\displaystyle q=4\lceil{R/{\mathfrak{c}}}\rceil.

For any
z\in\mathscr{D}
, we define the control parameters

(7.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Phi_{k}(z)=\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k}}+\frac{1}{\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{q}}\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{q-k},\qquad&\text{for $0\leqslant k\leqslant q$};\\
\Phi_{k}(v)=\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{-k},\qquad&\text{for $k>q$}.\end{aligned}

Since
q\geqslant 4/{\mathfrak{c}}
, for
k=1
, we have

(7.17)

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(z)=\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)}+\OO\left(n\left(\frac{\log n}{n^{\mathfrak{c}}}\right)^{q}\right)\asymp\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)},

where we used the lower bound
|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\gtrsim 1
from (
6.4
).

For
z\in\mathscr{D}
,
\Phi_{k}(z)\lesssim\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{-k}
. Thus for
k+k^{\prime}>q
, we have

\Phi_{k}(z)\Phi_{k^{\prime}}(z)\lesssim\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{-k-k^{\prime}}=\Phi_{k+k^{\prime}}(z)
. For
k+k^{\prime}\leqslant q
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Phi_{k}(z)\Phi_{k^{\prime}}(z)&\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k}}\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k^{\prime}}}+\Phi_{k}(z)\frac{1}{\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{q}}\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{q-{k^{\prime}}}\\
&\qquad+\Phi_{k^{\prime}}(z)\frac{1}{\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{q}}\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{q-{k}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k+k^{\prime}}}+\frac{1}{\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{q}}\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{q-k-k^{\prime}}=\Phi_{k+k^{\prime}}(z),\end{split}

where for the last line, we used
\Phi_{k}(z)\lesssim\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{-k}\ll(n/\log n)^{k}
. Thus
\Phi_{k}(z)
satisfies

(7.18)

\displaystyle\Phi_{k}(z)\Phi_{k^{\prime}}(z)\lesssim\Phi_{k+k^{\prime}}(z).

The statement (
7.2
) will eventually be a consequence of the following proposition, which states that if we have some (weak) a priori estimate on
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
, then the dynamical loop equation can be used to obtain an improved estimate on
{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Adopt
Assumption

6.1
. Suppose that
r=1
,
s_{1}=-1
, and
v_{1}=v\in\mathscr{D}\setminus\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
; let
z
be a complex number with either
z\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
or
z\in\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r})
. Further assume for any
1\leqslant k\leqslant 2q
(as defined in (
7.15
)) we have

(7.19)

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big|\leqslant\log n\cdot\Phi_{k}(v).

Then, we have the improved estimate

(7.20)

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}\Phi_{k}(v),\quad 1\leqslant k\leqslant 2q,

for some constant
{\mathfrak{C}}=\mathfrak{C}(R)>1
.

Proof.

In the following we prove (
7.20
) for
z=v
; the proofs in the other cases when
z=\overline{v}
or
z\in\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
follow from essentially the same argument. We denote
\eta=\dist\big(v,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)
. Thanks to (
6.79
), for any
z\in\mathscr{D}
with
\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\gtrsim\eta
, we have that

(7.21)

\displaystyle\left|\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)-\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(\infty)-b(z)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{n\eta}\lesssim 1.

For
q\leqslant k\leqslant 2q
, (
7.20
) follows from (
7.21
) and the trivial bound (
7.12
),

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(k)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta^{k}}=\Phi_{k}(v).

For
k\leqslant q
, we recall that from (
7.5
),
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(k)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)+b(w)\big)
is a collection of values in the form,

(7.22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{v,\overline{v}}}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}},\quad k_{1}+k_{2}=k+1,

where the contour
\omega_{v,\overline{v}}
encloses
v,\overline{v}
, and the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
v,\overline{v}
.

From our construction,
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
is analytic for
w\in\mathscr{D}
, since all the denominators in (
7.9
) are bounded away from
0
. However,

\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1=1/n(w-v)
has a pole at
v
. Along
\big\{w\in\mathbb{C}:|w-v|=\eta/3\big\}
and
\big\{w\in\mathbb{C}:|w-\overline{v}|=\eta/3\big\}
, (
6.28
) gives
\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|\lesssim 1/n\eta
. So, we can expand
\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)
as

(7.23)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{j=1}^{q}&\frac{(-1)^{j-1}\big({\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)^{j}}{j}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q+1}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=1}^{q}\frac{(-1)^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}}{j_{1}+j_{2}}{j_{1}+j_{2}\choose j_{1}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)^{j_{1}}\left(\frac{\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q+1}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{j_{1}+j_{2}=1}^{q}\frac{(-1)^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}}{j_{1}+j_{2}}{j_{1}+j_{2}\choose j_{1}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)^{j_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}}\frac{1}{n^{j_{2}}(w-v)^{j_{2}}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q+1}}\right).\end{split}

Thanks to the trivial bound (
7.12
), the contribution from the error term
\OO((n\eta)^{-(q+1)})
is bounded by
\OO((n\eta)^{-(q+1)}\eta^{-k})
, which is smaller than
\Phi_{k}(v)
.
By plugging (
7.23
) into the first term in (
7.22
), we get expressions involving linear combinations (with bounded coefficients) of quantities of the form

(7.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{v,\overline{v}}}&\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)^{j_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}w}{n^{j_{2}}(w-v)^{k_{1}+j_{2}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\in\frac{1}{n^{j_{2}}}{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(k_{1}+k_{2}+j_{2}-1)}\left(\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)^{j_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}}\right),\end{split}

where
1\leqslant j_{1}+j_{2}\leqslant q
.
Thanks to (
7.11
), (
7.24
) decomposes to sum of products involving

(7.25)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{j_{2}}}{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}_{1})}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)^{j_{1}}{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}_{2})}\left(\frac{1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}}\quad\hat{k}_{1}+\hat{k}_{2}=k_{1}+k_{2}+j_{2}-1=k+j_{2}.

In the following we show that under our hypothesis (
7.19
), for
0\leqslant\hat{k}\leqslant 2q
,

(7.26)

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k})}({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}}(v),

which together with (
7.18
) and (
7.11
), would imply

(7.27)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}_{1})}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}\right)^{j_{1}}\lesssim\sum\left|\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\tilde{k}_{u})}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}\right)\right|\lesssim\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\Phi_{\tilde{k}_{u}}(v)\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}_{1}}(v),\quad j_{1}\geqslant 1,

where the sum is over index sets
(\tilde{k}_{1},\tilde{k}_{2},\ldots,\tilde{k}_{j_{1}})
such that
\sum_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\tilde{k}_{u}=\hat{k}_{1}
.

For
q\leqslant{\hat{k}}\leqslant 2q
, (
7.26
) follows from the trivial bound (
7.12
),

{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{({\hat{k}})}({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})})\lesssim\eta^{-\hat{k}}=\Phi_{\hat{k}}(v)
.
For
{\hat{k}}\leqslant q
, we recall the expression of
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
from (
7.9
). For the last term in (
7.9
) we have

(7.28)

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{({\hat{k}})}\left(\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}-\frac{f(w)}{f(w)+1}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/f(v)+1]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}}}\leqslant\Phi_{\hat{k}}(v),\quad{\hat{k}}\geqslant 0,

where we used (
7.13
).
Next we need to estimate the first term in (
7.9
)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{({\hat{k}})}\Bigg(\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\frac{\hat{g}(w)}{\hat{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}-1\right)\Bigg),

which, thanks to (
7.11
), is a linear combination of

(7.29)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime})}\left(\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}\right){\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime})}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\frac{\hat{g}(w)}{\hat{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}-1\right).

If
\hat{k}^{\prime}=0
, we can use (
7.8
) to bound the second term of (
7.29
) as

(7.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v+1/n)e^{-b(v+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v)e^{-b(v)}}\frac{\hat{g}(v)}{\hat{g}(v+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(v)}-1=\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v+1/n)e^{-b(v+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v)e^{-b(v)}}-1+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).\end{split}

For the first term on the right side of (
7.30
), by a Taylor expansion, using (
7.19
), we have

(7.31)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Bigg|\log\bigg(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v+1/n)e^{-b(v+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v)e^{-b(v)}}\bigg)\Bigg|&\lesssim\left|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\frac{\partial_{v}^{j}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v)-b(v)\big)}{n^{j}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q+1}}\right)\right|\\
&\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{q}n^{-j}\log n\cdot\Phi_{j}(v)+\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q+1}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\log n|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]|}{n^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\lesssim\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}v}\ll 1,\end{split}

where we used
\Phi_{j}(v)/n^{j}
is decreasing in
j
,
q\geqslant 1/{\mathfrak{c}}
thus
1/(n\eta)^{q+1}\leqslant 1/n
, and the bound
n\dist(v,I)\geqslant n^{{\mathfrak{c}}}\gg\log n
. By plugging (
7.31
) into (
7.30
), we get

(7.32)

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v+1/n)e^{-b(v+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(v)e^{-b(v)}}\frac{\hat{g}(v)}{\hat{g}(v+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(v)}-1=\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]}\right).

Then for
\hat{k}^{\prime}=0
, by plugging (
7.32
) into (
7.29
), and using (
7.13
), we have if
\hat{k}\geqslant 1
,

(7.33)

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:linear}|\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/f(v)+1]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}-1}}\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]}\right)\leqslant\Phi_{\hat{k}}(v),

or
\hat{k}=0
,

(7.34)

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:linear}|\lesssim\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]}\right)\leqslant\Phi_{0}(v).

For
\hat{k}^{\prime}\geqslant 1
, using (
7.11
) again, the second term on the right side of (
7.29
) is a linear combination of quantities of the form

(7.35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime}-\hat{k}^{\prime\prime})}\left(\frac{\hat{g}(w)}{\hat{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}\right){\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime\prime})}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right|\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)^{\bm{1}(\hat{k}^{\prime}-\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1)}\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime\prime})}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right|,\end{split}

where we used (
7.8
), as well as the analyticity of
\hat{g}
and
\psi
on
\Lambda
(enabling the use of (
7.12
) with
\eta
of order
1
), to bound the first term.

If
\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}=0
, we can bound the last term on the right side of (
7.35
) by
\OO(1)
using (
7.31
). By plugging (
7.35
) into (
7.29
) and using (
7.13
), the sum of such terms with
\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}=0
is bounded

(7.36)

\displaystyle\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime}-1}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)^{\bm{1}(\hat{k}^{\prime}-\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1)}\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}}(v).

To estimate the second term on the right side of (
7.35
) with
1\leqslant\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}\leqslant q
, we first show that

(7.37)

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime\prime})}\log\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right|\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}(v).

By a Taylor expansion, we have

(7.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\Bigg|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime\prime})}\log\bigg(&\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\bigg)\Bigg|\\
&\lesssim\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{({\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}})}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{q-{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}}\frac{\partial_{w}^{j}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)}{n^{j}}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q-{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}+1}}\right)\right)\right|\\
&\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{q-{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}}n^{-j}\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{({\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}+j)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big|+\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{({\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}})}\OO\left(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q-{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}+1}}\right)\right|\\
&\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{q-{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}}n^{-j}\log n\cdot\Phi_{{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}+j}(v)+\frac{1}{n\eta}\frac{1}{\eta^{q}}\frac{1}{n^{q-{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}}}\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}(v),\end{split}

where in the third inequality we used our assumption (
7.19
) to bound the sum, and the trivial bound (
7.12
) for the last term. In the last line, we used that
n\dist(v,I)\geqslant n^{{\mathfrak{c}}}\gg\log n
, so
n^{-j}\log n\cdot\Phi_{{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}+j}(v)\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}(v)
for
j\geqslant 1
.
Thanks to (
7.11
) and the relation (
7.18
), (
7.38
) implies that

(7.39)

\displaystyle\Bigg|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{({\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}})}\bigg(\log\Big(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\Big)\bigg)^{j}\Bigg|\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}(v),\quad j\geqslant 1.

Using (
7.39
) as input, we get the following bound for the second term on the right side of (
7.35
),

(7.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime\prime})}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right|\\
&\qquad=\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime\prime})}\Bigg(1+\frac{1}{j!}\sum_{j=1}^{q-\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}\bigg(\log\Big(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\Big)\bigg)^{j}+\OO\bigg(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q-\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}+1}}\bigg)\Bigg)\right|\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}(v),\end{split}

for
1\leqslant\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}\leqslant q
. By plugging (
7.40
) and (
7.35
) into (
7.29
), the sum of such terms with
\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1
is bounded by

(7.41)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}=1}^{\hat{k}^{\prime}}\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime}-1}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)^{\bm{1}(\hat{k}^{\prime}-\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 1)}\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}(v)\lesssim\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime}-1}}\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime}}(v)\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime}}}\left(\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}^{\prime}}}+\frac{1}{\eta^{q}}\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{q-\hat{k}^{\prime}}\right)\leqslant\Phi_{\hat{k}}(v),\end{split}

where we used that
\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}}(v)
is nondecreasing in
\hat{k}^{\prime\prime}
in the first line; in the second line we used the last estimate in (
7.13
).

We conclude from (
7.33
), (
7.34
), (
7.36
) and (
7.41
) that for
0\leqslant\hat{k}\leqslant q
,

(7.42)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\Bigg|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k})}\bigg(\frac{f(z+1/n)}{f(z+1/n)+1}\Big(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z+1/n)e^{-b(z+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)e^{-b(z)}}\frac{\hat{g}(z)}{\hat{g}(z+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(z)}-1\Big)\bigg)\Bigg|\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}}(v).\end{split}

The claim (
7.26
) follows from combining (
7.28
) and (
7.42
).

Now we can use (
7.14
) and (
7.27
) to upper bound (
7.25
). There are several cases,
If
\hat{k}_{1}=0
, then
\hat{k}_{2}=k+j_{2}
. We may assume that
j_{2}\geqslant 1
, for otherwise the second factor in (
7.25
) vanishes. In this case, (
7.25
) becomes

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:tt1copy}|\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{j_{2}}}{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(k+j_{2})}\left(\left(\frac{1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}}\right)\lesssim\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/f(v)+1]}{n^{j_{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{k+j_{2}-1}}\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v).

If
\hat{k}_{2}=0
, then
\hat{k}_{1}=k+j_{2}
. We may assume that
j_{1}\geqslant 1
(for otherwise the first factor in (
7.25
) vanishes). In this case, (
7.25
) becomes

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:tt1copy}|\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{j_{2}}}{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{k+j_{2}}\left(({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w))^{j_{1}}\right)\lesssim\frac{\Phi_{k+j_{2}}(v)}{n^{j_{2}}}\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v).

If
\hat{k}_{1},\hat{k}_{2}\geqslant 1
, then we may assume that
j_{1},j_{2}\geqslant 1
, and (
7.25
) becomes

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:tt1copy}|\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}_{1}}(v)\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/f(v)+1]}{n^{j_{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}_{2}-1}}\lesssim n^{-(j_{2}-1)}\Phi_{\hat{k}_{1}}(v)\Phi_{\hat{k}_{2}-1}(v)\lesssim n^{-(j_{2}-1)}\Phi_{k+j_{2}-1}(v)\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v).

It follows that the first term in (
7.22
) is bounded by

(7.43)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega_{v,\overline{v}}}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}}\right|\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v).

For the second term in (
7.22
), we may deform
\omega+
so that it is bounded away from
v
. Then, any
w\in\omega+
is bounded away from
v
, so (
6.26
) gives

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1}{f(w)+1}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{n},\qquad\text{meaning}\qquad\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|=\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}_{0}(w)\big|+\OO\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Thus it follows

(7.44)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega+}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}}\right|\lesssim\max_{w\in\omega+}\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{n}+\max_{w\in\omega+}\big|{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|.

For the last term on the right side, recall the expression of
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
from (
7.9
)

(7.45)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)|&\lesssim\left|\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\frac{\hat{g}(w)}{\hat{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}-1\right|+\left|\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}-\frac{f(w)}{f(w)+1}\right|\\
&\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)}+\frac{1}{n}\lesssim\Phi_{1}(v)\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v),\end{split}

where in the first line, the first term on the right side is bounded as in (
7.32
), the second term is bounded by using (
7.13
) and the fact that
w
is bounded away from
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
; for the second line, we used (
6.4
). We conclude from plugging (
7.43
), (
7.44
) and (
7.45
) into (
7.5
) that

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(k)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big|=|\eqref{e:toest1}|\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v).

This finishes the proof of
Proposition

7.1
, by taking
{\mathfrak{C}}
larger than the above implicit constant.
∎

Now we can establish the first statement of
Proposition

6.4
, given by equation (
6.14
).

Proof of (
6.14
).

We recall from (
7.2
) and (
7.17
) that (
6.14
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(1)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big|\lesssim\Phi_{1}(v),

where
\Phi_{1}(v)
is explicitly given by (
7.17
).
Thanks to
Proposition

7.1
, a weak estimate of the form,

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(k)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big|\leqslant\log n\cdot\Phi_{k}(v),\qquad\text{for $v\in\mathscr{D}$},

for
k\in[\![{1,2q}]\!]
, implies the improved estimate

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(k)}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w))\big)\Big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}\Phi_{k}(v),\quad 1\leqslant k\leqslant 2q.

For
v\in\mathscr{D}\cap\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
then
\dist(v,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant{\mathfrak{r}}
,
Proposition

6.7
gives that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\right|=\left|\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-z)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{z})^{k_{2}}}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{n},

so that
\big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-b)\big|\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v)
for
z=v,\overline{v}
or
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
.
In particular, if we take
\eta_{0}={\mathfrak{r}}
, then (
7.20
) holds for
v\in\mathscr{D}
with
\dist\big(v,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta_{0}
. We define the sequence

\displaystyle\eta_{i+1}=\eta_{i}-n^{-3q},\quad i\geqslant 0.

We show by induction on
i
that the claim (
6.14
) holds for
v\in\mathscr{D}\cap\{w:\dist(w,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}])\geqslant\eta_{i}\}
, whenever
\eta_{i}\gg 1/n
(meaning
i=\mathcal{O}(n^{3q})
). Thanks to (
7.21
) and the trivial bound (
7.12
), we have for any
z,v\in\mathscr{D}
, and
1\leqslant k\leqslant 2q
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{v}{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-b)\big|\lesssim n^{k+1}\leqslant n^{2q+1}.

In particular
{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-b)
is Lipschitz in
v
with Lipschitz constant at most
n^{2q+1}
. Therefore if (
7.20
) holds for
v\in\mathscr{D}
with
\dist\big(v,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta_{i}
, then (
7.19
) holds for
v\in\mathscr{D}
with
\dist\big(v,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta_{i+1}
. And
Proposition

7.1
implies that (
7.20
) also holds for
v\in\mathscr{D}
with
\dist\big(v,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\geqslant\eta_{i+1}
. In this way, by repeatedly using
Proposition

7.1
, we conclude that
\big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-b)\big|\lesssim\Phi_{k}(v)
holds for any
v\in\mathscr{D}
and
z\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
or
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
.
∎

7.3.
Proof of Statement (
6.15
)

We next proceed to prove the second statement of
Proposition

6.4
, given by (
6.15
). To that end, we take
R=4p
,
s_{1}=s_{2}=\cdots=s_{2p-1}=1
,
s_{2p}=s_{2p+1}=\cdots=s_{4p-2}=-1
, and
s_{4p-1}=-1,v_{4p-1}=-v
.
For any
0\leqslant r\leqslant 2p-1
, the derivative
\partial_{z}\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
after specializing at
z=v,v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
, gives the
(r+1)
st joint cumulants of

(7.46)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{v-x_{i}},\quad\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\overline{v}-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\overline{v}-x_{i}}.

For any subset
J\subseteq[\![{1,2p-1}]\!]
, we denote

\partial_{V_{J}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}=(\prod_{j\in J}\partial_{v_{j}})\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
.
In order to establish (
6.15
), it suffices to show that for any subset
2
2

2

Here,
J
does not contain repeated elements (for otherwise the associated cumulant below is equal to
0
).

J\subseteq[\![{1,2p-1}]\!]
with
0\leqslant|J|=r\leqslant 2p-1
we have

(7.47)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\partial_{V_{J}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)|_{z=v,v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\right|\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]|}{n^{r}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{2r}}.

Indeed, assuming (
7.47
), we deduce that the
(r+1)
-th joint cumulants of (
7.46
) are bounded by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{n^{r}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{2r}}.

Then, the left side of (
6.15
) is a combination of the first
(2p)
-th cumulants of (
7.46
)

(7.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}&\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}\right]\right|^{2p}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\sum(r_{u}+1)=2p}\prod_{u}\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]\big|}{n^{r_{u}}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2r_{u}}}\\
&\lesssim\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2}}\right)^{p}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n^{2p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{4p-2}},\end{split}

where the sum is over positive integer sequences
(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{k})
such that
\sum_{u=1}^{k}(r_{u}+1)=2p
. To get the last line, we used the fact that the largest term in the sum is either obtained when
k=p
,
r_{1}=r_{2}=\cdots=r_{p}=1
or when
k=1
,
r_{1}=2p-1
.

Set
R=4p
, and then define the parameter
q>1
as in (
7.15
). Further denote the control parameters
by setting

(7.49)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Phi_{k,0}(z)=\Phi_{k}(z),\qquad&\text{for $k\geqslant 0$};\\
\Phi_{k,\ell}(z)=\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]\big|}{n^{\ell}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{k+2\ell-1}}+\frac{1}{\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{q}}\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{q-k-\ell},\qquad&\text{for $\ell\geqslant 1$ and $q\leqslant k+\ell$};\\
\Phi_{k,\ell}(z)=\dist\big(z,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)^{-k-\ell},\qquad&\text{for $\ell\geqslant 1$ and $k+\ell>q$}.\end{aligned}

Since
q\geqslant 4R/{\mathfrak{c}}\geqslant 16p/{\mathfrak{c}}
, for
k=1
and
\ell\leqslant 2p-1
, we have

(7.50)

\displaystyle\Phi_{1,\ell}(z)=\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]\big|}{n^{\ell}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2\ell}}+\OO\left(n^{2p}\left(\frac{\log n}{n^{\mathfrak{c}}}\right)^{q}\right)\asymp\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]\big|}{n^{\ell}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2\ell}},

where we used the lower bound
\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]\big|/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\gtrsim 1
from (
6.4
).
Moreover, similarly to (
7.18
), one can check that
\Phi_{k,\ell}(z)
satisfies

(7.51)

\displaystyle\Phi_{k,\ell}(z)\Phi_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}(z)\lesssim\Phi_{k+k^{\prime},\ell+\ell^{\prime}}(z).

The statement (
7.47
) will be deduced as a consequence of the following two propositions, which state that if we have some (weak) a priori estimates on
\partial_{V_{J}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
, then the dynamical loop equation can be used to obtain improved estimates on
\partial_{V_{J}}\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(z)
.

Proposition 7.2
.

Take
s_{1}=s_{2}=\cdots=s_{2p-1}=1
,
s_{2p}=s_{2p+1}=\cdots=s_{4p-2}=-1
, and
s_{4p-1}=-1,v_{4p-1}=-v
, for some
v\in\mathbb{C}
. The following two statements hold.

(1)

Suppose
v\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
. Assume for any
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
and
J\subseteq[\![{1,2p-1}]\!]
with
1\leqslant k+|J|\leqslant 2q
that

(7.52)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\Big(\partial_{V_{J}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big)\Big|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log n}{n^{1\vee|J|}}.

Then we have the improved estimate

(7.53)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\Big(\partial_{V_{J}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big)\Big|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\bigg|\leqslant\frac{{\mathfrak{C}}}{n^{1\vee|J|}},

for some constant
\mathfrak{C}=\mathfrak{C}(R)>1
.

(2)

Suppose
v\in\mathscr{D}
. Assume for any
z=v,\overline{v}
or
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})

J\subseteq[\![{1,2p-1}]\!]
with
1\leqslant k+|J|\leqslant 2q
that

(7.54)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\Big(\partial_{V_{J}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big)\Big|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\bigg|\leqslant\log n\cdot\Phi_{k,|J|}(v).

Then we have the improved estimate

(7.55)

\displaystyle\bigg|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\Big(\partial_{V_{J}}(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\Big)\Big|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\bigg|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}\Phi_{k,|J|}(v),

for some constant
{\mathfrak{C}}=\mathfrak{C}(R)>1
.

Let us mention that the first statement of
Proposition

7.2
is nearly a special case of the second statement, except that in the former we allow
\dist(v,I)
to grow with
n
. As such, these two parts of the proposition are very closely related; the only reason why we separated their statements is that the definition of
\Phi_{k,|J|}(v)
involves the function
f(v)
, whose domain is
\Lambda
and is thus not defined for general
v\in\mathscr{D}(\mathfrak{r})
.

Proof of
Proposition

7.2
.

Here, we only establish the second statement of
Proposition

7.2
. The proof of the first statement is entirely analogous, by replacing every appearance of
\Phi_{k,|J|}(v)
below with the quantity
1/n^{1\vee|J|}
.

In the following we prove (
7.55
) for
z=v
; the proofs in the other cases that
z=\overline{v}
or
z\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
follow from essentially the same argument. To simplify notation, we take
|J|=r\leqslant 2q-1
and
J=\{1,2,\cdots,r\}
; we also denote
\eta=\dist\big(v,[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)
.
For
q\leqslant k+r\leqslant 2q
, using (
7.21
), the trivial bound (
7.12
) implies

(7.56)

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{D}}^{(k)}_{z}\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\big|_{z=v,v_{i}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta^{k+r}}=\Phi_{k,r}(v).

It therefore suffices to consider the case
1\leqslant k+r\leqslant q
, in which case the proof will be similar to that of
Proposition

7.1
. Specifically, by taking derivative with respect to
v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{r}
on both sides of (
7.4
), then specializing
z=v
and
v_{i}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
, we get

(7.57)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\frac{\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r}}\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big){\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}}.

By expanding
\log\big(1+{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)
as in (
7.23
), we must estimate

(7.58)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}&\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r}}\left(\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)^{j_{1}}\left(\frac{\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}}\frac{1}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}}\right){\rm d}w\\
&=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\omega}\sum\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\prod_{u=1}^{j_{2}}\partial_{V_{J^{\prime}_{u}}}\big(\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1\big)\frac{1}{\big(f(w)+1\big)^{j_{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}}},\end{split}

where the sum is over all index sets
J_{1},J_{2},\ldots,J_{j_{1}},J_{1}^{\prime},J_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,J_{j_{2}}^{\prime}
such that
\bigcup_{u=1}^{j_{1}}J_{u}\cup\bigcup_{u=1}^{j_{2}}J^{\prime}_{u}=[\![{1,r}]\!]
, and
j_{1}+j_{2}\leqslant q
.
Thanks to the trivial bound (
7.12
), the contribution from the error term in (
7.23
) is at most
\big|{\mathcal{E}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big|^{q+1}\eta^{-k-r}=\OO\big((n\eta)^{-q-1}\eta^{-k-r}\big)
, which is smaller than
\Phi_{k,r}(v)
for
\eta\geqslant n^{\mathfrak{c}-1}
and
q\geqslant 4R/\mathfrak{c}
.
We recall the expression of
\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)
from (
6.20
),

\displaystyle\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1=\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{4p-1}\bigg(1-\displaystyle\frac{1}{n(v_{j}-w)}\bigg)^{-s_{j}}-1.

If
|w-v_{j}|\gtrsim\eta
, then
\big|1/n(v_{j}-w)\big|\lesssim 1/n\eta\ll 1
, so
\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1
can be Taylor expanded as a sum of terms in the form

(7.59)

\displaystyle\prod_{j\in L}\frac{1}{n(w-v_{j})},

where
L
is a multi-set of
[\![{1,4p-1}]\!]
, with
1\leqslant|L|\leqslant q
, plus an error
\OO((n\eta)^{-(q+1)})
. Again, thanks to the trivial bound (
7.12
), the contribution from such error term in (
7.58
) is
\OO((n\eta)^{-(q+1)}\eta^{-(k+r)})
, which is smaller than
\Phi_{k,r}(v)
. The derivative of (
7.59
) with respect to
V_{J^{\prime}_{u}}
is nonzero only if
J^{\prime}_{u}\subseteq L
. If this is the case, then

(7.60)

\displaystyle\partial_{V_{J^{\prime}_{u}}}\prod_{j\in L}\frac{1}{n(w-v_{j})}=\prod_{j\in J^{\prime}_{u}}\frac{\text{multiplicity of $j$ in $L$}}{n(w-v_{j})^{2}}\prod_{j\in L\setminus J^{\prime}_{u}}\frac{1}{n(w-v_{j})},

where
L\setminus J^{\prime}_{u}
is the multi-set obtained from
L
by removing a copy of each element in
J_{u}^{\prime}
.

After specializing
v_{j}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
, the right side of (
7.60
) is in the form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{\ell_{1u}+\ell_{2u}-|J_{u}^{\prime}|}(w-v)^{\ell_{1u}}(w-\overline{v})^{\ell_{2u}}},\quad 2|J^{\prime}_{u}|\leqslant\ell_{1u}+\ell_{2u}\leqslant q+|J^{\prime}_{u}|.

Therefore after specializing
v_{j}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}
, the leading order term of

\prod_{u=1}^{j_{2}}\partial_{V_{J^{\prime}_{u}}}\big(\psi^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-1\big)
is a linear combination (with bounded coefficients) of terms of the form

(7.61)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}(w-v)^{\ell_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{\ell_{2}}},\quad 2\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|\leqslant\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leqslant j_{2}q+\sum_{u=1}^{j_{2}}|J_{u}^{\prime}|,

where
\ell_{1}=\sum_{u}\ell_{1u}
and
\ell_{2}=\sum_{u}\ell_{2u}
.
By plugging (
7.61
) into (
7.58
), we get

(7.62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}&\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}\oint_{\omega}\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{1}{\big(f(w)+1\big)^{j_{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}+\ell_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}+\ell_{2}}}\\
&=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}\oint_{\omega_{v,\overline{v}}}\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{1}{\big(f(w)+1\big)^{j_{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}+\ell_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}+\ell_{2}}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}\oint_{\omega+}\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{1}{\big(f(w)+1\big)^{j_{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}+\ell_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}+\ell_{2}}},\end{split}

where the contour
\omega_{v,\overline{v}}
encloses
v,\overline{v}
, and the contour
\omega+\subseteq\mathscr{D}(2{\mathfrak{r}})
encloses
[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]
and
v,\overline{v}
. See Figure
13
.
Thanks to (
7.11
), the first term on the right side of (
7.62
) decomposes to sum of terms of the form

(7.63)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}_{1})}\left(\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w))\right){\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}_{2})}\left(\frac{1}{f(w)+1}\right)^{j_{2}},

where
\hat{k}_{1}+\hat{k}_{2}=k_{1}+k_{2}+\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-1=k+\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}
.

In the following we show that under our hypothesis (
7.54
), for
0\leqslant\hat{k}+|\hat{J}|\leqslant 2q
,

(7.64)

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k})}(\partial_{V_{\hat{J}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k},|\hat{J}|}(v),\quad\hat{J}\subseteq[\![{1,r}]\!].

Together with (
7.11
) and (
7.51
), (
7.64
) would imply for
j_{1}\geqslant 1
that

(7.65)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}_{1})}\left(\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\right)\lesssim\sum\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\tilde{k}_{u})}\left(\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}\right)\right|\lesssim\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\Phi_{\tilde{k}_{u},|J_{u}|}(v)\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k},\sum_{u}|J_{u}|}(v),

where the sum is over index sets
(\tilde{k}_{1},\tilde{k}_{2},\ldots,\tilde{k}_{j_{1}})
such that
\sum_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\tilde{k}_{u}=\hat{k}
.

If
\hat{J}=\emptyset
, (
7.64
) is (
7.26
). In the following we assume that
|\hat{J}|\geqslant 1
. For
q\leqslant\hat{k}+|\hat{J}|\leqslant 2q
, (
7.64
) follows from the trivial bound

\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k})}(\partial_{V_{\hat{J}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})})\right|\lesssim 1/\eta^{\hat{k}+|\hat{J}|}=\Phi_{\hat{k},|\hat{J}|}
.
For
\hat{k}+|\hat{J}|\leqslant q
, we recall the expression of
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}
from (
7.9
), and we need to estimate

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k})}\left(\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}\frac{\hat{g}(w)}{\hat{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}\partial_{V_{\hat{J}}}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right),

which, thanks to (
7.11
), is a linear combination of

(7.66)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime})}\left(\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}\frac{\hat{g}(w)}{\hat{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}\right){\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime})}\partial_{V_{\hat{J}}}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right).

By essentially the same argument as for (
7.39
), we have

(7.67)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime})}\partial_{V_{\hat{J}}}\left(\log\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right)^{j}\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime},|\hat{J}|}(v),\quad\hat{k}^{\prime}\geqslant 0.

Using (
7.67
) as input, we get the following bound for the second term on the right side of (
7.66
),

(7.68)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}&\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime})}\partial_{V_{\hat{J}}}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right|\\
&\qquad=\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}^{\prime})}\left(\frac{1}{j!}\sum_{j=1}^{q-\hat{k}^{\prime}}\partial_{V_{\hat{J}}}\left(\log\left(\frac{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w+1/n)e^{-b(w+1/n)}}{{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)e^{-b(w)}}\right)\right)^{j}+\OO\left(\frac{1}{(n\eta)^{q-\hat{k}^{\prime}+1}\eta^{|\hat{J}|}}\right)\right)\right|\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime},|\hat{J}|}(v),\end{split}

for any
\hat{k}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
.

By plugging (
7.68
) into (
7.66
), if
\hat{k}^{\prime}=\hat{k}
, we have

|\eqref{e:linearl}|\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k},|\hat{J}|}(v)
.
For
k^{\prime}\leqslant\hat{k}-1
, using (
7.8
) and (
7.13
), we can bound the first term in (
7.29
) as

(7.69)

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{(\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime})}\left(\frac{f(w+1/n)}{f(w+1/n)+1}\frac{\hat{g}(w)}{\hat{g}(w+1/n)}e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(w)}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime}-1}}.

By plugging (
7.68
) and (
7.69
) into (
7.66
), the sum of terms with
\hat{k}^{\prime}\leqslant\hat{k}-1
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\hat{k}^{\prime}=0}^{\hat{k}-1}\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}-\hat{k}^{\prime}-1}}\Phi_{\hat{k}^{\prime},|\hat{J}|}(v)\lesssim\Phi_{\hat{k},|\hat{J}|}(v),

where we have used the last inequality in (
7.13
). This finishes the proof of (
7.64
).

Now we can use (
7.14
) and (
7.65
) to upper bound (
7.63
). There are two cases, namely, either
\hat{k}_{2}=0
or
\hat{k}_{2}\geqslant 1
:

(7.70)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\eqref{e:tt1copy2}|&\lesssim\frac{\Phi_{\hat{k}_{1},\sum_{u}|J_{u}|}(v)}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J_{u}^{\prime}|}}\left(\bm{1}(\hat{k}_{2}=0)+\frac{\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(v)/(f(v)+1)]\big|\bm{1}(\hat{k}_{2}\geqslant 1)}{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[v]\dist(v,I)^{\hat{k}_{2}-1}}\right)\\
&\lesssim\frac{\Phi_{\hat{k}_{1}+\hat{k}_{2},\sum_{u}|J_{u}|}(v)}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J_{u}^{\prime}|}}=\frac{\Phi_{k+\ell_{1}+\ell_{2},\sum_{u}|J_{u}|}(v)}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J_{u}^{\prime}|}}\leqslant\frac{\Phi_{k+2\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|,\sum_{u}|J_{u}|}(v)}{n^{\sum_{u}|J_{u}^{\prime}|}}\lesssim\Phi_{k,r}(v),\end{split}

where for the second to last inequality we used that
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\geqslant 2\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|
from (
7.61
), and
\Phi_{k,\ell}(v)/n^{k}
is deceasing in
k
: for the last inequality, we used
\sum_{u}|J_{u}^{\prime}|+\sum_{u}|J_{u}|=r
. For the second term in (
7.62
), since
w\in\omega+
is bounded away from
v
, we have

(7.71)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}\oint_{\omega+}\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)\frac{1}{\big(f(w)+1\big)^{j_{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{(w-v)^{k_{1}+\ell_{1}}(w-\overline{v})^{k_{2}+\ell_{2}}}\right|\\
&\qquad\lesssim\max_{w\in\omega+}\bigg|\frac{\prod_{u=1}^{j_{1}}\partial_{V_{J_{u}}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{0}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)\big)}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\Phi_{0,\sum_{u}|J_{u}|}(v)}{n^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}\lesssim\frac{\Phi_{0,\sum_{u}|J_{u}|}(v)}{n^{\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|}}\lesssim\Phi_{k,r}(v),\end{split}

where we used (
7.64
) and
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\geqslant 2\sum_{u}|J^{\prime}_{u}|
from (
7.61
) in the second line.
We conclude from plugging (
7.70
) and (
7.71
) into (
7.62
)

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\partial_{v_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}\cdots\partial_{v_{r}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-b(w)\big)\big|_{z=v,v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\bar{v}\}}\Big|=|\eqref{e:toest3}|\lesssim\Phi_{k,r}(v).

This finishes the proof of
Proposition

7.1
.
∎

Now we can establish the second statement of
Proposition

6.4
, given by (
6.15
).

Proof of (
6.15
).

We recall from (
7.47
) and (
7.48
) that (
6.15
) follows from the estimates of the derivatives of
\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-b
:

\big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(1)}\partial_{V_{J}}\log({\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}-b(w))|_{z=v,v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\bar{v}\}}\big|\lesssim\Phi_{1,{|J|}}(v)
, for any
J\subseteq[\![{1,2p-1}]\!]
, where
\Phi_{1,|J|}(v)
is explicitly given in (
7.50
).
Thanks to Propositions
7.2
, if we have some weak estimates for
\big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\partial_{V_{J}}(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w))|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\big|
, then it implies better estimates.
Next we show that (
7.52
) holds for
\dist\big(\{z,v\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\asymp n
. In fact, for
J=\emptyset
, (
7.52
) follows from
Proposition

6.7
. For
|J|\geqslant 1
, thanks to (
7.21
), and the trivial bound (
7.12
) (with
\eta\asymp n
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\partial_{V_{J}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\big|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\bar{v}\}}\Big|\lesssim\eta^{-k-|J|}\lesssim\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{1\vee|J|}}.

Thus, (
7.52
) holds for
\dist\big(\{z,v\},[{\mathfrak{a}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{b}}^{\prime}]\big)\asymp n
.

Then a continuity argument quickly implies that

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\partial_{V_{J}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\big|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\Big|\lesssim\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{1\vee|J|}},\quad\text{holds for any $z,v\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})$};

we omit further details, since they are very similar to the one implemented in the proof of (
6.14
) (by repeatedly using the first statement of
Proposition

7.2
). In particular, for any
v\in\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})
, the assumption (
7.54
) in
Proposition

7.2
holds. Then, by a continuity argument that is again very similar to the one used in the proof of (
6.14
) (by repeated use of the second statement of
Proposition

7.2
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{D}}_{z}^{(k)}\partial_{V_{J}}\big(\log{\mathcal{A}}^{({\bm{v}},\bm{s})}(w)-b(w)\big)\big|_{v_{j}=v_{j+2p-1}\in\{v,\overline{v}\}}\Big|\lesssim\Phi_{k,|J|}(v),\quad\text{holds for any $v\in\mathscr{D}$ and $z\in\{v,\bar{v}\}\cup\mathscr{D}({\mathfrak{r}})$}.

This finishes the proof of (
6.15
).
∎

7.4.
Proof of
Proposition

5.5

Finally, in Section
7.4
, we prove
Proposition

5.5
using
Proposition

6.4
as input.

Proof of
Proposition

5.5
.

We recall the relation between
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
from (
6.12
), and
b(z)
from (
6.10
). Then

\displaystyle\partial_{z}b(z)=\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\displaystyle\oint_{\omega}\displaystyle\frac{\log\mathcal{B}(w)dw}{(w-z)^{2}},

where the contour
\omega\subseteq\Lambda
contains
[\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}]
, but not
z
. The transition probability (
4.9
) is a special case of
\mathbb{P}
, and so
Proposition

5.5
follows if we show

(7.72)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}\right]=\partial_{z}b(z)+\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)}\right).\end{split}

and

(7.73)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}\right]\right|^{2p}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2}}\right)^{p}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n^{2p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{4p-2}}.\end{split}

We notice the trivial bound for the exponent involved in
\mathbb{P}
, given by

(7.74)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant m}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})+\OO(1/n)\right|\lesssim 1.

Then, by (
6.14
) and (
6.12
), (
7.72
) is equivalent to

(7.75)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}&\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\partial_{z}b(z)\bigg)\\
&\quad\times\bigg(e^{\sum_{i,j}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})+\OO(1/n)}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[e^{\sum_{i,j}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})+\mathcal{O}(1/n)}\right]\bigg)\Bigg]=\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)}\right).\end{split}

We will prove (
7.75
) by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. In the following, we estimate the variances of the first and second term in (
7.75
).

We notice that
\big|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]/(n\Imaginary z)\big|\ll 1
for
z\in\mathscr{D}
. We can replace the inner expectation in (
6.15
) by
\partial_{z}b(z)
, and the error is negligible:

(7.76)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\partial_{z}b(z)\right|^{2p}\\
&\qquad\qquad\lesssim\left|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}\right]-\partial_{z}b(z)\right|^{2p}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}\right]\right|^{2p}\\
&\qquad\qquad\lesssim\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2}}\right)^{p}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n^{2p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{4p-2}}.\end{split}

In particular, by taking
p=1
, we have

(7.77)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\partial_{z}b(z)\right|^{2}\lesssim\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2}}.\end{split}

For the second term in (
7.75
), we recall that
\kappa(z,w)
is analytic in a neighborhood of
[{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{b}}]
. The Laplace transform of
\sum_{ij}e_{i}e_{j}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})/n^{2}
can be computed as an integral of its expectation under deformation of
\mathbb{Q}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\exp\Bigg(t\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq m}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})\Bigg)\right]=1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\sum_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})\exp\Bigg(\theta\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq m}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})\Bigg)\right]{\rm d}\theta.

This can be analyzed the same way as in
[
Hua21
, Section 8.2]
, using the loop equation of the deformed measure, and it gives

(7.78)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left|\exp\bigg(\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq m}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})\bigg)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\Bigg[\exp\bigg(\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq m}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})\bigg)\Bigg]\right|^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}.

Then using (
7.74
), we conclude from (
7.78
) that

(7.79)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left|\exp\bigg(\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq m}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})+\OO(1/n)\bigg)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\Bigg[\exp\bigg(\sum_{ij}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j})\bigg)\Bigg]\right|^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}.

Using (
7.77
) and (
7.79
), (
7.75
) follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle|\eqref{e:smallg}|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}=\OO\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)}\right).

This finishes the proof of (
7.72
).

To prove (
7.73
), similarly to in (
7.76
), using (
7.72
) as input, we can replace the inner expectation in (
7.73
) by
\partial_{z}b(z)
. In this way, (
7.73
) is equivalent to

(7.80)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{z-x_{i}-e_{i}/n}-\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}-\partial_{z}b(z)\right|^{2p}\lesssim\left(\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{2}}\right)^{p}+\frac{|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f(z)/(f(z)+1)]|}{n^{2p-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z]\dist(z,I)^{4p-2}}.

Thanks to the relation (
6.12
) between
\mathbb{P}
and
\mathbb{Q}
, and the trivial bound (
7.74
), we conclude (
7.80
) from (
7.76
) and (
7.79
).
∎

Appendix A
Thin Slice of Polygonal Domains

In this section, we check that thin slices of polygonal domains with their (tilted) limiting continuum height functions satisfy Assumption
2.4
.
Throughout this section, we recall the notation from
Section

2.2
.
We will focus on polygonal domains
\mathfrak{P}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
, given as follows.

Definition A.1
.

A subset
\mathfrak{P}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is
polygonal
if it is simply-connected and its boundary
\partial\mathfrak{P}
consists of a finite union of line segments, each of which is parallel to an axis of
\mathbb{T}
. The domain
3
3

3

We assume throughout that all vertices of
n\mathfrak{P}
are in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.

\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{P}_{n}=n\mathfrak{P}\subset\mathbb{T}
, is then tileable and is therefore associated with a (unique, up to global shift) boundary height function
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{h}_{n}
. By translating
\mathfrak{P}
if necessary, we will assume that
\mathfrak{P}\subset\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geqslant 0}
and that
(0,0)\in\partial\mathfrak{P}
. Then by shifting
\mathsf{h}
if necessary, we will further suppose that
\mathsf{h}(0,0)=0
. Under this notation, we set
h:\partial\mathfrak{P}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by
h(u)=n^{-1}\mathsf{h}(nu)
for each
u\in\partial\mathfrak{P}
. Moreover, we abbreviate
\mathrm{Adm}(\mathfrak{P})=\mathrm{Adm}(\mathfrak{P};h)
;
\mathfrak{L}(\mathfrak{P})=\mathfrak{L}(\mathfrak{P};h)
; and
\mathfrak{A}(\mathfrak{P})=\mathfrak{A}(\mathfrak{P};h)
, they do not depend on the above choice of global shift fixing
h
.

The following result from
[
KO07
,
ADPZ20
]
describes properties of the limit shape
H^{*}
and complex slope
f_{t}(x)
for polygonal domains
{\mathfrak{P}}
as in
Definition

A.1
.

Proposition A.2

(
[
KO07
,
ADPZ20
]
)
.

Adopt the notation of
Definition

A.1
, and assume that the domain
\mathfrak{R}=\mathfrak{P}
is polygonal with at least
6
sides. Then following statements hold.

(1)

For any
(x,t)\in\mathfrak{P}\setminus\mathfrak{L}(\mathfrak{P})
, we have
\nabla H^{*}(x,t)\in\big\{(0,0),(1,0),(1,-1)\big\}
.

(2)

The arctic boundary
\mathfrak{A}=\mathfrak{A}(\mathfrak{P})
is an algebraic curve, and its singularities are all either ordinary cusps or tacnodes.

(3)

Fix
(x_{0},t_{0})\in\overline{\mathfrak{L}}
. There exists a neighborhood
\mathfrak{U}\subset\mathbb{C}^{2}
of
(x_{0},t_{0})
and a real analytic function
Q_{0}:\mathfrak{U}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that, for any
(x,t)\in\mathfrak{U}\cap\overline{\mathfrak{L}}
, we have

(A.1)

\displaystyle Q_{0}\big(f_{t}(x)\big)=x\big(f_{t}(x)+1\big)-tf_{t}(x).

There exists a nonzero rational function
Q
such that, for any
(x,t)\in\overline{\mathfrak{L}}
, we have

(A.2)

\displaystyle Q\bigg(f_{t}(x),x-\displaystyle\frac{tf_{t}(x)}{f_{t}(x)+1}\bigg)=0.

(4)

For any
(x,t)\in\overline{\mathfrak{L}}
,
(x,t)\in\mathfrak{A}({\mathfrak{P}})
if and only if
f_{t}(x)
is a double root of (
A.1
).

Proof.

Proposition

A.2
follows essentially from results in
[
KO07
,
ADPZ20
]
. Its first statement is
[
ADPZ20
, Theorem 1.10]
, and the second statement follows from
[
ADPZ20
, Theorem 1.2, Theorem 1.10]
. It follows from
[
ADPZ20
, Theorem 1.5]
that the complex slope
f_{t}(x)
extends continuously to a real number on the arctic boundary.

For any connected component
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{P}})
of the liquid region, with arctic boundary
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
,
we consider the following map from the closure of the liquid region
\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}\cup{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
to
\mathbb{CP}^{2}

(A.3)

\displaystyle(x,t)\in\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}\mapsto(f(x,t),z(x,t)):=\left(f_{t}(x),x-t\frac{f_{t}(x)}{f_{t}(x)+1}\right)\in\mathbb{CP}^{2}.

For
(x,t)\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
, the claim (
A.1
) follows from
[
Gor21
, Theorem 10.5]
. Assuming
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
is simply connected (we will show it in
Lemma

A.3
),
[
ADPZ20
, Theorem 5.1]
gives a decomposition of the map
(x,t)\mapsto f(x,t)
. It implies that for any
(x_{0},t_{0})\in{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
, there exists a small neighborhood
{\mathfrak{U}}
of it, such that the map
(x,t)\mapsto f(x,t)
is injective for
(x,t)\in{\mathfrak{U}}\cap\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\prime}
. Thus the same argument as above, there exists a continuous map
Q_{0}
such that
z(x,t)=Q_{0}(f(x,t))/(f(x,t)+1)
for
(x,t)\in{\mathfrak{U}}\cap\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}
, where
{\mathfrak{U}}
is a small neighborhood of
(x_{0},t_{0})\in{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
, and
Q_{0}
is analytic on
f({\mathfrak{U}}\cap{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime})
. By Schwarz reflection principle, we can extends
Q_{0}
to a real analytic function in a neighborhood of
f(x_{0},t_{0})
. This and the discussion above gives (
A.1
).

Since the image of the arctic boundary
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
under the map (
A.3
) is real, we can glue the image of the map (
A.3
) restricted to the liquid region
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
, and its complex conjugate along the image of the arctic boundary to get an immersed Riemann surface in
\mathbb{CP}^{2}
:

\{(f,z)=(f(x,t),z(x,t))\in\mathbb{CP}^{2}:(x,t)\in\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\prime}\}\cup\{(f,z)=(\overline{f(x,t)},\overline{z(x,t)})\in\mathbb{CP}^{2}:(x,t)\in\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}}^{\prime}\}
. Since
Q_{0}
is real analytic, the local charts around any point
(f(x_{0},t_{0}),z(x_{0},t_{0}))
for
(x_{0},t_{0})\in{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
on the arctic boundary are given by
z=Q_{0}(f)/(f+1)
.

f(x,t)
with its complex conjugate and
z(x,t)
with its complex conjugate are two meromorphic functions on this Riemann surface. By
[
Jos13
, Theorem 5.8.1]
, there exists a rational function
Q^{\prime}
such that
Q^{\prime}(f(x,t),z(x,t))=0
for any
(x,t)\in\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}}
, which gives (
A.2
) by taking product of
Q^{\prime}
corresponding to each connected component of
{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{P}})
.

The fourth statement follows from the facts that
(x,t)\in\mathfrak{A}(\mathfrak{P})
if and only if
f_{t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}
, by (
2.7
), and that any root of (
A.1
) is real if and only if it is a double root, as
Q_{0}
is real anaytic (see also the discussion at the end of
[
KO07
, Section 1.6]
).

∎

Lemma A.3
.

Adopting the notation of
Proposition

A.2
, any connected component
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathfrak{L}(\mathfrak{P})
is simply connected.

Proof.

Assume to the contrary that
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
is not simply connected. Then there exists a closed curve
\gamma\subseteq{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
, which is not homotopy equivalent to a point in
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
. It follows that the curve
\gamma
encloses a frozen region
{\mathfrak{F}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{P}}\setminus\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{P}})}
. From
Definition

A.1
, the polygonal domain
{\mathfrak{P}}
is simply connected, so the curve
\gamma
separates the frozen region
{\mathfrak{F}}
from the boundary
\partial{\mathfrak{P}}\subset\overline{{\mathfrak{P}}}
; see Figure
14
. We denote the boundary of
{\mathfrak{F}}
by
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}=\partial\mathfrak{F}\subseteq{\mathfrak{A}}({\mathfrak{P}})
.

\partial{\mathfrak{P}}
{\mathfrak{F}}
\gamma
{\mathfrak{F}}
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{P}})
\xi
u
v

Figure 14.
Shown to the left is the frozen region
{\mathfrak{F}}
, which is separated from the boundary
\partial{\mathfrak{P}}
by a curve
\gamma\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
. Shown to the right are two points
u,v
on the boundary
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathfrak{F}}
such that
H^{*}(v)>H^{*}(u)
.

By the first part of
Proposition

A.2
, for almost any
(x,t)\in\mathfrak{F}
we have
\nabla H^{*}\in\big\{(0,0),(1,0),(1,-1)\big\}
. We consider two cases for the behavior of
\nabla H^{*}
inside
{\mathfrak{F}}
; either
\nabla H^{*}|_{\mathfrak{F}}
is continuous or discontinuous. If
\nabla H^{*}
is discontinuous at some point
w\in{\mathfrak{F}}
, then
[
DSS10
, Theorem 1.3]
implies that there exists a segment connecting
w
and
\partial{\mathfrak{P}}
staying outside the liquid region, which contradicts the fact that
\gamma\in{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
separates
\mathfrak{F}
from
\partial{\mathfrak{P}}
. In the second case,
\nabla H^{*}
is continuous inside
{\mathfrak{F}}
; thus, it takes a constant value in
\big\{(0,0),(1,0),(1,-1)\big\}
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\nabla H^{*}\equiv(0,0)
on
{\mathfrak{F}}
, so
H^{*}|_{\mathfrak{F}}
is constant. By continuity,
H^{*}
is the same constant on the boundary
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathfrak{F}}
.

By the second part, the arctic boundary
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
is an algebraic curve with finitely many singularities, which are all either first order cusps or tacnodes; by
[
ADPZ20
, Theorem 1.3(c)]
, at every nonsingular point
\xi\in{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
, the boundary
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
is locally strictly convex; and, by
[
ADPZ20
, Theorem 1.5, Theorem 1.10]
, the unit tangent vector
\tau(\xi)
to
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
at any
\xi\in\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
depends continuously on
\xi
. Therefore, when
\xi
moves along
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
, the tangent vector angle winds around the unit circle
\mathbb{S}^{1}
at least once. Hence, there exists a nonsingular point
\xi\in{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
such that the unit tangent vector
\tau(\xi)
has positive
x
-coordinate and negative
y
-coordinate.

Shift the tangent line at
\xi
in the normal direction to
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
, slightly into the liquid region, such that it intersects
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
at two points,
u
and
v
. By the
local strict convexity of
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
at
\xi
, the segment from
u
to
v
(which is parallel to
\tau(\xi)
) remains strictly inside the liquid region
\mathfrak{L}(\mathfrak{P})
; see Figure
14
. In the liquid region,
\nabla H^{*}
stays inside strictly the triangle
\mathcal{T}
from (
2.1
). Since
v-u
is parallel to
\tau(\xi)
, which has positive
x
-coordinate and negative
y
-coordinate, it follows that
\nabla H^{*}(z)\cdot\tau(\xi)>0
for any
z\in\mathfrak{L}(\mathfrak{P})
. Thus,

(A.4)

\displaystyle H^{*}(v)-H^{*}(u)=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla H^{*}\big(\theta v+(1-\theta)u\big)\cdot(v-u)\rm d\theta>0,

which contradicts the fact that
H^{*}
is constant on
{\mathfrak{A}}^{\prime}
. Hence,
{\mathfrak{L}}^{\prime}
is simply connected.
∎

Assumption A.4
.

Under the notation of
Definition

A.1
, assume the following four properties hold.

(1)

The arctic boundary
\mathfrak{A}=\mathfrak{A}(\mathfrak{P})
has no tacnode singularities.

(2)

No cusp singularity of
\mathfrak{A}
is also a tangency location of
\mathfrak{A}(\mathfrak{P})
.

(3)

There exists an axis
\ell
of
\mathbb{T}
such that any line connecting two distinct cusp singularities of
\mathfrak{A}
is not parallel to
\ell
.

(4)

Any intersection point between
\mathfrak{A}
and
\partial\mathfrak{P}
must be a tangency location of
\mathfrak{A}
.

It has been proven in
[
AH21
, Lemma 6.2]
,
under
Assumption

A.4
, for any
u\in\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{P}})}
, there exists a trapezoid
{\mathfrak{D}}(u)\subseteq{\mathfrak{P}}\cap({\mathbb{R}}\times[{\mathfrak{t}}_{1},{\mathfrak{t}}_{2}])
in the form (
2.10
), containing
u
(flip
{\mathfrak{P}}
if necessary), with liquid region
{\mathfrak{L}}
, and complex slope
f:{\mathfrak{L}}\mapsto\mathbb{H}^{-}
satisfying
2.8
. For all
{\mathfrak{t}}_{1}\leqslant t\leqslant{\mathfrak{t}}_{2}
, we define slices of the liquid region (along the horizontal line
y=t
) by
I_{t}=\big\{x:(x,t)\in\overline{\mathfrak{L}}\big\}
.
Then the following constraints hold:

(1)

The boundary height function
h
is constant along both
\partial_{\ea}(\mathfrak{D})
and
\partial_{\we}(\mathfrak{D})
.

(2)

Either
\partial_{\north}(\mathfrak{D})
is packed with respect to
h
or there exists
\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}>\mathfrak{t}_{2}-{\mathfrak{t}}_{1}
such that
H^{*}
admits an extension to time
{\mathfrak{t}}_{1}+\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
.

(3)

There exists
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}\in[\mathfrak{t}_{1},\mathfrak{t}_{2}]
such that the following holds. For
t\in[\mathfrak{t}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}]
, the set
I_{t}
consists of one nonempty interval, and for
t\in(\tilde{\mathfrak{t}},\mathfrak{t}_{2}]
, the set
I_{t}
consists of two nonempty disjoint intervals.
4
4

4

Observe if
\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}=\mathfrak{t}_{2}
, then
I_{t}
always only consists of one nonempty interval.

(4)

Any tangency location along
\partial\overline{\mathfrak{L}}
is of the form
\min I_{t}
or
\max I_{t}
, for some
t\in(\mathfrak{t}_{1},\mathfrak{t}_{2})
. At most one tangency location is of the form
\min I_{t}
, and at most one is of the form

. There tangency locations are on the west and east boundary of
{\mathfrak{D}}(u)
.

(5)

There exists an algebraic curve
Q
such that, for any

, we have

\displaystyle Q\bigg(f_{t}(x),x-\displaystyle\frac{tf_{t}(x)}{f_{t}(x)+1}\bigg)=0.

Furthermore, the curve

“approximately comes from a polygonal domain” in the following sense. There exists a polygonal domain

satisfying

with liquid region

\displaystyle Q(u,v)=\displaystyle\frac{u+1}{\alpha^{-1}u+1}Q_{\mathfrak{L}_{0}}(\alpha^{-1}u,v).

We remark that in (

With a slight abuse of notation, we denote the shifted version of

.

Theorem A.6
.

Fix an integer
d\geqslant 2
. Let
X\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
denote a compact subset, whose boundary
\partial X
is an irreducible separating set of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
(A separating set
\Omega
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
is said to be an irreducible separating
set of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
provided no proper closed subset of
\Omega
separates
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
). Let
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a continuous mapping that is locally one-to-one on
X\setminus Z
, where
Z\subset X
is such that
Z\cap X^{\circ}
(
X^{\circ}
is the interior of
X
) is a discrete set and
\partial X\setminus Z
is not empty. If
f|_{\partial X}
is one-to-one, then
f
is a homeomorphism of
X
onto
f(X)
.

The map
\pi
in (
A.7
) satisfies the assumptions of
Theorem

A.6
with
Z
the critical point of
\pi
. It is a bijection, if it is one-to-one on the boundary. The boundary of the liquid region
{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime})\cap{\mathfrak{D}}^{t}
consists of three parts: the north boundary, the south boundary, and the arctic curve boundary

(A.8)

\displaystyle\big\{(x,{\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime})\in\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime})}\big\}\cup\big\{(x,t)\in\overline{{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime})}\big\}\cup\overline{\big\{(x,r)\in\partial{{\mathfrak{L}}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime})}:r\in(t,{\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime})\big\}}.
