as in (
C.30
)

(C.31)

\displaystyle G(z,z^{\prime};0)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}\frac{M}{2}f^{0}(ru)f^{0}(ru^{\prime}),

for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant 3
.

To use the deformation formula Proposition
C.1
, we need that the righthand side of (
C.4
) is analytic in a neighborhood of
\mathscr{C}_{\theta}=\{\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)\cup\overline{\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)}:x\in I_{{\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}}^{*}\}
. Later we will see that
\mathscr{C}_{\theta}
will stay close to the contour
\mathscr{C}^{0}
. For this we define two more contours
\mathscr{C}^{0+}
and
\mathscr{C}^{0-}
, such that
\mathscr{C}^{0+}
encloses
\mathscr{C}^{0}
, and

\mathscr{C}^{0}
encloses
\mathscr{C}^{0-}
; See Figure
21
. Moreover, we take
\mathscr{C}^{0\pm}
to be in a radius
1/(10r)
neighborhood of
\mathscr{C}^{0}
, and distance
1/(100r)
away from
\mathscr{C}^{0}
, where
r
is from (
C.30
) and (
C.31
). We define
\mathscr{A}^{\pm}
be the closure of the annulus between the contours
\mathscr{C}^{0\pm}
and
\mathscr{C}^{2}
, and will take
\mathscr{A}_{\theta}=\mathscr{A}^{+}
in Proposition
C.1
.

Later, we will show that
G(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
is analytic for
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant 3
, this will guarantee that the righthand side of (
C.4
) is analytic in a radius
\OO(1/r)
neighborhood of
\mathscr{C}_{0}
.
For
z
between the two contours
\mathscr{C}^{0-},\mathscr{C}^{2}
, we will show that

1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;\theta)/(f(z;\theta)+1))
is not
0
.

Thanks to Proposition
B.1
, there exists a large constant
{\mathfrak{C}}
,

(C.32)

\displaystyle 1/{\mathfrak{C}}\leqslant|f(z;0)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}},\quad|f(z;0)+1|\geqslant 1/{\mathfrak{C}},\quad|\partial_{z}f(z;0)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}},\quad z\in\mathscr{C}^{i},\quad 0\leqslant i\leqslant 2,

and (by increasing
{\mathfrak{C}}
if necessary), we also have

(C.33)

\displaystyle|1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;0)/(f(z;0)+1))|\geqslant 1/{\mathfrak{C}},\quad z\in\mathscr{C}^{1}\cup\mathscr{C}^{2},

since
\mathscr{C}^{1},\mathscr{C}^{2}
are bounded away from
\mathscr{C}^{0}
.

We recall
F(z;\theta)
from (
C.4
), we can rewrite it as a function of
\log f(z;\theta)
and
\partial_{z}\log f(z;\theta)

\displaystyle F(z;\theta)=\tilde{F}(\log f(z;\theta),\partial_{z}\log f(z;\theta)),\quad\tilde{F}(x,y)=\frac{-({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)e^{x}/(1+e^{x})^{2}}{1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)ye^{x}/(1+e^{x})^{2}}.

For any
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})=(\log f(z;0),\partial_{z}\log f(z;0))
with
z\in\mathscr{C}^{2}
,
\tilde{F}(x,y)
is analytic in a small neighborhood of
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
. There exist small
{\mathfrak{b}}>0
and large
r^{\prime}>0,M>0
(we will take
r
in (
C.30
) much bigger than
r^{\prime}
) such that

(C.34)

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(x,y)\subset_{\tilde{x},\tilde{y}}Mf^{0}(r^{\prime}u)f^{0}(r^{\prime}v),

for any
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})
satisfying
|\tilde{x}-\log f(\tilde{z};0)|,|\tilde{y}-\partial_{z}\log f(\tilde{z};0))|\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}
for some
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
.

Next we analyze the system of equations (
C.27
),(
C.28
) and (
C.29
), show the assumptions of Proposition
C.2
hold, and Proposition
3.4
follows.

Proof of Propositon
3.4
.

We recall the contour
\mathscr{C}^{0},\mathscr{C}^{1},\mathscr{C}^{2},\mathscr{C}^{3},\mathscr{C}^{0\pm}
; see Figure
21
. Define
r_{\theta}=re^{\theta}
(where
r
is from (
C.30
) and (
C.31
)) for
0\leqslant\theta\leqslant 1
, and
\mathscr{A}^{\pm}
the closure of the annulus between
\mathscr{C}^{0\pm}
and
\mathscr{C}^{2}
. We prove that for
{\mathfrak{c}}
small enough, the following holds for
0\leqslant\theta\leqslant 1
,

(1)

The righthand side of (
C.4
) is analytic on
\mathscr{A}^{+}
.

(2)

For any
z\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
,

\displaystyle 1/2{\mathfrak{C}}\leqslant|f(z;\theta)|\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{C}},\quad|f(z;\theta)+1|\geqslant 1/2{\mathfrak{C}},\quad|\partial_{z}f(z;\theta)|\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{C}},

and for any
z\in\mathscr{C}^{1}\cup\mathscr{C}^{2}
:

(C.35)

\displaystyle|1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;\theta)/(f(z;\theta)+1))|\geqslant 1/2{\mathfrak{C}}.

(3)

For
z\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
, it holds

(C.36)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\log f(z;\theta)-\log f(z;0)|,|\partial_{z}\log f(z;\theta)-\partial_{z}\log f(z;0)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}},\end{split}

where
{\mathfrak{b}}
is from (
C.34
).

(4)

For any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{i}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant 3
,

(C.37)

\displaystyle\log f(z;\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z}}(M/r^{2}_{\theta})f^{1}(r_{\theta}u),\quad B(z,z^{\prime};\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}Mf^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime}),

and for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant 3
,

(C.38)

\displaystyle G(z,z^{\prime};\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}Mf^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime}).

In the following proof,
C
will represent some constant which may differ from line to line.
It may depend on
r,r^{\prime},M,{\mathfrak{b}},{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{C}},({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)
and the length of the contours
\mathscr{C}^{i}
, but independent of
{\mathfrak{c}}
.
We denote
\sigma=\sigma(\beta)
the first time any of the above conditions fails.
Then for
\theta\leqslant\sigma
and
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
, we have from (
C.37
)

(C.39)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\log f(z;\theta)-\log f(\tilde{z};\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z}}(M/r_{\theta})uf^{1}(r_{\theta}u),\quad\partial_{z}\log f(z;\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z}}(M/r_{\theta})f^{0}(r_{\theta}u),\\
&\partial_{z}\log f(z;\theta)-\partial_{z}\log f(\tilde{z};\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z}}Muf^{0}(r_{\theta}u).\end{split}

For
\theta\leqslant\sigma
, we have
|f(z;\theta)+1|\geqslant 1/2{\mathfrak{C}}
. Thus for
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
, (
C.37
) also implies that

(C.40)

\displaystyle\frac{f(z;\theta)}{(f(z;\theta)+1)^{2}}\subset_{\tilde{z}}Cf^{0}(r_{\theta}u),

where we view the lefthand side as a function of
\log f(z;\theta)
, composed by
e^{x}/(e^{x}+1)^{2}
, and used (
C.26
); the constant
C
depends on
{\mathfrak{C}}
.

It follows from plugging (
C.37
) into (
C.27
), we get

(C.41)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\mathscr{C}^{3}}B(z,w;\theta)\int_{{\mathfrak{a}}(s)}^{{\mathfrak{b}}(s)}\frac{\beta^{1}(x)-\beta^{0}(x)}{w-x}{\rm d}x{\rm d}w\\
&\subset_{\tilde{z}}Mf^{(0)}(r_{\theta}u)\max_{w\in\mathscr{C}^{3}}\oint_{\mathscr{C}^{3}}\left|\int_{{\mathfrak{a}}(s)}^{{\mathfrak{b}}(s)}\log(w-x)(\partial_{x}\beta^{1}(x)-\partial_{x}\beta^{0}(x)){\rm d}x\right|{\rm d}w\subset_{0}{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{(0)}(r_{\theta}u),\end{split}

where for the last inequality, we used our assumption that
d(\partial_{x}\beta^{1},\partial_{x}\beta^{0})=d(\partial_{x}\beta,\partial_{x}H_{s}^{*})\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
, and
w\in\mathscr{C}^{3}
is distance
{\mathfrak{r}}
away from
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
. So
\log(w-x)
as a function of
x
, is Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant
1/{\mathfrak{r}}
, and the integral is bounded by
\OO({\mathfrak{c}}/{\mathfrak{r}})
; the constant
C
depends on
{\mathfrak{r}},M
and the contour
\mathscr{C}^{3}
.

Thanks to (
C.34
), (
C.36
) and (
C.39
), for
\theta\leqslant\sigma
and
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{1}\cup\mathscr{C}^{2}
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}F(z;\theta)=\tilde{F}(\log f(z;\theta),\partial_{z}\log f(z;\theta))\\
&\subset_{\tilde{z}}Mf^{0}(r^{\prime}(\log f(z;\theta)-\log f(\tilde{z};\theta)))f^{0}(r^{\prime}(\partial_{z}\log f(z;\theta)-\partial_{z}\log f(\tilde{z};\theta)))\\
&\subset_{\tilde{z}}Mf^{0}(zr^{\prime}(M/r_{\theta})f^{1}(r_{\theta}u))f^{0}(zr^{\prime}Mf^{0}(r_{\theta}u))\end{split}

Then (
C.25
) and (
C.26
) implies that for
\theta\leqslant\sigma
and
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{1}\cup\mathscr{C}^{2}

(C.42)

\displaystyle F(z;\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z}}Mf^{0}(r_{\theta}u),\quad F(z;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z}}{\mathfrak{c}}CMf^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)\subset_{0}4{\mathfrak{c}}CMf^{0}(r_{\theta}u),

provided that
r_{\theta}=re^{\theta}\geqslant 4r^{\prime}M
.
For
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{0}
, we can write
F(z;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)
in terms of
G(z;w;\theta)
as in (
C.7
):

\displaystyle F(z;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\frac{({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)f(z;\theta)}{(f(z;\theta)+1)^{2}}\oint_{\mathscr{C}^{3}}G(z,w;\theta)\int_{{\mathfrak{a}}(s)}^{{\mathfrak{b}}(s)}\frac{\beta^{1}(x)-\beta^{0}(x)}{w-x}{\rm d}x{\rm d}w.

Using (
C.38
) and (
C.40
), the same as for (
C.41
), we get for
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{0}
,

(C.43)

\displaystyle F(z;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z}}{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{0}(r_{\theta}u),

where
C
depends on
{\mathfrak{C}},{\mathfrak{r}},({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)
.

Next can estimate
\partial_{\theta}B(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
using the expression (
C.28
), for the first term on the righthand side of (
C.28
), using (
C.25
), (
C.37
), (
C.42
) and (
C.43
), we get for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{i}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant 3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathscr{C}^{k}}B(z,w;\theta)F(w;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(w;\theta)B(w,z^{\prime};\theta){\rm d}w\\
&\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})\int_{\mathscr{C}^{k}}4{\mathfrak{c}}CM^{3}|{\rm d}w|=4{\mathfrak{c}}CM^{3}|\mathscr{C}^{k}|f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime}),\end{split}

where
|\mathscr{C}^{k}|
is the length of the contour. For the second term on the righthand side of (
C.28
), similarly we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\bm{1}(\text{$z$ outside ${\mathscr{C}^{k}}$})\partial_{z}(F(z;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)B(z,z^{\prime};\theta))\\
&\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}\partial_{u}(4{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{(0)}(r_{\theta}u)M^{2}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime}))=16{\mathfrak{c}}CM^{2}\partial_{u}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime}).\end{split}

We have the same estimates for the last term on the righthand side of (
C.28
). Combining them together,
we have the following upper bound of
\partial_{\theta}B(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{i}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant 3
,

(C.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{\theta}B(z,z^{\prime};\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{c}}C(f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})+\partial_{u}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})+f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)\partial_{u^{\prime}}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})),\end{split}

where
C
depends on
{\mathfrak{r}},{\mathfrak{C}},M
and the contours.

By the same argument as for (
C.44
), we can analyze the differential equation (
C.29
) of
\partial_{\theta}G(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant 3
,

(C.45)

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}G(z,z^{\prime};\theta)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{c}}C(f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})+\partial_{u}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})+f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)\partial_{u^{\prime}}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})).

We recall that
r_{\theta}=re^{\theta}
. By integrating (
C.41
) from
\theta=0
to
\sigma
, we get for any
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
,

(C.46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log f(z;\sigma)-\log f(\tilde{z};0)&\subset_{\tilde{z}}\int_{0}^{\sigma}{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{(0)}(r_{\theta}u){\rm d}\theta={\mathfrak{c}}C\int_{0}^{\sigma}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\frac{e^{\theta j}(ru)^{j}}{(j+1)^{2}}{\rm d}\theta\\
&={\mathfrak{c}}C\left(\sigma+\sum_{j\geqslant 1}\frac{(e^{\sigma j}-1)(ru)^{j}}{j(j+1)^{2}}{\rm d}\theta\right)\subset_{0}2{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{1}(r_{\sigma}u).\end{split}

Similarly by taking derivative on both sides of (
C.41
), then integrating from
0
to
\sigma
, for any
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
we have

(C.47)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\log f(z;\sigma)-\partial_{z}\log f(\tilde{z};0)\subset_{\tilde{z}}2{\mathfrak{c}}C\partial_{u}f^{1}(r_{\sigma}u)\subset_{0}2er{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{0}(r_{\sigma}u).

By integrating (
C.44
) from
\theta=0
to
{\sigma}
, we get for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{i}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant 3
,

(C.48)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}B(z,z^{\prime};\sigma)-B(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime};0)\\
&\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{c}}C\int_{0}^{\sigma}(f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})+\partial_{u}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})+f^{0}(r_{\theta}u)\partial_{u^{\prime}}f^{0}(r_{\theta}u^{\prime})){\rm d}\theta\\
&={\mathfrak{c}}C\int_{0}^{\sigma}\sum_{j,k\geqslant 0}\frac{e^{\theta(j+k)}(ru)^{j}(ru)^{k}+re^{\theta(j+k)}j(ru)^{j-1}(ru)^{k}+re^{\theta(j+k)}(ru)^{j}k(ru)^{k-1}}{(j+1)^{2}(k+1)^{2}}{\rm d}\theta\\
&\subset_{0,0}3r{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{0}(r_{\sigma}u)f^{0}(r_{\sigma}u^{\prime}).\end{split}

Similarly by integrating (
C.45
) from
\theta=0
to
{\sigma}
, we get for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
1\leqslant i\leqslant 3
,

(C.49)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G(z,z^{\prime};\sigma)-G(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime};0)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}3r{\mathfrak{c}}Cf^{0}(r_{\sigma}u)f^{0}(r_{\sigma}u^{\prime}).\end{split}

The estimates (
C.46
) and (
C.47
) imply that for any
z\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
, we have

(C.50)

\displaystyle|\log f(z;\sigma)-\log f(z;0)|\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{c}}C,\quad|\partial_{z}\log f(z;\sigma)-\partial_{z}\log f(z;0)|\leqslant 2er{\mathfrak{c}}C.

Thus if we take
{\mathfrak{c}}
sufficiently small, (
C.50
) together with the initial estimates (
C.32
) give that

(C.51)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|f(z;\sigma)-f(z;0)|\leqslant 1/3{\mathfrak{C}},\quad|\partial_{z}f(z;\sigma)-\partial_{z}f(z;0)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{C}}/3,\\
&\left|\frac{\partial_{z}f(z;\sigma)}{(f(z;\sigma)+1)^{2}}-\frac{\partial_{z}f(z;0)}{(f(z;0)+1)^{2}}\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{3({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t){\mathfrak{C}}}.\end{split}

Thus (
C.51
) and (
C.32
) give for any
z\in\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
0\leqslant i\leqslant 2
,

(C.52)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&1/2{\mathfrak{C}}<|f(z;\sigma)|<2{\mathfrak{C}},\quad|f(z;\sigma)+1|>1/2{\mathfrak{C}},\quad|\partial_{z}\log f(z;\sigma)|<2{\mathfrak{C}}.\end{split}

And (
C.51
) and (
C.33
) implies that for any
z\in\mathscr{C}^{1}\cup\mathscr{C}^{2}

(C.53)

\displaystyle|1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;\sigma)/(f(z;\sigma)+1))|>1/2{\mathfrak{C}}.

We in fact have (
C.53
) for any
\theta\leqslant\sigma
, this implies that as we deform from
\theta=0
to
\theta=\sigma
, no zero of
1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;\theta)/(f(z;\theta)+1))
enters
\mathscr{A}^{-}
from its inner boundary curve
\mathscr{C}^{2}
.
Thanks to the estimate (
C.43
), for any
\theta\leqslant\sigma
,
F(z;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)
defines an analytic field in a radius
1/(2er)
tube neighborhood of
\mathscr{C}^{0}
.
We recall the differential equation of
\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)
from (
C.4
),
then

(C.54)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\theta}\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)|=\left|F(\mathscr{C}(x;\theta);\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(\mathscr{C}(x;\theta);\theta)\right|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}MC,

for any
\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)
inside this radius
1/(2er)
tube neighborhood of
\mathscr{C}^{0}
.
By integrating both sides of (
C.54
) from
\theta=0
to
\theta\leqslant\sigma
, we get

(C.55)

\displaystyle|\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)-\mathscr{C}(x;0)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}MC\leqslant 1/(200r),

provided we take
{\mathfrak{c}}\leqslant{\mathfrak{r}}/200rMC
. We conclude that
\mathscr{C}_{\theta}=\{\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)\cup\overline{\mathscr{C}(x;\theta)}:x\in I_{{\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}}^{*}\}
stays in a radius
1/(200r)
tube neighborhood of
\mathscr{C}^{0}
. In particular it stays in between the contours
\mathscr{C}^{0+}
and
\mathscr{C}^{0-}
(recall from Figure
21
).

The above discussion also implies that no zero of
1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;\theta)/(f(z;\theta)+1))
enters
\mathscr{A}^{-}
from its outer boundary
\mathscr{C}^{0-}
. In fact, from (
C.38
),
G(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
is analytic for
\dist(z,\tilde{z})\leqslant 1/(2er)
, where
\tilde{z}\in\mathscr{C}^{0}
, and
z^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{3}
. In particular, it is analytic for
z\in\mathscr{C}^{0-}
. We recall the expression of
G(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
from (
C.6
), for
z\in\mathscr{C}^{0-},z^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{3}
, (
C.55
) implies that both
z,z^{\prime}
are inside the contour
\mathscr{C}_{\theta}
, and the Schiffer kernel
B(z,z^{\prime};\theta)\neq 0
from its definition (
C.1
). Thus the denominator
1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;\theta)/(f(z;\theta)+1))
in
G(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
is nonzero for
z\in\mathscr{C}^{0-}
, and
0\leqslant\theta\leqslant\sigma
.

We conclude that
1+({\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}-t)\partial_{z}(f(z;\theta)/(f(z;\theta)+1))\neq 0
for any
\mathscr{A}^{-}
. Together with (
C.43
), we conclude that the righthand side of (
C.4
) is analytic on
\mathscr{A}^{+}
for
0\leqslant\theta\leqslant\sigma
.

By comparing (
C.46
), (
C.48
), (
C.49
) with (
C.37
) and (
C.38
), and using the estimates of the initial data (
C.30
), (
C.31
) and the estimate (
C.50
), if we take
2er{\mathfrak{c}}C\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}/2
and
3{\mathfrak{c}}C\leqslant M/(2e^{2}r^{2})
, we have for any
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{i}\times\mathscr{C}^{j}
with
0\leqslant i<j\leqslant 3
,

(C.56)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\log f(z;\sigma)\subset_{\tilde{z}}(M/2r^{2}_{{\sigma}})f^{1}(r_{\sigma}u),\quad B(z,z^{\prime};\sigma)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}(M/2)f^{0}(r_{\sigma}u)f^{0}(r_{\sigma}u^{\prime}),\\
&|\log f(\tilde{z};\sigma)-\log f(\tilde{z};0)|,|\partial_{z}\log f(\tilde{z};\sigma)-\partial_{z}\log f(\tilde{z};0)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{b}}/2.\end{split}

and for
(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{i}
with
1\leqslant i\leqslant 3
,

(C.57)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G(z,z^{\prime};\sigma)\subset_{\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}(M/2)f^{0}(r_{\sigma}u)f^{0}(r_{\sigma}u^{\prime}).\end{split}

We conclude from (
C.52
), (
C.53
) and (
C.56
) and (
C.57
) that
\sigma=1
, provided we take
{\mathfrak{c}}
small enough.

Therefore
if the boundary data
\beta
is sufficiently close to
H_{t}^{*}(x)
such that
d(\partial_{x}\beta,H_{t}^{*})\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
, then
\sigma=1
. The map as constructed from (
C.54
) using the analytic field
F(z;\theta)\partial_{\theta}\log f(z;\theta)

\displaystyle x\in I_{{\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}}^{*}\mapsto\mathscr{C}(x;0)\mapsto\mathscr{C}(x;1),

gives an analytic Jordan curve. Moreover,
|\partial_{z}^{k}B(z,z^{\prime};\theta)|\lesssim 1
uniformly for any
(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{1}
.
We recall the defining relation of
K(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
from (
C.2
). For
(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{0}\times\mathscr{C}^{1}
, we have
|z-z^{\prime}|\geqslant{\mathfrak{r}}
, and

|\partial_{z}^{k}K(z,z^{\prime};\theta)|\lesssim|\partial_{z}^{k}B(z,z^{\prime};\beta,s)|+1/|z-z^{\prime}|^{2+k}\lesssim 1
. Since
\partial_{z}^{k}K(z,z^{\prime};\theta)
is analytic for
z,z^{\prime}
inside the contours
\mathscr{C}^{0}
, by the maximal principle, we conclude that
|\partial_{z}^{k}K(z,z^{\prime};\theta)|\lesssim 1
for any
0\leqslant k\leqslant 2
and
z
inside the contour
\mathscr{C}^{0}
,
z^{\prime}
inside the contour
\mathscr{C}^{1}
.
Thus the assumptions in Proposition
C.2
hold, and Proposition
3.4
follows.

∎

Appendix D
Proof of
Proposition

3.5

In this section we establish
Proposition

3.5
, which are quantitative properties of the complex Burgers equation that are used throughout this paper.
Throughout, we without loss of generality restrict ourselves to
z\in\overline{\mathbb{H}^{+}}
(namely,
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z\geqslant 0
), for the proof when
z\in\mathbb{H}^{-}
follows from conjugation. Then,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[f^{*}_{t}(z)]\leqslant 0;\qquad\arg^{*}\big(f^{*}_{t}(z)\big)=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[\log f_{t}^{*}(z)\big];\qquad\sin\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log f_{t}^{*}(z)]\big)=\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[f_{t}^{*}(z)\big]}{\big|f_{t}^{*}(z)\big|}.

Recall the decompositions from (
3.25
) and (
3.27
), given by

(D.1)

\displaystyle\log f_{t}^{*}(z)=m_{t}^{*}(z)+\log g_{t}^{*}(z),\quad\log f_{t}^{*}(z)=\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z)+\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z),

where
\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)
is uniformly bounded and real analytic in a neighborhood of
[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)]
, and
\log g_{t}^{*}(z)=\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)-\log\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)+\log\big({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z\big)
. In particular, uniform boundedness and analyticity of
\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)
imply that the derivative of
\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)
is also uniformly bounded.
Thus we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)]=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z)]+\OO(|z-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z|)=\OO(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z)
, where the last equality is from that
\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)
is real analytic. Together with (
D.1
), we get

(D.2)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z)\big]=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[\log f_{t}^{*}(z)\big]+\OO(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z).

We will see that in all the cases the error term
\OO(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[z])
is negligible.

Further recall from Item (d) of the third part of
Assumption

2.4
that locally around
(x,r)
(which was mapped to
(f_{t}^{*}(z),z)=(f_{r}^{*}(x),x+(t-r)f_{r}^{*}(x)/(f_{r}^{*}(x)+1))
under the map (
3.4
), there exists a real analytic function
Q_{0}
in one variable such that

(D.3)

\displaystyle Q_{0}\big(f^{*}_{r}(x)\big)=Q_{0}\big(f^{*}_{t}(z)\big)=x\big(f^{*}_{r}(x)+1\big)-rf^{*}_{r}(x)=z\big(f^{*}_{t}(z)+1\big)-tf^{*}_{t}(z).

The first statement (
3.28
) of the proposition follows from (
3.2
).

Next, let

\displaystyle\chi_{t}^{*}(z)=\displaystyle\frac{f^{*}_{t}(z)}{f^{*}_{t}(z)+1};\qquad Q_{1}(u)=(1-u)Q_{0}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{u}{1-u}\bigg).

We can rewrite (
D.3
) as an equation of
\chi_{t}^{*}(z)
:

(D.4)

\displaystyle Q_{1}\big(\chi^{*}_{t}(z)\big)=z-t\chi^{*}_{t}(z).

At point
(E(t),t)
on the arctic curve
{\mathfrak{A}}({\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime})
,
f_{t}^{*}(E(t))
is a double root of
Q_{0}\big(f^{*}_{t}(E(t))\big)=E(t)\big(f^{*}_{t}(E(t))+1\big)-tf^{*}_{t}(E(t))
and are thus characterized by the equations

(D.5)

\displaystyle Q_{1}\Big(\chi^{*}_{t}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)=E(t)-t\chi^{*}_{t}\big(E(t)\big);\qquad Q_{1}^{\prime}\Big(\chi^{*}_{t}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)=-t.

Recalling that
\big(E(t),t\big)
is a cusp singularity of the arctic boundary if and only if
\chi_{t}^{*}(z)
is a triple root of (
D.4
) (and that there are no quadruple or higher roots of (
D.4
)), we have that

(D.6)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bigg|Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)\bigg|\asymp 1,\qquad&\text{if $\big(E(t),t\big)$ is bounded away from a cusp};\\
\bigg|Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)\bigg|=0\quad\text{and}\quad\bigg|Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)\bigg|\asymp 1,\qquad&\text{if $\big(E(t),t\big)=\big(E(t_{c}),t_{c}\big)$ is a cusp}.\end{aligned}

Now let us establish the three parts of
Proposition

3.5
.

Proof of the first statement of
Proposition

3.5
.

By the first statement of (
D.6
), if
\big(E(t),t\big)
is bounded away from the cusp point, then
\chi_{t}^{*}(z)
has square root behavior in a small neighborhood of
E(t)
, namely,

(D.7)

\displaystyle\chi^{*}_{t}(z)=\chi_{t}^{*}(E(t))+\sqrt{C_{t}(z)\big(z-E(t)\big)},\qquad\text{where $C_{t}(z)$ is analytic for $\big|z-E(t)\big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}$},

where the square root has negative imaginary part for
\Imaginary z>0
. We further have
C_{t}(z)=\overline{C_{t}(z)}
and

\displaystyle C_{t}(E(t))=\frac{2}{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big(\chi^{*}_{t}(E(t))\big)},\qquad\Big|C_{t}\big(E(t)\big)\Big|\asymp 1.

This gives (
3.29
). For
(z,t)
bounded away from the tangency locations and cusp points, the claim (
1
) follows from (
D.7
) and (
D.2
). This establishes the first statement of the proposition.
∎

Proof of the second statement of
Proposition

3.5
.

We next establish the second statement concerning the behavior of the complex slope near the tangency locations. At the tangent point
\big(E_{1}(t_{1}),t_{1}\big)=\big({\mathfrak{a}}(t_{1}),t_{1}\big)
of slope
1
, the complex slope
f^{*}_{t_{1}}\big(E_{1}(t_{1})\big)=\infty
, so that
\chi^{*}_{t_{1}}\big(E_{1}\big(t_{1})\big)=1
. Since no tangency location is also a cusp, we also have by (
D.6
) that
-Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)\asymp 1
. Hence, (
D.7
) reduces to

(D.8)

\displaystyle\frac{f_{t_{1}}^{*}(z)}{f_{t_{1}}^{*}(z)+1}=1+\sqrt{C_{t_{1}}(z)\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t_{1})\big)},\qquad\text{for $\big|z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t_{1})\big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}$}.

By Taylor expansion of
Q_{1}
and
Q_{1}^{\prime}
from (
D.5
) around
\chi_{t_{1}}^{*}(E_{1}(t_{1}))=1
, we get

(D.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{1}(t)-E_{1}(t_{1})&=t-t_{1}-\frac{(t-t_{1})^{2}}{2Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)}+\mathcal{O}\big(|t-t_{1}|^{3}\big),\\
\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{1}}^{*}\big(E_{1}(t_{1})\big)&=\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}(t)\big)-1=-\frac{t-t_{1}}{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)}+\mathcal{O}\big(|t-t_{1}|^{2}\big).\end{split}

We recall that
{\mathfrak{a}}(t)={\mathfrak{a}}(t_{1})+t-t_{1}=E_{1}(t_{1})+t-t_{1}
. Thus (
D.9
) implies that
E_{1}(t)-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\asymp|t_{1}-t|^{2}
, and together with (
D.7
)

(D.10)

\displaystyle\frac{f_{t}^{*}(z)}{f_{t}^{*}(z)+1}=\chi_{t}^{*}(z)=1+\frac{t_{1}-t}{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)}+\sqrt{C_{t}(z)\big(z-E_{1}(t)\big)}+\mathcal{O}\big(|t_{1}-t|^{2}\big),\qquad\text{for $\big|z-E_{1}(t)\big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}$}.

We can use (
D.10
) to solve for
f_{t}^{*}(z)
,

(D.11)

\displaystyle f_{t}^{*}(z)=\bigg(\frac{t-t_{1}}{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)}-\sqrt{C_{t}(z)\big(z-E_{1}(t)\big)}+\mathcal{O}\big(|t_{1}-t|^{2}\big)\bigg)^{-1}-1.

For
0\leqslant t-t_{1}\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\big|z-E_{1}(t)\big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
, the right side of (
D.11
) is large, and so
\arg^{*}\big(f^{*}_{t}(z)\big)
is of the same order as
\arg^{*}\big(f_{t}^{*}(z)+1\big)
. This gives

(D.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi+\arg^{*}\big(f_{t}^{*}(z)\big)\asymp\pi+\arg^{*}\big(f_{t}^{*}(z)+1\big)&\asymp\pi-\arg\left(\displaystyle\frac{t-t_{1}}{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)}-\sqrt{C_{t}(z)\big(z-E_{1}(t)\big)}+\OO\big(|t_{1}-t|^{2}\big)\right).\end{split}

We notice that
-Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)\asymp 1
and
(t-t_{1})/Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)+\OO(|t_{1}-t|^{2})\asymp t_{1}-t
is real. Thus, denoting
z=E_{1}(t)-\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta
, for
\kappa\geqslant 0
, the denominator in (
D.11
) behaves like
t_{t}-t-\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}+\mathrm{i}\eta/\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
and, for
\kappa\leqslant 0
, it behaves like
t_{1}-t-\eta/\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}+\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}
. Thus, we can further simplify (
D.12
) as

(D.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eqref{e:mtbeh3}\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|}\wedge 1&\kappa\leqslant 0.\end{array}\right.\end{split}

By plugging (
D.13
) into (
3.27
), and recalling
\arg\big(\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)\big)=\mathcal{O}(\eta)
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\pi+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z)]=\pi+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log f_{t}^{*}(z)]-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log\tilde{g}_{t}^{*}(z)]\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|}\wedge 1&\kappa\leqslant 0.\end{array}\right.

The estimates (
D.13
) and (
D
) together implies (
2
).

Next we assume that
0\leqslant t_{1}-t\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\big|z-E_{1}(t)\big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
. Since the line through
\big(\mathfrak{a}(t),t\big)
with slope
1
is tangent to the arctic boundary, (
2.9
) implies that
\chi_{t}^{*}\big(\mathfrak{a}(t)\big)=1
. Thus, (
D.7
) implies that

(D.16)

\displaystyle f_{t}^{*}(z)=\Big(\sqrt{C_{t}\big({\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)\big({\mathfrak{a}}(t)-E_{1}(t)\big)}-\sqrt{C_{t}(z)\big(z-E_{1}(t)\big)}\Big)^{-1}-1.

Multiplying both sides of (
D.16
) by
z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)
gives

(D.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\arg^{*}\Big(f_{t}^{*}(z)\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)\Big)&=-\arg^{*}\left({\mathfrak{a}}(t)-z+\frac{\sqrt{C_{t}\big({\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)\big({\mathfrak{a}}(t)-E_{1}(t)\big)}+\sqrt{C_{t}(z)\big(z-E_{1}(t)\big)}}{-C_{t}\big({\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)+\OO\big(\big|z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big|+|{\mathfrak{a}}(t)-E_{1}(t)|\big)}\right)\\
&\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{t_{1}-t}\wedge 1&\kappa\leqslant 0,\end{array}\right.\end{split}

where we have again denoted
z=E_{1}(t)-\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta
, where in the first line we used
C_{t}(\mathfrak{a}(t))(\mathfrak{a}(t)-E_{1})-C_{t}(z)(z-E_{1})=(\mathfrak{a}(t)-z)(C_{t}(\mathfrak{a}(t))+\OO(|{\mathfrak{a}}(t)-E_{1}(t)|)+\mathcal{O}\big(|z-\mathfrak{a}(t)|^{2}\big)
, and in the second line we used the square root behavior of
\sqrt{C_{t}(z)(z-E_{1}(t))}
. By rearranging (
D.17
), we conclude

\displaystyle-\arg^{*}f_{t}^{*}(z)\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)}+\arg\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big),&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|}\wedge 1&\kappa\leqslant 0,\end{array}\right.

where for the second line, we used that for
\kappa\leqslant 0
we have

\displaystyle\arg\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)\asymp\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{|\kappa|+\big|E_{1}(t)-\mathfrak{a}(t)\big|+\eta}\lesssim\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{|\kappa|+(t-t_{1})^{2}+\eta}\lesssim\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|},

is smaller than the leading term. Moreover,

\displaystyle-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[m_{t}^{*}(z)\big]=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[\log g_{t}^{*}(z)(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t))\big]-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[\log f_{t}^{*}(z)\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)\big]\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|}\wedge 1&\kappa\leqslant 0,\end{array}\right.

where we used that
\log g_{t}^{*}(z)\big(z-{\mathfrak{a}}(t)\big)
is real analytic in a small neighborhood of
{\mathfrak{a}}(t)
, thus its imaginary part is of size
\OO(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z)
, which is small compared with the leading term.
The estimates (
D
) and (
D
) together imply (
2
) and (
2
).

The proof is very similar close to the other tangency location
\big(E_{2}(t_{2}),t_{2}\big)=\big({\mathfrak{b}}(t_{2}),t_{2}\big)
, so in this case we only state the analogous bounds. Here, the tangency location
\big(E_{2}(t_{2}),t_{2})\big)
is of slope
\infty
, and so the tangent line through
\big(\mathfrak{b}(t),t\big)
to the arctic boundary has slope
0
; thus,
\chi^{*}_{t_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}}(t_{2}))=0
. We also again have the estimate
{\mathfrak{b}}(t)-E_{2}(t)\asymp|t_{2}-t|^{2}
. For
0\leqslant t-t_{2}\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
and
|z-E_{2}(t)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
we have

\displaystyle\pi+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\tilde{m}_{t}^{*}(z)]\asymp\pi+\arg^{*}(f_{t}^{*}(z))\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|}\wedge 1,&\kappa\leqslant 0,\end{array}\right.

where
z=E_{2}(t)+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta
.
For
0\leqslant t_{2}-t\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
and
|z-E(t)|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
, we have

\displaystyle-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[m_{t}^{*}(z)]=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log g_{t}^{*}(z)/({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z)]-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\log f_{t}^{*}(z)/({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z)]\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|}\wedge 1,&\kappa\leqslant 0,\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle-\arg^{*}(f_{t}^{*}(z))\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}+|t-t_{1}|)}+\arg({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-z),&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{|t-t_{1}|}\wedge 1,&\kappa\leqslant 0.\end{array}\right.

where
z=E_{2}(t)+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta
.
∎

Proof of the third statement of
Proposition

3.5
.

The cusp point
(E_{c},t_{c})
is characterized by the equations

(D.24)

\displaystyle Q_{1}\big(\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\big)=E_{c}-t_{c}\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c});\qquad Q_{1}^{\prime}\big(\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\big)=-t_{c};\qquad Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big(\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\big)=0.

We recall from (
D.6
),
|Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\big(\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\big)|\asymp 1
.
In the following we use (
D.24
) to study the complex slope
f_{t}^{*}(z)
and
\chi_{t}^{*}(z)
in a neighborhood of
(E_{c},t_{c})
. For
t\leqslant t_{c}
, the arctic curves
\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t),t\big),\big(E_{2}^{\prime}(t),t\big)
are characterized by

(D.25)

\displaystyle Q_{1}\Big(\chi^{*}_{t}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)=E(t)-t\chi^{*}_{t}\big(E(t)\big),\quad Q_{1}^{\prime}\Big(\chi^{*}_{t}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)=-t,\qquad\text{for $E(t)$ equal to either $E_{1}^{\prime}(t)$ or $E_{2}^{\prime}(t)$}.

Using (
D.24
), we can solve (
D.25
). By taking the difference between (
D.24
) and (
D.25
), and expanding around
(E_{c},t_{c})
we get

(D.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}t_{c}-t=Q_{1}^{\prime}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)-Q_{1}^{\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)&=\frac{Q_{t}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c}))}{2}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big)^{2}\\
&\qquad+\OO\bigg(\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big|^{3}\bigg).\end{split}

Moreover, recalling
c(t)=E_{c}+(t-t_{c})\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})
, we have that

\displaystyle E(t)-c(t)

\displaystyle=E(t)-E_{c}+(t_{c}-t)\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})

\displaystyle=t\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-t\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})+Q_{1}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)\Big)-Q_{1}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)

\displaystyle=t\big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)+Q_{1}^{\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\displaystyle\frac{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)}{2}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big)^{2}+\displaystyle\frac{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)}{6}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big)^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\Big|\chi_{t}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\Big|^{4}\bigg)

and thus

(D.27)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} E(t)-c(t)&=(t-t_{c})\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big)+\frac{Q_{t}^{\prime\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)}{6}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big)^{3}\\
&\qquad+\OO\bigg(\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big|^{4}\bigg).\end{aligned}

It follows from (
D.26
) that
\big|\chi_{t}^{*}(E(t))-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big|\asymp|t_{c}-t|^{1/2}
and, more precisely,

(D.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\chi^{*}_{t}(E(t))&=\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\pm\sqrt{\frac{2(t_{c}-t)}{Q^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\big(\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\big)}}+\OO\big(|t_{c}-t|\big),\\
E(t)-c(t)&=\mp\sqrt{\frac{8(t_{c}-t)^{3}}{9Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\big(\chi^{*}_{t}(E_{c})\big)}}+\OO\big(|t_{c}-t|^{2}\big),\end{split}

where the second equality follows from inserting the first into (
D.27
). We conclude that
E_{2}^{\prime}(t)-E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\asymp(t_{c}-t)^{3/2}
. In a small neighborhood of
E_{1}^{\prime}(t)
, namely,
0\leqslant t_{c}-t\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\big|z-E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}(t_{c}-t)^{3/2}
, we can solve the equation
Q_{1}\big(\chi^{*}_{t}(z)\big)=z-t\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E(t)\big)
by expanding around
\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t),t\big)
. This gives

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}z-E_{1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=t\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-t\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)+Q_{1}\big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)\big)-Q_{1}\Big(\chi_{t}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=t\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-t\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)+Q_{1}^{\prime}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big)\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\displaystyle\frac{Q^{\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\big)}{2}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big)^{2}+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}(t)\big)\Big|^{3}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\big)}{2}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big)^{2}+\OO\bigg(\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big|^{3}\bigg).

Thus, since
Q_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)=0
, we deduce by Taylor expansion and (
D.28
) that

\displaystyle z-E_{1}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)}{2}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\Big)+\mathcal{O}\big(|t-t_{c}|\big)\bigg)\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\OO\bigg(\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big|^{3}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)}{2}(t_{c}-t)}\cdot\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\OO\bigg(|t_{c}-t|\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big|^{2}+\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big)\Big|^{3}\bigg).

By rearranging, we conclude that

(D.29)

\displaystyle\chi^{*}_{t}(z)-\chi^{*}_{t}\big(E^{\prime}_{1}(t)\big)=\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{2}{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\big(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)}\bigg)^{1/4}\frac{\sqrt{z-E^{\prime}_{1}(t)}}{(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}}+\OO\left((t_{c}-t)^{1/4}\big|z-E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big|^{1/2}+\frac{\big|z-E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big|}{t_{c}-t}\right).

It therefore follows that

\displaystyle-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\big[f_{t}^{*}(z)\big]\asymp-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\chi_{t}^{*}(z)]\asymp\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\eta}{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}},&\kappa\geqslant 0,\\
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|+\eta}}{(t_{c}-t)^{1/4}}&\kappa\leqslant 0,\end{array}\right.

where we have denoted
z=E_{1}(t)+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta
, with
\big|z-E_{1}^{\prime}(t)\big|\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}(t_{c}-t)^{3/2}
. The claims (
3.38
) and (
3
) then follow from (
D.29
), (
D
), and the decomposition (
D.1
).

In the following we study
\chi_{t}^{*}(z)
away from the two points
E_{1}^{\prime}(t)
and
E_{2}^{\prime}(t)
.
By a Taylor expansion, using (
D.24
)
in a neighborhood of
(E_{c},t_{c})
, we obtain

(D.32)

\displaystyle\begin{split}z-E_{c}-(t-t_{c})\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})&=Q_{1}\big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)\big)+t\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-E_{c}-(t-t_{c})\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})\\
&=(t-t_{c})\big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)+\frac{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c}))}{6}\big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big)^{3}\\
&\qquad+\OO\Big(\big|\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})\big|^{4}\Big).\end{split}

If we plug in
z=c(t)=E_{c}+(t-t_{c})\chi^{*}_{t_{c}}(E_{c})
in (
D.32
), we get that

Q_{1}\big(\chi_{t}^{*}\big(c(t)\big)\big)=c(t)-t\chi_{t}^{*}\big(c(t)\big).

By comparing with (
D.3
), we conclude that
\chi_{t}^{*}\big(c(t)\big)=\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c})
, which by (
D.32
) gives

(D.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}z-c(t)=(t-t_{c})\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(c(t)\big)\Big)+\frac{Q_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}(E_{c}))}{6}\Big(\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(c(t)\big)\Big)^{3}+\OO\bigg(\Big|\chi_{t}^{*}(z)-\chi_{t_{c}}^{*}\big(c(t)\big)\Big|^{4}\bigg).\end{split}

By the first statement of (
D.28
), for
z
in a small neighborhood of
c(t)
satisfying
\dist\big(z,[E^{\prime}_{1}(t),E^{\prime}_{2}(t)]\big)\geqslant{\mathfrak{c}}(t_{c}-t)^{3/2}
(equivalently,
\big|z-c(t)\big|\lesssim(t_{c}-t)^{3/2}
), the linear term on the right side of (
D.33
) is the leading term, and so
\chi^{*}_{t}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(c(t)\big)
behaves like
\big(z-c(t)\big)/(t-t_{c})
.
For
z
in the larger neighborhood of
c(t)
satisfying
{\mathfrak{c}}(t_{c}-t)^{3/2}\leqslant\dist\big(z,[E^{\prime}_{1}(t),E^{\prime}_{2}(t)]\big)\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
(equivalently,
\mathfrak{c}(t_{c}-t)^{3/2}\lesssim|z-c(t)|\lesssim 1
), the cubic term on the right side of (
D.33
) dominates
\chi_{t}(z)-\chi_{t}^{*}\big(c(t)\big)
behaves like
\big(z-c(t)\big)^{1/3}
, and we have

\displaystyle-\Imaginary\big[\chi_{t}^{*}(z)\big]\asymp-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\left[\displaystyle\frac{f^{*}_{t}(z)}{f^{*}_{t}(z)+1}\right]\asymp-\Imaginary\big[f_{t}^{*}(z)\big]\asymp\big|z-c(t)\big|^{1/3}.

This finishes the proof of (
3.41
).
∎

Appendix E
Solving for the Correction Term

In this section we sketch the proof of
Proposition

4.3
, which closely follows
[
Hua21
, Section 9]
.
For any time
s\in[0,{\mathfrak{t}})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, and any particle configuration
(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\subseteq[{\mathfrak{a}}(s),{\mathfrak{b}}(s)]
, we make the following ansatz

\displaystyle N_{s}(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})=\frac{V({\bm{x}})e^{n^{2}Y_{s}({\bm{x}})}E_{s}(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})}{\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{n}(x_{j}-{\mathfrak{a}}(s)))\Gamma_{n}({\mathfrak{b}}(s)-x_{j}-1/n)},

where
\Gamma_{n}(k/n)=(k/n)((k-1)/n)\cdots(1/n)=k!/n^{k}
and

\partial_{x}\beta(x)=\rho(x;{\bm{x}})
. Then from our construction (
4.5
), we have

\displaystyle E_{\mathfrak{t}}(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})=1,\quad(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\in[{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathfrak{t}}),{\mathfrak{b}}({\mathfrak{t}})].

Then we can rewrite the recursion (
4.6
) as a recursion for
E_{s}(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})
: for any time
s\in[0,{\mathfrak{t}})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, and
{\bm{x}}=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\in[{\mathfrak{a}}(s),{\mathfrak{b}}(s)]

(E.1)

\displaystyle E_{s}({\bm{x}})=\sum_{{\bm{e}}\in\{0,1\}^{m}}a_{s}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})E_{(s+1/n)}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n),

where

(E.2)

\displaystyle a_{s}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})=\frac{V({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{V({\bm{x}})}\prod_{j=1}^{m}(x_{j}-{\mathfrak{a}}(s))^{1-e_{j}}({\mathfrak{b}}(s)-x_{j}-1/n)^{e_{j}}e^{n^{2}(Y_{(s+1/n)}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-Y_{s}({\bm{x}}))}.

We denote the associated partition function by

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}_{s}({\bm{x}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{{\bm{e}}\in\{0,1\}^{m}}a_{s}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}}).

The equation (
E.1
) can be solved using the Feynman–Kac formula. For any time
s\in[0,{\mathfrak{t}})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, to solve for
E_{s}({\bm{x}})
, we construct a Markov process
\{{\bm{x}}_{t}\}_{t\in[s,{\mathfrak{t}}]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}
starting from the configuration
{\bm{x}}_{s}={\bm{x}}
with a time dependent generator given by

(E.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{t}f({\bm{x}})=\sum_{{\bm{e}}\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\frac{a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})}{\tilde{Z}_{t}({\bm{x}})}(f({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-f({\bm{x}})),\quad{\bm{x}}=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\in[{\mathfrak{a}}(t),{\mathfrak{b}}(t)].

We remark that the boundary constraints of the double-sided trapezoid domain
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}
(recall from (
)) is encoded in the jump probabilities
a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})
. In particular, observe under this jump process that, if a particle
x_{j}
is at
{\mathfrak{a}}(t)
, then it has to jump to the right, for otherwise
e_{j}=0
, implying
a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})=0
. Similarly, if a particle
x_{j}
is at
{\mathfrak{b}}(t)-1/n
, then it has to stay, for otherwise
e_{j}=1
, implying
a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})=0
.
Thus, for non-intersecting Bernoulli random walks in the form (
E.3
), particles (the
x_{j}
) are constrained to remain inside
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}
.

Using the Markov process
\{{\bm{x}}_{t}\}_{t\in[s,{\mathfrak{t}}]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}
defined above,
E_{s}
can be solved using the Feynman-Kac formula.

Proposition E.1

(
[
Hua21
, Proposition 9.3]
)
.

For any
s\in[0,{\mathfrak{t}}]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}
and
{\bm{x}}\subset[{\mathfrak{a}}(s),{\mathfrak{b}}(s)]
, we have

(E.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{s}({\bm{x}})&=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\prod_{t\in[s,{\mathfrak{t}}]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\tilde{Z}_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})\right|{\bm{x}}_{s}={\bm{x}}\right].\end{split}

To use
Proposition

E.1
to solve for the correction terms
E_{s}
, we need to understand the Markov process
\{{\bm{x}}_{t}\}_{t\in[s,{\mathfrak{t}}]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}
with generator
{\mathcal{L}}_{t}
as given in (
E.3
). For the weights
a_{s}({\bm{e}},{\bm{x}})
as in (
E.2
), all the terms are explicit, except for
Y_{(s+1/n)}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-Y_{s}({\bm{x}})
. We need to derive a
1/n
-expansion of
Y_{(s+1/n)}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-Y_{s}({\bm{x}})
.
We recall the variational problem from (
3.12
)

(E.5)

\displaystyle W_{s}(\beta)=\sup_{H\in\mathrm{Adm}({{\mathfrak{D}}^{s}};\beta)}\int_{s}^{{\mathfrak{t}}^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sigma(\nabla H){\rm d}x{\rm d}t.

with minimizer
H_{t}(x;\beta,s)
and the complex slope
f_{t}(x;\beta,s)
. The complex slope can be extended to a neighborhood of
[{\mathfrak{a}}(s),{\mathfrak{b}}(s)]
. We also recall the decomposition
f(z;\beta,s)=e^{\tilde{m}(z;\beta,s)}\tilde{g}(z;\beta,s)
from (
3.21
).

Proposition E.2

(
[
Hua21
, Proposition 6.3]
)
.

There exists a universal constant
{\mathfrak{c}}>0
. For any boundary profile
\beta=\beta(x;{\bm{x}})
sufficiently close to
H_{s}^{*}
, i.e.,
d(\partial_{x}\beta(x;{\bm{x}}),\partial_{x}H_{s}^{*}(x))\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
, the following holds

(E.6)

\displaystyle n^{2}(Y_{(s+1/n)}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-Y_{s}({\bm{x}}))=\sum_{i=1}^{m}e_{i}\chi(x_{i};\beta,s)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant m}e_{i}e_{j}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j};\beta,s)+\zeta({\bm{x}},s)+\OO(1/n),

where the error
\OO(1/n)
is uniform in
\beta
, and

(E.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\chi(x_{i};\beta,s)&=\log\tilde{g}(x_{i};\beta,s)+\frac{1}{n}\left(\partial_{s}(\log\tilde{g}(x_{i};\beta,s))+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}(z;\beta,s)\right)\\
\kappa(x_{i},x_{j};\beta,s)&=-\frac{1}{2}K(x_{i},x_{j};\beta,s),\end{split}

the functions
\tilde{g}_{s}(x;\beta,s),K(x,y;\beta,s)
are as in (
3.21
), (
C.2
), and

(E.8)

\displaystyle\zeta({\bm{x}},s)=(n\partial_{s}Y_{s}({\bm{x}})+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{s}^{2}Y_{s}({\bm{x}})).

Using
Proposition

E.2
, we can rewrite
a_{s}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})
in the following form

(E.9)

\displaystyle a_{s}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\frac{V({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{V({\bm{x}})}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\phi^{+}(x_{j};\beta,s)^{e_{i}}\phi^{-}(x_{j};\beta,s)^{1-e_{i}}e^{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}e_{i}e_{j}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j};\beta,s)+\zeta({\bm{x}},s)+\OO(1/n)},

where
\beta=\beta(x;{\bm{x}})
, and

\displaystyle\phi^{+}(x;\beta,s)=({\mathfrak{b}}(s)-x-1/n)e^{\chi(x;\beta,s)},\quad\phi^{-}(x;\beta,s)=x-{\mathfrak{a}}(s).

Instead of solving (
E.4
) directly, we make another ansatz.
The formula for the error term
E_{s}({\bm{x}})
from (
E.4
) is expressed in terms of the non-intersecting Bernoulli random walk
\{{\bm{x}}_{t}\}_{t\in[s,{\mathfrak{t}})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}
starting from
{\bm{x}}_{s}={\bm{x}}
. Let
\beta=\beta(x;{\bm{x}})
. We make the following ansatz that the leading order term of
E_{s}({\bm{x}})
is given by the expression (
E.4
) with
{\bm{x}}_{t}
replaced by
H_{t}(x;\beta,s)
:

(E.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{s}({\bm{x}})=e^{F_{s}({\bm{x}})}E_{s}^{(1)}({\bm{x}}),\quad F_{s}({\bm{x}})=\log\left(\prod_{t\in[s,{\mathfrak{t}})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}}\tilde{Z}_{t}(H_{t}(x;\beta,s))\right).\end{split}

The following proposition states that (
E.10
) is a good approximation.

Proposition E.3

(
[
Hua21
, Proposition 9.3]
)
.

There exists a constant
\mathfrak{c}>0
, for any time
s\in[0,T)\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, and any particle configuration
(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{m})\in[{\mathfrak{a}}(s),{\mathfrak{b}}(s)]
such that
\beta=\beta(x;{\bm{x}})
is sufficiently close to
H_{s}^{*}
, i.e.,
d(\partial_{x}\beta(x;{\bm{x}}),\partial_{x}H_{s}^{*}(x))\leqslant{\mathfrak{c}}
,
E_{s}^{(1)}({\bm{x}})
as in (
E.10
) satisfies

(E.11)

\displaystyle\frac{E_{t}^{(1)}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{E_{t}^{(1)}({\bm{x}})}=e^{\OO(1/n)}.

Given these results,
Proposition

4.3
follows from plugging (
E.9
), Propositions
E.2
and
E.3
into (
E.12
).

Proof of
Proposition

4.3
.

The transition probability is given by

(E.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\bm{x}}_{t+1/n}={\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n|{\bm{x}}_{t}={\bm{x}})=\frac{N_{t+1/n}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{N_{t}({\bm{x}})}=a_{t}({\bm{e}};{\bm{x}})\frac{E_{t+1/n}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{E_{t}({\bm{x}})}.

Let
\beta=\beta(x;{\bm{x}})
.
The quantity
F_{s}({\bm{x}})
is a sum of
\log\tilde{Z}_{t}
. The formula of
\log\tilde{Z}_{t}
for
t\in[s,{\mathfrak{t}})\cap\mathbb{Z}_{n}
is explicitly given in
[
Hua21
, Proposition 9.1]
up to error
\OO(1/n^{2})
. It also gives the functional derivative
\partial_{\beta}F_{s}(\beta)(z)
of
F_{s}
, with respect to
\beta
up to error
\OO(1/n)
,

(E.13)

\displaystyle F_{s}({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)-F_{s}({\bm{x}})=(1/n)\sum_{i=1}^{m}e_{i}\partial_{\beta}F_{s}(\beta)(x_{i})+\OO(1/n).

Thanks to (
E.9
), Propositions
E.2
and
E.3
, we can rewrite (
E.12
) as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z_{t}({\bm{x}})}\frac{V({\bm{x}}+{\bm{e}}/n)}{V({\bm{x}})}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\phi^{+}(x_{i};\beta,t)^{e_{i}}\phi^{-}(x_{i};\beta,t)^{1-e_{i}}e^{\sum_{i,j}\frac{e_{i}e_{j}}{n^{2}}\kappa(x_{i},x_{j};\beta,t)+\OO(1/n)},

where
Z_{t}({\bm{x}})=\tilde{Z}_{t}({\bm{x}})/e^{\zeta({\bm{x}},t)}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phi^{+}(z;\beta,t)=\phi^{+}(z;\beta,t)e^{\frac{1}{n}\partial_{\beta}F_{t}(\beta)(z)}=\tilde{g}(z;\beta,t)({\mathfrak{b}}(t)-1/n-z)e^{\frac{1}{n}\psi(z;\beta,t)},\\
&\psi(z;\beta,t)=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\log\tilde{g}(z;\beta,t)+\partial_{t}(\log\tilde{g}(z;\beta,t))+\partial_{\beta}F_{t}(\beta)(z)+\OO(1/n),\end{split}

\partial_{\beta}F_{t}(\beta)(z)
is the functional derivative of
F_{t}
with respect
\beta
as in (
E.13
).
This finishes the proof of
Proposition

4.3
.
∎
